# The Teaching Core of the Kephalaia

*Extracted from the Coptic Kephalaia of the Teacher (Gardner, Brill 1995).*
*Later editorial layers — hagiographic frame, pastoral instructions,*
*and explicit Christian overlay — have been removed. Mixed passages*
*have been repaired to recover the teaching content. The text is*
*presented in its original chapter order.*

---

**Chapters analyzed**: 123
**Chapters with core content**: 111
**Core text words**: ~50,371

## Chapter 0
### Introduction

*The opening unit (¶1–9) is a compact cosmogonic survey in an impersonal, system-internal register (Separation of the two principles; attack of Darkness; First Man; Living Spirit’s construction; Third Ambassador; final conflagration and binding). From ¶10 onward the chapter shifts to a justificatory/protreptic frame (Mani’s books, exhortations to write, institutional categories, and comparative apostle-history), with strong editorial fatigue and institutional drift; this later material is not part of the oldest correspondential-teaching substrate.*

(1) [ ... ] Light [ ... ] which he has unveiled [ ... (2) ... ] his disciples [ ... (3) ... ] entire [ ... ] of wisdom [ ... ... ] word in the beginning [ ... ... ] concerning the separation [ ... ... ] the heights [ ... ] to the heights [ ... ] and they spread out [ ... ... unt]il they should make war [ ... ] kingdom [ ... ... ]ight [ ... ... ] in the heights [ ... f]rom all the aeons [ ... ... ] the Light, the one that no [ ... ... ] in it.

[ ... ] the Darkness, the world [ ... ] their root [ ... ] which poured forth [ ... ... ] the one that the earth has [ ... ... ... ] Darkness [ ... ... ] it is far from the Light [ ... ]

for each one of them exists [ ... ... ] they are divided from one another by their [ ... ... ] in an eternality since the [ ... ] (4) the Darkness has made war against the Light, desiring that it would reign over an essence not its own. It spread a quarrel [ ... ] it disturbed [ ... ... ] came up, it [ ... in this great] need.

The power came forth from [ ... ... which] is [the Fir]st Man. He [ ... ... ] the armour of Light [ ... ] all [the enmity]. He crucified it in [ ... ... ] the armour [ ... ... ]

[ ... ] he brought the sun [ ... ... ] the Light aeons [ ... ... the] Living Spirit spread out the universe [ ... ] the earth [ ... ... ] the Light and the Darkness in the beginning [ ... ] and the garments. He gave [them ... ... ] He set in order the [ ... ... ] and the wheel of the course [ ... ... ] in the heights, that they would [ ... ... ] of the Darkness. He stood firm [ ... f]ixed. He constructed the foundation [ ... ] all of it was set up.

Underneath [ ... ... ] all of them; and also above he [ ... ... thre]e earths, they being conjoined. He enclosed the earth in [ ... f]our mountains and three vessels [ ... ] in its seas and its mountains [ ... set in] order upon it. This universe stood firm [ ... ] a bond for the evil powers [ ... ... li]ght that they joined with the Darkness [ ... ]

Then the Third Ambassador! [He purified the Light] by his image. He became [redeemer of] all things.

(5) [ ... ] they are in all the [ ... ... ... u]niverse, and the fire blazes in it, and it is devoured [ ... ] all [ ... ] of the Light also, and he becomes purifier, and goes up to his essence. Conversely, the Darkness which is in [ ... ] out, and it is gathered in to a bond [ ... ] unveil [ ... ... ] and they go in to him and become [ ... ... pe]rfume [ ... s]et up [ ... an]d they reign for ever, and a single God comes to be [ ... ov]er the totality, being above the totality. You find no opponent from this time on against the Father, the King of the Light and the [ ... ... ]

[ ... ] which they occur in, from the beginning [ ... ] they are mixed and joined with one another.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: System-internal cosmogonic register: Separation/Heights/Aeons/Light, without institutional application or citation.
- ¶2: Cosmic dualism stated as ontology (Darkness vs Light), no audience-address or institutional anchoring.
- ¶3: Core cosmology (eternal separation; war of Darkness against Light) is present, but wrapped in later ‘to you / I have written’ authorial framing.
- ¶4: Standard cosmogonic action sequence (emanation/descent of First Man; armour of Light) in mythic-process voice.
- ¶5: System-building cosmogony: Living Spirit constructs/sets in order cosmic mechanisms (wheel/course; foundation), entirely within the cosmic architecture.
- ¶6: Architectonic description (three earths; four mountains; three vessels; cosmic ‘bond’)—classic impersonal, structural teaching.
- ¶7: Named cosmological redeemer-function (Third Ambassador purifies Light) stated without Gospel/NT vector.
- ¶8: Eschatological closure (universal fire; ascent of Light; binding of Darkness; one God over totality) remains wholly within the cosmic system; no institutional ‘pointing-at’.
- ¶9: Brief cosmological axiom on mixture/joining (the fundamental condition of the cosmos).
- ¶10: Canonical-source self-reference: listing Mani’s named books (‘Great Gospel’, ‘Treasury’, etc.) is a compilation/authorizing frame, not the older substrate.
- ¶11: Authorizing rhetoric about ‘writer/teacher of truth’ and ‘I have revealed’ belongs to a justificatory frame for the corpus, not impersonal cosmology.
- ¶12: Introduces the ‘first great lesson’ schema tied to Mani’s didactic program and includes autobiographical-sounding ‘my pains / my afflictions’; not a self-contained cosmic mapping sequence.
- ¶13: Editorial seam (flagged): pivot to institutional categories (‘teachers’, ‘leaders’, ‘elect’, ‘catechumens’) and exhortation to record unwritten material—cosmos → community administration.
- ¶14: Direct imperative address (‘You must remember… write… gather…’)—application voice, not substrate exposition.
- ¶15: Community stratification (‘elect’, ‘suckling babes’, sects/heresies) and pedagogical accommodation—church/institutional discourse.
- ¶16: Transmission/authority discussion (‘apostles’, ‘earliest fathers’, ‘church’, ‘book’) continues the prologue’s justificatory frame rather than cosmological mechanics.
- ¶17: Explicit Jesus-history in ‘land of the west’ with disciples writing parables/signs—material entering via Christian tradition/NT narrative vector.
- ¶18: Hagiographic-historical comparison (Zarathustra; disciples writing what is ‘read today’). Though Iranian, it functions as prologue frame about textual corruption, not cosmic mapping.
- ¶19: Buddha comparison (churches; disciples writing scriptures) serves the same justificatory frame, not substrate cosmology.
- ¶20: Argument about churches passing away due to not writing books—meta-textual institutional framing, not system-internal teaching.
- ¶21: Exhortation with application aim (‘you write after me… so that… leads you not astray’) and ‘commandments’ language—later pastoral layer.
- ¶22: Direct imperatives (‘remember! And write…’)—protreptic application voice.
- ¶23: Continues the exhortatory payoff of writing (‘they shall give benefit…’) within the same pastoral prologue function.

## Chapter 1
### Concerning the Ad[vent] of the Apostle.

*This chapter is dominated by later layers: dialogue framing, apostolic/church succession doctrine, and extended NT-derived Jesus/Paraclete material. Only small fragments (¶24–25) preserve schematic cosmological “mystery” content that stays inside the Light–Darkness system (depths/heights, war, mingling, ships, Adam). Even these appear embedded in Mani’s autobiographical Paraclete-revelation rhetoric, so they are extracted as MIXED rather than CORE.*

the hidden mystery, the one that is hidden from the worlds and the generations, the mystery of the depths and the heights. He unveiled to me the mystery of the Light and the Darkness; the mystery of the calamity of conflict, and the war, and the great [...], the battle that the Darkness spread about. Afterwards, he unveiled to me also: How the Light [...] the Darkness, through their mingling this universe was set up [...]

the way that the ships were constructed; to enable the gods of Light to be in them, to purify the Light from creation. Conversely, the dregs and the effluent [...] to the abyss. The mystery of the fashioning of Adam, the first man. Also, the mystery of the tree of knowledge, which Adam ate from; his eyes saw.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue setup: disciples questioning; hagiographic container language. No cosmological system exposition.
- ¶2: Dialogue attribution (“The apostle says… I will recount…”). This is compilation framing, not substrate.
- ¶3: Direct address (“O my beloved ones”) and analogy applied to “apostles” and “churches” (institutional-historical referents), not cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶4: Continuation of the farmer/gardener exemplum as application to apostolic activity; natural imagery is used to explain institutional succession, not cosmic architecture.
- ¶5: Institutional categories (elect/catechumens) and church-formation language; flagged as editorial extension and reads as ecclesial application.
- ¶6: Apostle/church ‘forms’ made free—still oriented to apostolic institution; not impersonal cosmological mechanics.
- ¶7: Returns to the farmer analogy applied to apostolic labour/harvest; no independent cosmic degree-structure.
- ¶8: Programmatic claim about apostles ‘from the beginning of the moulding of humanity’—salvation-history framing, not the old systematic cosmology.
- ¶9: Mythic-hagiographic account of an apostle leaving/assisting his church as helper/guardian; still institution-facing, not cosmos→cosmos correspondences.
- ¶10: Merchant simile applied to the apostle’s joy/rest; exemplum rhetoric, not substrate mapping.
- ¶11: Tree/fruits and ceaseless labour analogy explicitly used to justify uninterrupted apostolic succession; institutional doctrine rather than cosmological system description.
- ¶12: Apostolic succession plus ‘church’ ascent/descent; embedded salvation-history logic and Christianized vocabulary (‘sin’) rather than impersonal cosmic mechanics.
- ¶13: Enumerated salvation-history chain of apostles (Sethel… Buddha… Zarathustra… Jesus). This is historiographic/gnostic framing, not the oldest correspondential substrate.
- ¶14: NT-derived Jesus narrative (Jews, twelve/seventy-two, Judas, Satan). Entry vector is Gospel/NT tradition.
- ¶15: Passion/resurrection sequence in explicit NT narrative form (cross, grave, three days, breathing Spirit). Later Christian overlay.
- ¶16: Still within the Jesus-apostles dossier; dependent on the NT/apostolic narrative stream.
- ¶17: Paul as a named apostolic figure and reinforcement of Jesus’ apostles—NT figure/narrative anchor.
- ¶18: Moralizing decline theme (‘broad road’ vs ‘narrow’) in exhortational register; not cosmological mechanism.
- ¶19: Narrative about a ‘righteous man’ in the last church and the church perishing—salvation-history framing, not core cosmology.
- ¶20: Brief simile (‘tree… plucked… without fruit’) functioning as rhetorical bridge in the church-history narrative.
- ¶21: Explicit Paraclete saying attributed to the saviour (‘Just like the saviour said…’), from Johannine tradition.
- ¶22: Paraclete speech paraphrasing John (upbraid the world concerning sin/righteousness/judgement). Christian overlay.
- ¶23: Continues the Paraclete-from-Jesus-to-now timeline; even with month imagery, the conceptual entry is Johannine/Christian synthesis.
- ¶24: Cosmic-system vocabulary and mechanics (Depths/Heights; Light vs Darkness; war; mingling establishing the universe) suggest an older teaching summary, but it is embedded in later first-person Paraclete-revelation narrative, requiring extraction.
- ¶25: Begins with internal cosmological mechanism (ships purifying Light; dregs to abyss; Adam; tree of knowledge) characteristic of systematic Manichaean cosmology, then pivots into church-institution taxonomy (elect/catechumens), indicating a later graft.
- ¶26: Totalizing self-claim (‘seen the totality… single body… single Spirit’) functions as hagiographic climax and identity assertion, not impersonal substrate exposition.
- ¶27: Missionary itinerary and ‘good election’—biographical/apostolate narrative.
- ¶28: Historical travelogue with kings/regions and honor by Shapur—hagiographic biography layer.
- ¶29: Self-authorization (‘I have chosen you… holy church… not one among the apostles…’) and eschatological claim; community-facing and promotional.
- ¶30: Meta-textual authorial claim about having written books and repeating the lesson; compilation/teacherly frame.
- ¶31: Formulaic closing response: disciples rejoice, thank, and confess Mani as Paraclete—classic hagiographic closure.

## Chapter 2
### The Second, concerning the Parable of the Tree.

*This chapter is front-loaded with Christian overlay: Gospel proof-texting of the two trees (¶1–3, ¶10) and NT narrative exempla (Judas/Paul; ¶7–9). The oldest teaching substrate appears mainly in the middle and second half as systematic correspondential exposition: the “good tree”/“bad tree” each having five limbs (intellectual qualities) mapped to cosmic beings/ships/realms (¶11–12, ¶15, ¶17–19, ¶22–24). Two clear editorial/institutional grafts cross the cosmos→institution boundary by identifying “taste” and salvific process with the “holy church,” teachers, catechumens, and alms (¶13, ¶16), and another applies the “bad fruits” taste to “sects” (¶20). Several paragraphs are mixed where a basically cosmological mapping has had sect/church phrases inserted (notably ¶15, ¶22, ¶24).*

because [ ... ] tree, as it grows on this composite earth, [ ... ] date palm, because [ ... ], for not absolutely every part of the date palm is used!

[ ... ] their child, as it strikes [ ... ] while first one [ ... ] in them [ ... ] at the end there is one [ ... ] it occurs in its fibres and its branches [ ... ] There is a bad dust in them [ ... ] date palm [ ... ] before the tree that [ ... ] bad; because they give the decision [ ... ] the one that they name [ ... ] the type [ ... ] name it: tree [ ... ] is sweet [ ... ] writing [ ... ] they shall establish [ ... ] man [ ... ] it is sweet [ ... ] seed of the [ ... ] and it is used for their nourishment [ ... ] will explain [ ... ] may not join to the [ ... ] mankind, as they are named [ ... ]

[ ... ] how I will reveal to you [ ... ] the expound[er ... ] the trees [ ... ] the Father [ ... ] all of them, as he [ ... ] in the [ ... ] world [ ... ] this good tree, the [ ... ] the light of this good tree [ ... ] the Pillar [ ... ]

... the intellectuals that are cloaked upon its body [ ... ] this good tree. They are the light elements that are mingled, being compounded in the totality.

Behold, this is the good tree [ ... ]

[ ... ] it has five limbs. [They are consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind.] [Its counsel] is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. [Its insight is the Fir]st Man who dwells in the ship of living waters. Its thought is the Third Ambassador [who dwells in] the ship of living fire, that shines in [ ... ] Also, the mind is the Father who dwells in greatness, who is perfect in the aeons of light.

It is consideration that shall [ ... ] they shall be raised up [ ... ] is counsel; they shall be raised up to insight, which is the First Man who dwells in the ship of the night; from insight they shall rise up to thought, which is the Ambassador who dwells in the ship of the day. And he too, great glorious thought, shall bring them in to mind; which is the Father, the God of truth, the great Mind of all the aeons of glory.

This is the good tree that shall give good fruit, [ ... ] is the entire life and light for ever [ ... ] occurs [ ... ] ever, it is blessed [ ... ]

[ ... ] bad [ ... ] happen in [ ... ] from [ ... ] also [ ... ] the way that it is [ ... ] the good tree [ ... ] of the fire [ ... ] they are the ruling-power [ ... ] heaven and the wheel [ ... ] they are the five fleshes that walk [ ... ] fruit [ ... te]aching as of error, the mystery that [ ... wh]ich Satan appointed in the world.

[ ... I will] reveal to you and you [ ... ] The bad tree has five limbs. They are consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind. Its consideration is the law of death. Its counsel is transmigration [ ... ] in type after type. Also, its insight is the furnaces of fire [ ... ge]henna; which is full of smoke. Thought [ ... ] vessel. Its mind is the [ ... ] the lump, the last bond, the [ ... ], they that Satan cast away [ ... ] the great [ ... ] and they fall [ ... ] the Darkness [ ... ] all [ ... ] and they take away [ ... ] are swallowed in to the lump, and they are bound [ ... ] the fruit of Darkness, the one that they [ ... ] for ever and ever [ ... ] his face [ ... ] will arrive at that place; and they have no repentance therein.

[ ... ] this is the explanation of the bad tree, as I have explained to you, which you [ ... ] to it [ ... ] good fruit [ ... ] its fruit bad [ ... ] in it, in its existent days [ ... ] in it.

it being changed from [ ... ] which lies in its good earth [ ... life] and light; as death and Darkness do not exist [ ... ] Also, the bad tree is Matter [ ... ] [ ... wh]ich occurs in its bad earth [ ... ] Darkness and death.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Sets the parable within a Jesus-and-disciples scene; this enters via Gospel tradition rather than the internal cosmological system.
- ¶2: Explicit Gospel citation formula (“As it is written in the Gospel…”) and quoted saying; by definition a later Christian overlay layer.
- ¶3: Continues petition + “it is written… scriptures,” i.e., proof-text framing rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶4: Discusses how ‘sects’ interpret the parable; polemical/intra-community positioning rather than cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶5: Contains a late dialogue-title frame and sectarian polemic, but preserves an older natural-correspondential argument about mixture in composite nature (tree/date-palm example) used to explain why composite beings are not absolutely one-sided.
- ¶6: Impersonal, process-style exposition using a natural exemplar (fibres/branches/dust/seed) to describe mixture; no citation, no church-institution identifiers.
- ¶7: NT narrative exemplar (Judas; “Satan entered him… handed the saviour… Jews… wood/cross”); enters via Christian tradition.
- ¶8: Continues Judas Iscariot exemplar; Christian narrative overlay.
- ¶9: Paul persecution/conversion exemplar (“persecutor… church of God…”); even if used to argue ‘mingling,’ the entry vector is NT biography.
- ¶10: Introduces the ‘saviour preached’ parable and urges listening; this is a Christian overlay bridge into Mani’s exposition.
- ¶11: Begins internal cosmological identification of the ‘good tree’ with Light-system realities (Father, Pillar, Light), shifting from citation to system description.
- ¶12: Pure cosmological mechanics (light elements mingled/compounded; ‘intellectuals’ on the tree’s body) with no institutional application.
- ¶13: Editorial seam into institution: identifies ‘taste’ as the ‘holy church… teachers… catechumens,’ a cosmos→community mapping (flagged extension).
- ¶14: Deictic summary within the teaching sequence; no citation or institutional cross-mapping.
- ¶15: Core correspondential structure (five intellectual limbs mapped to named cosmic beings and ships) is present, but one limb has been editorially redirected to the institutional ‘holy church,’ which breaks the internal-system mapping rule.
- ¶16: Institutional/cultic graft: catechumens, alms, purification in the holy church; cosmos→church application rather than cosmological mechanism.
- ¶17: Systematic degree-ascent mapping (consideration→counsel→insight→thought→mind) aligned with cosmic stations (First Man/ships/night-day/Father). Internal cosmic architecture dominates.
- ¶18: Concludes the good-tree teaching in cosmological terms (life/light forever) without institutional anchoring.
- ¶19: Returns to internal mythic mechanics (ruling-power, heaven/wheel, five fleshes; Satan as cosmic agent). Though polemical language (“error”) appears, the paragraph still reads as system description.
- ¶20: Defines ‘taste’ as contemporary ‘sects… teachers… law after law’; this is explicit cosmos→social-institution identification.
- ¶21: Summarizes via appeal to ‘scriptures’ and ‘our master’; functions as proof-text/recognition claim (“all the sects know it”), suggesting a later scripturalizing layer in this parable-based chapter.
- ¶22: Core negative parallel to the five-limb ascent: a structured descent mapping into transmigration, furnaces/gehenna/smoke, vessel, and the final lump/bond in Darkness. A small editorial sect-reference is embedded in the first limb.
- ¶23: Wrap-up of the bad-tree exposition; despite second-person rhetoric, it does not pivot into church rules or Gospel citation and stays within the explanatory register.
- ¶24: Underlying core dualist ontology is present (Matter vs life/light; death/Darkness exclusion), but the paragraph is wrapped with audience-address/application voice that likely reflects editorial or homiletic delivery.
- ¶25: Beatitude-style exhortation (“Blessed is every one… separates them…”). Though consistent with dualism, it is applied to the hearer’s capability rather than expounding the system.
- ¶26: Conditional exhortation about separating principles leading to ascent/fall; application to the hearer rather than system mapping.
- ¶27: Concluding beatitude/confession-like line (“Blessed is whoever can believe!”); hortatory closure rather than cosmological exposition.

## Chapter 3
### The Interpretation of Happiness, Wisdom and Power; what they signify.

*The chapter’s core substrate is a systematic five-fold correspondential schema mapping “happiness / wisdom / power” across successive cosmic levels (Land of Light → Sun/ship of living fire → ship of living waters → elements). The opening paragraphs are a dialogue/topic-introduction frame. After the four cosmic iterations, an editor adds an institutional extension (“Now, moreover… in the holy church”), matching the prior syntactic pattern but shifting Side-B of the mapping from cosmos→church; the final line then closes with a beatitude-style exhortation.*

[The happin]ess of the glorious one is the Father, the God of [truth, who] is established in the great land of [light]. His glorious wis[d]om is his Great Spirit that [ ... ] below, which flows through all his aeons, and t[h]ey float therein. His great power is all the gods, the rich ones and the angels who were summoned from him as they [ ... ] t]hey that are called aeons [ ... ]

[ ... ] which is called] the sun [ ... the ship of] living fire, [ ... the Third Amba]ssador, the second greatne[ss ... The] glorious [happiness] is the [Living] Spirit, [ ... , the wisdom] is the Mother of Lif[e ... and great power] is [all] the gods, [the] rich o]nes and the angels who are within the ship.

Again, [h]appiness, wisdom and power exist in the s[hip of liv]ing waters [ ... ] the happiness [ ... ] the Mind of the Father. Also, wisdom [is the Vir]gi]n of [Li]ght. And the power that is [i]n the ship is [all] the go[ds], the [ri]ch ones and the angels who are established i[n it].

Again, these three exist in the elements: happiness, wis[d]o]m and power. The happiness is [the Pillar of Glory], the Perfect Man. Wisdom is the [five sons of the] Living Spirit; and great powe[r is ... the fi]ve sons of the First Man [ ... who are encl]osed and compounded in the totality, that [ ... ] while he supports the totality.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic opening (“Once again… speaks to his disciples… assembly of the church”).
- ¶2: Topic-question and contrast with ‘the world’ without cosmological mapping; functions as introductory dialogue framing rather than the impersonal correspondential substrate.
- ¶3: Continuation of introductory setup (“People boast… Now… is revealed… as I have told you”) in a discourse-address register, not systematic cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶4: Direct address (“listen that I may reveal to you”) introducing the schema; not itself the substrate’s structural mapping layer.
- ¶5: Systematic correspondential identification of the triad within the highest cosmic domain (Father / Great Spirit / gods-angels-aeons). Impersonal expository voice; cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶6: Second iteration of the same triadic mapping, now within the sun/ship of living fire; named cosmological beings (Third Ambassador, Living Spirit, Mother of Life) in a fixed structural pattern.
- ¶7: Third iteration (“Again… exist in…”) continuing the cosmological series with stable, list-like correspondential identifications; no institutional anchoring.
- ¶8: Fourth iteration situating the triad in the mixed cosmos (“elements”), still mapping cosmic entity→cosmic function/degree (Pillar of Glory/Perfect Man; five sons of Living Spirit; five sons of First Man).
- ¶9: Editorial seam: bridge connective “Now, moreover” extends the prior ‘Again… exist in…’ pattern but shifts mapping Side-B to institutional identities (holy church, Apostle of Light, leaders/teachers, elect/catechumens). By rule, the whole paragraph is a later pastoral graft, not MIXED.
- ¶10: Beatitude/exhortation-style closure (“Blessed… is every one who will know…”) and ‘five churches’ summary-language functioning as an editorial wrap-up after the institutional seam; not impersonal substrate exposition.

## Chapter 4
### Concerning the Four Great Days that have come forth from one another, together with the Four Nights

*The chapter is dominated by a schematic cosmological taxonomy (four Days / four Nights, each with twelve ‘hours’) in an impersonal expository voice—strongly CORE. The only clear later intrusions are brief institutional/application anchors (“his church”, “sects”, “old man”) and the formulaic opening/closing benedictions. No explicit NT citation overlay appears.*

The first great day is the Father, the God of truth, the fir[st ... ... m]idst the aeons of his greatness, in his [l]iving kingdom. The twelve hours of this great day are the twelve great rich Gods of greatness. These, who are the first evocations that he evoked (to mirror) his greatness, he spread out to the four climes, three by three before his face.

The second day is the Third Ambassador, the one who dwells in the light ship. His twelve hours are the twelve virgins that he evoked in his greatness.

[The third day is the Pillar of Glory, the great porter that is greater than] all [the po]rters; the one that supports all [ ... ], those above and those below. Its twelve hours are the five sons of the First Man; the five sons of the Living Spirit, who support all the weights of the universe; together with the summons and the obedience, which are counted to their ten brethren. These are the twelve light hours of the third day.

The fourth day is Jesus the Splendour. His twelve hours are the twelve wisdoms, which are his [light] h[o]u[rs].

[ ... ]: For just as four day[s ex]ist, s[o] too four nights exist.

The first night is the land of darkness. It has twelve black [shadows] therein, which are its dark h[o]ur[s]. The twelve shadows of the first night are the five elements of the land of darkness, [which] poured forth from its five sense organs; and [ ... ] which [ ... ] in its [fi]ve elements and its five [ ... ]; the five [sp]irits that dwell in its elements [ ... ]. These are the twelve shadows and spirits of the first night.

The second night is Matter, the sculptress [ ... ... ] who has sculpted [ ... ] and the entire ruling-power that is in the worl[ds of darkness]. She has fashioned it in five sense organs, five [male] and five female, two per world; together with the fire and the lust [that] dwell in men and women, inflaming [them] inward to one another. These are the twelve spirits of this second night.

Indeed she, this Matter, the thought of death, ga[ve] the King of the realms of Darkness and his powers strength for the war and the fight, against the aeons of greatness. Yet, he was caught! They laid a snare for him through the Fi[rst M]an. [He] was ta[ken] t[o ... ] through the powers [ ... He] was brought up from the land of death and s[et ... ab]ove and below in the [whole] universe; in the five par[t]s in the heavens above, as well as the five [parts] in the earths below; together with the heat and the cold that are the father and the mother, their fire and their l[u]st.

This is the second night that is begotten from the first night. They laid a snare for her through the First M[a]n. She was brought up by the hand of the Living Spirit, and set in this composite univers[e ... ] he[r], above and below. The [upper side is the sense] organs of males; the lower side [ ... ] fe[mal]es.

The thir[d night is the five] worlds of flesh [ ... five] male, and five female; that were begotten from the powers [abo]ve. They have fallen upon the earth, being displayed in wh[at is d]ry and what is moist; together with the fire and the lust [that d]we[l]l in them, which drives them inward to one another.

[Mat]te[r] herself, the thought of death, is the [mother] of them all; as among them she is named 'the night'. [The worlds] of male and female fleshes, together with the fire and the l[ust], shall [ ... ] twelve hours of [ ... ... ] night. Again, [it] too, the third night that is in the worlds of fl[e]sh, was begotten from the second night; which the Living [Spir]it crucified in the uni[verse ... , ab]ove and below in heaven [ ... ... ].

The fourth night is the law of Sin, which is the dark spirit who speaks in the twelve spirits. They are the nakedne[ss]es, the twelve zodiacal signs of Matter. They are her thrones;

And, also, the hours of this fourth night are the twelve e[vi]l spirits; the [ ... ] of the old man, which [are ... ... ... ... di]fferentiate the day and night [ ... ... of] death.

This is the fourth night [ ... ] that have come forth of one another. One has [ ... ... ] just like the days of lig[ht ... ] they manifested one another. So als[o ... ] they did arise out of one another.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue attribution (“Once again the enlightener speaks”) is hagiographic frame, not part of the substrate teaching.
- ¶2: Systematic degree-mapping within the cosmos (Day ↔ Father ↔ twelve hours ↔ twelve rich Gods ↔ four climes). Impersonal structural exposition marks CORE.
- ¶3: Continues the same internal cosmological mapping (Day ↔ cosmic entity ↔ twelvefold hypostases) with no institutional reach.
- ¶4: Cosmos→cosmos correspondential systematisation (Pillar / porter-function; pentads + summons/obedience making twelve). No exhortation or contemporary identifications.
- ¶5: The identification of the fourth Day with a cosmic being and a twelvefold set is CORE in form; “church” shifts the mapping outward to the community and is likely a later editorial application.
- ¶6: Summative benediction (“Blessed is every one…”) functions as editorial closure/exhortation rather than systematic exposition.
- ¶7: Brief structural hinge introducing the matching fourfold Night taxonomy; remains within the system (days↔nights) without institutional application.
- ¶8: Internal dark-cosmos structuring (night↔realm↔twelve shadows/hours, grounded in pentadic elements/sense-organs) is characteristic CORE schematisation.
- ¶9: Cosmic-architectonic description of Matter’s structuring work (sense-organs, male/female, fire/lust) stays within the mythic system despite anthropological language.
- ¶10: Mythic-cosmological process account (war, snare through First Man; cruciform emplacement above/below; pentadic heavens/earths) in impersonal narrative-exposition is CORE.
- ¶11: Genealogical unfolding (night-from-night) plus cosmic emplacement by the Living Spirit is internal system mechanics—CORE.
- ¶12: Systematic pentadic structuring of the ‘worlds of flesh’ and elemental display (dry/moist) continues the internal cosmological taxonomy.
- ¶13: Reinforces the night-genealogy and cosmic ‘crucifixion’/fixation above-below; still a self-contained mythic mechanics layer.
- ¶14: Zodiacal/twelvefold structuring is CORE in style, but the explicit identification with “sects” (and the ‘old man’ application) shifts the correspondence outward and signals later editorial use.
- ¶15: The twelvefold ‘evil spirits’ as hours continues the schema, but the editor anchors it polemically to “sects”; the remaining line still carries some anthropological residue (‘old man’), so extraction is conservative.
- ¶16: Structural recap in the same schematic voice (nights arising from one another) without institutional or citation markers—CORE.
- ¶17: Closing beatitude/exhortation (“Blessed is he…”) functions as editorial devotional closure rather than the cosmological schema itself.

---

## Chapter 5
### Concerning Four Hunters of Light and Four of Darkness.

*This chapter is largely a systematic correspondential teaching: each “hunter” is defined by a stable set of internal mappings (hunter → net/ship/sea) within Manichaean cosmic architecture. The main later intrusions are (a) dialogue frame formulas (“the apostle speaks…”) and (b) cosmos→institution/polemical pointing (“holy church”, “rules in every sect”, “erroneous teaching”), which break the substrate rule that both sides of the mapping remain inside the cosmic system. The beatitude-style closings are pastoral exhortation.*

The first hunter is the First Man who was sent from the greatness. He threw himself down to the five storehouses of Darkness, he caught and siezed the Enmity [... ...] his net also [... ...] out over all the children of Darkness [... ...]. His ship is his four sons who are swathed over his body. The sea is the land of Darkness [... ...]; his net is [...] and his powers.

The second hunter is the Third Ambassador. This one, for by his light image, which he revealed to the depths below, he hunted after the entire light that is in all things; as it is established in them. His net is his light image, [...] the whole universe and took it prisoner, to this likeness [... His] ship is his light ship. [The sea] is the universe [...] which were hunted after by his net [...] his [glorious] image.

The third hunter is Jesus the Splendour, who came from the greatness, who hunts after the light and life; and he [...] it to the heights. His net is his wisdom, [the] light wisdom with which he hunts the souls, catching them in the net. The sea is the error of the universe, the law of Sin [...], the souls that are drowning in it [...]. He catches them in his net. They are the souls [... th]em by his light wisdom.

The fourth hunter is the great counsel that [...] that lives in the circuit [...] entire universe in it today. Yet, at the end, in the dissolution of the universe, this very counsel of life will gather itself in and sculpt its soul in the Last Statue. Its net is its Living Spirit, because with its Spirit it can hunt after the light and the life that is in all things; and build it upon its body. Its ship, in which it is established, is this light cloud whereby it itself travels in the five elements [...], the great fire that will burn all the buildings of [... ...] in its net is the light and the life. It can rescue and free it from all bonds and fetters.

The first hunter is the King of they who belong to the Darkness, who hunted after the living soul with his net at the beginning of the worlds. His net is his fire and his Lust that he has put upon the living soul, with which he has entangled it [...], through all his powers.

[The] second hunter is the evil counsel that lives in [...] that hunts after the light [...] and [...] up from [...] the earths to heaven. It binds them with its powers, which bring them to the heavenly worlds above. Its net is [...] whereby they are drawn up from the abyss [to the heig]hts.

The third hunter is Lust [...] walks in every power of the flesh that walks [...] in the [...] the living souls [... ...] them in its bodies, which [... ...].

The fourth [hunter is the spirit] of Darkness, the law of Sin and death,

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue attribution (“Once again the apostle speaks…”) is compiling-community frame; no cosmological mapping content is expounded here beyond announcing a list.
- ¶2: Impersonal, systematic correspondential exposition: cosmic agent (First Man) mapped to ship/sea/net wholly within the mythic-cosmological system; no audience address or institutional identification.
- ¶3: Systematic cosmic mechanics (image-revelation, hunting light from “all things”) and fixed correspondences (net/ship/sea) remain internal to the cosmology; no NT citation formula or institutional application.
- ¶4: Core cosmological entity (“Jesus the Splendour”) presented in the same hunter/net/sea mapping style, but interrupted by an ecclesial identification (“holy church”), indicating later editorial application embedded in the older schematic.
- ¶5: Dense cosmological-process teaching (end-time dissolution, Last Statue, Living Spirit as ‘net’, light cloud/elemental travel, great fire) retains internal cosmic referents; the single temporal adverb “today” does not introduce institutions or direct exhortation.
- ¶6: Beatitude/exhortation to moral perfection and personal escape from punishment; shifts from describing cosmic structure to urging behavior/outcome for the listener.
- ¶7: Explicit structural hinge with dialogue/frame formula (“Once again he speaks… I will also teach you…”), functioning as editorial scaffolding between two lists.
- ¶8: Cosmic-agent description with internal correspondences (net=fire/Lust; target=living soul) and mythic time-marker (“beginning of the worlds”); no institutional polemic or audience address.
- ¶9: Systemic cosmological mechanics (binding/drawing light across earths→heavens; abyss→heights) expressed in the same hunter/net schema; stays inside the cosmic geography.
- ¶10: Personified cosmic force (Lust) acting as hunter; although anthropological (“flesh”), it remains within the mythic-ontological system rather than identifying contemporary institutions.
- ¶11: Opens with a plausible substrate-style identification of a cosmic hunter, then immediately pivots to present-world polemic and disciplinary rhetoric, marking a later application layer grafted onto the schema.
- ¶12: Beatitude/exhortation addressed to the hearer (“recognise… through knowledge”), promising salvation; not structural cosmological exposition.

---

## Chapter 6
### Concerning the Five Storehouses that have poured forth from the Land of Darkness since the [Beginning]; the Five Rulers, the Five Spirits, the Five Bodies, the Five Tastes.

*Chapter is dominated by systematic pentadic cosmology (storehouses→elements→trees→genera; five kings with bodies/metals/tastes; the King of Darkness’s shapes/properties/faculties). Late editorial/application material concentrates in the second half (notably identifying dark “spirits” with contemporary institutions/practices and ending with direct exhortation to the elect), consistent with the detected fatigue drift. Several sections also show signs of redaction/corruption but remain internal to the cosmic system.*

Five storehouses have arisen since the beginning in the land of darkness! The five elements poured out of them. Also, from the five elements were fashioned the five trees! Again, from the five trees were fashioned the five genera of creatures in each world, male and female. And the five worlds themselves have five kings therein, and five spirits, five bodies, five tastes; in each world, they not resembling one another!

The King of the world of Smoke [ ... ] who came up from the depth of darkness; this is he who is the head of all wickedness, and all malignity. The beginning of the spread of the war occured through him; all the battles, fights, quarrelling, dangers, destructions, fights, wrestling-contests! That is the one who first made arise danger and war, with his worlds and his powers. Afterwards, also, he waged war with the light. He pitched a battle with the exalted kingdom.

Now, regarding the King of Darkness, there are five shapes on him! His head is lion-faced; his hands and feet are demon- and devil-faced; his shoulders are eagle-faced; while his belly is dragon-faced; his tail is fish-faced. These five shapes, the marks of his five worlds, exist on the King of the realms of Darkness!

Now, there are five other properties in him. The first is his darkness. The second is his putridity. The third is his ugliness. The fourth is his bitterness, his own soul. The fifth is his burning, which burns like an iron () as if poured out from fire.

[There are] also three others in him! The first, that his body is hard and very tough, even as she has formed him [ ... ] cruel-hearted; namely Matter, who is the thought of death, [the o]ne who sculpted him from the nature of the land of darkness. This is the manner of the body of the ruler of Smoke. He is harder than every iron, copper and steel and lead; as there is no cleaver at all, nor any iron implement, can [ ... ] him and cut him. For Matter, his fashioner, has formed him [ ... ] strong and hard.

The second, that he wounds and kills by the word of his magic arts. His recitation and hearing, all his foolish instruction, make magic and invocations for him. When it pleases him, he can make an invocation over himself, and by his magic arts be hidden from his companions. Again, when it pleases him, he can be manifested over his powers and appear to them;

The third, that the King of the realms of Darkness knows the converse and language of his five worlds. He understands every thing he hears from their mouths, as they address one another; each one of them in his language. [Every] design they will consider against him, every snare they debate with one another to bring upon him, he knows them! He can also understand the gesturing they signal between one another. Yet, his powers and his rulers, who are subject to him, can not understand his wordy converse. While all these things are unveiled to him; still, their heart is not manifested to him. He knows not their mind nor their thought; he can not ponder their beginning and their end. Rather, he only knows and apprehends what is before his eyes.

Also, another different thing is found in the King of the realms of Darkness! For when it pleases him to move, he spreads all his limbs out and walks. When it comes to mind, he with[d]raws his limbs and takes them in, and is rolled (?) to his companions; and he falls to the ground like a grape and a great iron ball! He terrifies by his cry, he is frightful. He frightens his powers with his sound; because when he speaks, being like thunder in the clouds, he resembles the [ ... ] of the rocks [ ... ] When he cries out and [ ... ] and calls [ ... ] over his powers, they shall tremble and to[tter a]nd fall under foot; even as some birds would be [ ... ] the bird [ ... / ... ] and they fall down to the earth. Still, this thing only: he knows not what is far from him, he sees not who is at a distance, nor does he hear him. Rather, whoever is before his face he sees, hearing him and knowing him.

These signs and these evil marks are found in the chief of the demons and fiends, the King of all the mountains of Darkness [ ... the one] to [whom] the land of darkness has given birth, begotten in its cruelty, in its wickedness and its wrath [ ... ]

... more than all his fellow rulers, who are in all his worlds.

Gold is the body of the King of the realms of Darkness. The body of all the powers who belong to the world of Smoke is gold. And also, the taste of its fruits is salty.

In contrast, the King of the worlds of Fire is lion-faced, the foremost of all the beasts. Copper is his body. Again, the body of all the rulers who belong to Fire is copper. Their taste is the sour taste that is in every form.

Again, the King of the worlds of Wind is eagle-face. His body is iron. Also, the body of all they who belong to the Wind is iron. Their taste is the sharp taste that is in every form.

For his part, the King of the world of Water is fish-face. His body is silver. All the other rulers who belong to Water, silver is their body. Also, the taste of their fruit is the sweetness of water, the sweet taste that is in every (form).

Again, [the Kin]g of the world of Darkness is a dragon. His [body is lead] and tin. All the other rulers [who belong to the world of] Darkness, their body is lead and tin. And also, the taste of their fruits is bitterness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame attribution (“Once again… speaks to his disciples”) belongs to the compiling/redactional apparatus.
- ¶2: Highly schematic pentadic chain (storehouses→elements→trees→genera→worlds with fivefold attributes) in impersonal expository voice; fully cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶3: Cosmic narrative of primordial war and hierarchy; no audience-address, no institutional targets; remains within the mythic-cosmological system.
- ¶4: Classic correspondential substrate: realm/world-mark ↔ bodily/zoomorphic forms, systematically enumerated.
- ¶5: Enumerated fivefold intrinsic qualities; descriptive, non-exhortative; internal characterology of a cosmic ruler.
- ¶6: Internal cosmology of Matter as fashioner and the ruler’s material constitution; degree-like listing (“three others… the first…”). Despite textual uncertainty, it stays cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶7: Opens as internal description of a cosmic faculty (magic arts as intrinsic power), then crosses the diagnostic boundary with “nowadays / this world” and a direct command to the community—late application layer grafted onto older teaching.
- ¶8: Psychosymbolic cosmology (knowledge/limits of knowledge) described as a property of the dark king; no institutional pointing or exhortation.
- ¶9: Bestial/physical correspondences and sensory limitations presented as intrinsic traits; sustained impersonal descriptive mode.
- ¶10: Summary/recap within the same cosmological register; despite lacunae, remains internal to the mythic genealogy of Darkness.
- ¶11: Fragmentary continuation of the cosmological comparison; no frame or application markers.
- ¶12: Begins with classic correspondence (king/powers ↔ metal; fruits ↔ taste). Then shifts to present-tense socio-political application (“reigns today…”), indicating later editorial pointing.
- ¶13: Core portion is a tight realm→face/body-metal/taste schema. The latter ‘spirit… reigns in… leaders… sects’ is a later polemical mapping outward.
- ¶14: Pure correspondential pentad-member description until the ‘spirit’ clause, where side-B of the mapping leaves the cosmic system for contemporary cult sites.
- ¶15: Correspondential schema (face/metal/taste) is core-like; the excised ‘baptism of the waters’ identification is an editorial outward application (not a Gospel citation, but institutional polemic).
- ¶16: Core section continues the systematic mapping. The ‘till today’ oracular/possession material is a clear application pivot into the editor’s lived religious environment.
- ¶17: Direct second-person exhortation to the community (“my brethren… the holy elect… stay away…”) is institutional/pastoral application appended after the cosmological sequence, matching editorial-fatigue drift.

## Chapter 7
### The Seventh, concerning the Five Fathers

*The first half is dominated by an ordered, impersonal emanation-schema (Father → three emanations → further triads), characteristic of the systematic cosmological teaching layer. The second half shows clear editorial fatigue: institutional identifications (apostles/church), explicit elect/catechumen address (seam-flagged), and a Gospel-style citation/paraphrase (Jn 8) embedded in the third-power description.*

Five fathers exist; they were summoned forth one of one. [Also], one did come out of another!

The first Father is the Father of Greatness, the blessed one of glory, the [on]e who has no measure to his greatness; who also is the first only-begotten, the first eternal; who exists with fiv[e fa]thers for ever; the one who exists before every thing that has existed, and that will exist.

Now he, the glorious Father, summoned from him three emanations. The [fi]rst is the Gre[at] Spirit, the first Mother, who came out of the Father. She appeared first.

The seco[nd i]s the Belo[ved of] the Lights, the great glorious Beloved, [the one w]ho is honour[ed; w]ho came out of the Father. [He ma]nifested ou[t of h]im.

The third father is the Thir[d Ambassador], the eldest of [a]]] the counsell[ors; who came out of] the first Father. He appeared.

This is the first Father, the first power, the one from whom the three great powers came out. This is the first Father, the first eternal, the root of all the lights; from whom the three emanations came out. They have humiliated the Darkness. They have brought its heart's desire to naught. They have given themselves the victory. They have also given the victory to their aeons.

The second father, who came out of the first Father, is the Third Ambassador, the model of the King of lights. And again, he too summoned and sent out of him three powers.

One is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man; the one who bears up under all things; the great Pillar of blessing; the great porter, who is greater than all the porters.

The second is glorious Jesus the Splendour; [the] one through whom shall be given life eternal.

The third is the Virgin of Light, the glorious wisd[o]m; the one who takes away the heart of the [rul]ers and the powers by her image, as she fulfils the pleas[u]re of the greatness.

The third father, who came out of the second father: he is glorious Jesus the Splendour. And, again, he too summoned three evocations after the pattern of the second father.

The first power whom he summoned is the Light Min[d].

The second power whom Jesus summo[n]ed is the gre[at Jud]ge, who gives judgement on all the souls of mankind, [his] dwelling being established in the atmosphere under [ ... / ... ] wheel [ ... ] stars.

The third power is [the Y]outh, the gre[at ... ] light in his two persons, in [ ... ], I am speaking about that which has been established in the summons [and] the obedience.

The fourth father is the Light Mind. And, again, he too summoned three powers after the pattern of Jesus.

And also the fifth father is this Light Form; the one who shall appear to everyone who will go out from his body; and the thr[ee] great glorious angels who are come with her.

These are the five fathers who have come out of one another. They have appeared and man[ifes]ted through one another!

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue frame: "enlightener…apostle…disciples…revelation" situates a speaker and audience rather than expounding cosmological structure.
- ¶2: Programmatic, numbered cosmological schema stated impersonally (emanation chain).
- ¶3: Systematic definition of the highest divinity in timeless, superlative terms; no institutional anchoring.
- ¶4: Emanation mechanics in ordered triad form (core taxonomic voice).
- ¶5: Continues the triadic emanation list; “honoured” raises application-voice score but remains internal to the cosmological genealogy.
- ¶6: Completes the triad from the Father; impersonal listing of hypostases.
- ¶7: Cosmic process/outcome language (victory over personified Darkness; aeons) remains wholly within the mythic-cosmological system.
- ¶8: Iterative structural pattern: father → three powers (systematic cosmological recursion).
- ¶9: Cosmological hypostasis described by function/titles; no church/institutional identification.
- ¶10: Jesus the Splendour appears as a named cosmic power within a triad (core-compatible), though the phrasing “life eternal” could reflect later Christianized diction (uncertain).
- ¶11: Mythic-cosmic agency (rulers/powers as cosmic authorities) in impersonal explanatory voice.
- ¶12: Continues the emanation recursion (“after the pattern”); still internal taxonomy, not institutional application.
- ¶13: Opens as a cosmological triad member, then pivots to church legitimation language (“holy church”, “apostles”, “our pattern”).
- ¶14: Cosmic-judicial function located in cosmological geography (atmosphere/wheel/stars); no institutional address.
- ¶15: Begins with cosmological identification of a power (Youth) but then inserts a recognizably Johannine saying (NT entry vector).
- ¶16: Structural continuation of the father/triad pattern, with an added ecclesial gloss on “churches” likely from a later hand.
- ¶17: Institutional/mission language centered on “Apostle of Light” and “church of the flesh…humanity”; not impersonal cosmological mapping.
- ¶18: Hagiographic-institutional accompaniment motif (“counterpart…to the apostle…help…dangers”) rather than cosmological system exposition.
- ¶19: Seam-flagged institutional extension: elect/catechumens and renunciation requirement anchor to community discipline.
- ¶20: Core eschatological-cosmological appearance teaching with a later apostolic ‘pattern’ gloss appended.
- ¶21: Seam-flagged paragraph: although it lists angelic insignia, it culminates in an explicit institutional destinatary (“elect and the catechumens”), marking a late ecclesial application/extension.
- ¶22: Formal closure of the emanation chain in impersonal summary (system-complete statement).
- ¶23: Homiletic beatitude/exhortation (“Blessed is he…doers of good things”) oriented to moral identity and reward rather than cosmological mechanics.

## Chapter 8
### Concerning the Fourteen Vehicles that Jesus has boarded.

*¶2–8 preserve a compact, systematic correspondential list of ten cosmic “vehicles” (ships, pillar, summons/obedience, and five living elements) used to describe Jesus’ descent through the cosmological architecture. The chapter then shows strong editorial fatigue: ¶9 is a clear institutional graft (“holy church…brothers…sisters…catechumens”), and ¶10 is a homiletic/doxological summary with a beatitude rather than further cosmological mapping.*

When Jesus the Son of Greatness came to these worlds, at the time that he unveiled the greatness, he boarded ten vehicles. He journeyed in the universe by them.

The first vehicle is the light ship, since he received instructions from the Ambassador there.

The second carriage is the ship of the First Man, since his dwelling is established there.

The third is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man, since he shone forth there. He came below, he appeared in the world.

The fourth is the summons that he clothed upon his body. The fifth is the obedience that he swathed upon the summons.

The sixth is the living air, and since he assumed it too, he received anointment there. The seventh is the living wind that blows. The eighth is the light that illuminates. The ninth is the living water. The tenth is the living fire that is above them all.

He was journeying in these ten carriages among all the orders and all the [...], those above and those below.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic scene-setting (“Once again… speaks… to the congregation”) is the compiling community’s hagiographic frame, not part of the teaching substrate.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological exposition introducing a numbered structure (ten vehicles) within the system. Despite the name “Jesus,” this is Jesus-as-cosmic-being, not a Gospel citation.
- ¶3: List-item cosmological mapping (vehicle → cosmic location/relationship) stays entirely within the Manichaean cosmic hierarchy (Ambassador).
- ¶4: Systematic enumerated mapping; “First Man” and his ship are core cosmological actors/structures. (Manuscript page marker removed.)
- ¶5: Cosmic-station identification in numbered sequence; “Pillar of Glory/Perfect Man” functions as cosmological architecture in the descent schema.
- ¶6: Classic Manichaean redemptive mechanics expressed as correspondential ‘clothing’ (summons/obedience) without audience-address or institutional application.
- ¶7: Five-fold elemental sequence (living air/wind/light/water/fire) in a fixed enumerated structure—highly characteristic of the old systematic layer.
- ¶8: Wraps the tenfold scheme in universal cosmic scope (“orders…above…below”) still wholly internal to the cosmological system; retains lacuna.
- ¶9: Editorial seam/extension: the mapping crosses cosmos→institution (“holy church…brothers…sisters…catechumens”), applying the scheme to contemporary community structure rather than describing what cosmic things are.
- ¶10: Homiletic/doxological closure: moralizing salvation language, victory over ‘strangers,’ praise, and a beatitude (“Blessed is he…”) signal later pastoral framing rather than continued systematic cosmological mapping.

---

## Chapter 9
### The Explanation of the Peace, what it is; the Right Hand; the Kiss; the Salutation.

*The first half preserves a coherent cosmological teaching: five ritual “signs” are primordial mysteries enacted in the descent/ascent drama of the First Man (cosmos→cosmos). From ¶10 onward the same items are repeatedly re-applied to human custom “in the midst of mankind” and then to church initiation and hierarchy (cosmos→contemporary practice), with strong second-half pastoral/application drift (editorial fatigue). A probable scriptural/Christianized enthronement formula appears inside ¶14, embedded in an otherwise cosmological sequence.*

So, these five signs are the mystery of the First Man. He came forth with them from the aeon of light. Also, when he finished his contest, he went up with these good signs. They received him into the aeons of light.

The first peace is the one that the gods and the angels in the land of light gave to the First Man; when he comes out against the enemy. The gods and the angels were walking with him; escorting him, giving to him their peace and power, and their blessing and fortification. This is the first peace that the gods and the angels gave to the First Man; as he comes forth from the aeon of light.

The first right hand is the one that the Mother of Life gave to the First Man; when he comes out to the contest.

The first kiss is this one with which the Mother of Life embraced the First Man; as he separates from her, coming down to the contest. Even all the gods and the angels who are in the aeons of light were making to embrace him! [ ... ] all of them also, and the church tutelaries (?), and the kinsfolk who belong to the household of his people. They were making to embrace him as [he] separates from them, accompanying him out, embracing him with the kiss of affection and love.

The first salutation is the salutation with which the First Man made obeisance; when he comes out [to the depths] below. He was bending his knees, making obeisance to the God of truth, and all the aeons [of light] who belong to the household of his people; entreating them for a power to escort him, as he comes forth.

The first laying on of hands is the one that the Mother of Life bequeathed upon the head of the First Man. She armed him and made him mighty. She laid hands on him, and sent him to the battle. He descended. He completed his struggle with the great powers and the enmity.

By the [se] five signs and these five myst[e]ri[es] the First Man came forth from the aeons of light against the enemy. He humbled him. He was victorious against him by them.

Also, when he ascended from the war, he came in to the kingdom of the household of his people by these five mysteries!

The peace with which [he ascen]ded is the peace that the summons gave to him, [w]hen it was sent forth from the [Fa]ther of Life. It gave him the peace in the world of darkness. Also, that peace with which he descended is th[e sam]e as the one with which he ascended from the struggle!

Again, this second right hand is the one that the Living Spirit gave the First Man, when he [brou]ght him upward out from the struggle.

Also, this second kiss is the one with which the [Father o]f Life and the Mother of the living embraced the First Man, when he ascended from the struggle.

[The] second salutation with which the First Man made obeisance [ ... this] time to the God of truth and [ ... ] the blessed aeons and al[l] the aeons [ ... the] whole [land] of light, whe[n] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living brought the Man upward from the abyss of the struggle.

The second laying on of hands is this: When the Living Spirit brought the First Man upward from the war, he saved him from all the surging waves. He brought him upward, he gave him ease in the great aeons of light, which belong to the household of his people. He set [him] firmly before the Father, the Lord of the totality. Now, when he went up before the great Father of the lights, a voice came forth to him from the heights, saying: [ ... ] He received this great laying on of hands that he may become leader of his brethren in the new aeon.

At the time of their coming forth, the Light Form shall come forth before them; and she redeems them from the darkness to the light [ ... ] This Light Form calms the person, with the kiss and her quiet, from fear of the demons w[ho de]stroy his body. By her asp[ec]t and her image [the] heart of the elect one, who is come forth from his bo[d]y, shall be calm for [him]. Afterwards, the angel who h[o]lds the victory prize extends to him the right hand. And it draws him out of the abyss of his body, and accepts him in with the [k]iss and love. That soul shall make obeisance to its red[eem]er, who is this Light Form. And also, at the inst[ant when ... ] he shall be perfected and increased according to [ ... / ... ] in the household of the living ones, with the go[ds] and the angel[s] [An]d he receives the crown [ ... ] glory in the life for ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue/Q formula (“disciples… questioned the apostle”) functions as compiling frame; sets up topic rather than expounding cosmic mechanics.
- ¶2: Core teaching begins when the five signs are identified as intrinsic to the First Man’s descent/ascent (system-internal). The preceding sentences are an editorial/teaching-presentation bridge to ‘the world’ and to the speaker’s authority.
- ¶3: Systematic mythic explication (cosmic agents, land of light, descent against the enemy) without institutional/ethical application.
- ¶4: Tight cosmological identification: divine being → act toward First Man; no reach into human society.
- ¶5: Cosmic household scene (aeons of light; gods/angels; kin of the household). The language stays within the heavenly community rather than mapping onto contemporary rites.
- ¶6: Cosmic liturgy/action narrated as part of descent-myth; no “today/in the midst of mankind” hook.
- ¶7: Mythic arming/empowerment within the contest narrative; despite elevated rhetoric, it remains cosmological.
- ¶8: Summary of fivefold structure as operative mechanics in the cosmic struggle (degree/number pattern).
- ¶9: Completes the descent/ascent arc within the aeons; no institutional extension.
- ¶10: Opens with system-internal soteriology (Summons/Father of Life; world of darkness). The turn to “here and now” signals later application.
- ¶11: Clear seam from cosmological rescue (Living Spirit) to human handshake ritual.
- ¶12: Cosmic embrace is core; the ethnographic/social application is editorial.
- ¶13: System-internal reprise of obeisance within the ascent drama; the ‘today’ marker is a late anchoring to contemporary behavior.
- ¶14: Underlying narrative (Living Spirit’s rescue; presentation before the Father; leadership in the new aeon) is cosmological. The embedded enthronement formula is strongly biblicizing in diction, and the final ‘in the midst of mankind’ is editorial application.
- ¶15: Explicit historical legitimation (“proclaimed… by an apostle”) and explanation of institution in this world: hagiographic/compiling voice rather than the impersonal substrate.
- ¶16: Cosmic being (Light Mind) is used to ground church formation (“choose his church”): cosmos→institution mapping, characteristic of later ecclesial application.
- ¶17: Initiation order and community categories (“child of peace”, election to the faith, approach the church) are operational church teaching, not system-internal cosmology.
- ¶18: Explicit obeisance to the holy church and moral program (“good works”)—institutional/ethical overlay.
- ¶19: Church-building language (charity; conformed/built up) with ritual sequence applied to communal life; no cosmological mechanics being expounded.
- ¶20: Core is an internal eschatological choreography (Light Form; angel with victory prize; right hand/kiss as cosmic actions). The tail-end institutional cataloging suggests later community redaction.
- ¶21: Direct exhortation and address (“my brethren and my limbs… let you…”) anchors to audience practice; not substrate exposition.
- ¶22: Second-person encouragement/exhortation about understanding the rites; fragmentary but clearly audience-directed application.
- ¶23: Explicit church hierarchy (teachers, deacons, presbyters) and command to honor offices: institutional discipline layer.
- ¶24: Second-person pastoral exhortation (“my beloved ones…”) with practical/moral assurances; the cosmological substrate is no longer being expounded.

## Chapter 10
### Concerning the Interpretation of the Fourteen [Great Aeons] of Light, about which Sethel has spoken in [his Prayer]

*The chapter opens with a clear dialogue-frame request (disciples → apostle) and a brief promise-to-interpret. The core teaching appears as a compact, systematic fourteenfold enumeration (5+2+5+2), with cosmos→cosmos mapping (aeons/elements/sons/call-hearing/fathers) and an impersonal explanatory voice. The final paragraph preserves substantial cosmological teaching about the hidden land/plenitude and the Ambassador’s kingship, but is introduced through an interpretive link to Seth’s prayer; that Seth-prayer linkage is likely a later editorial anchoring to a gnostic tradition rather than the oldest substrate.*

Happen you know this, that the [ ... ] below abide in fourteen [great aeons ...]: the five elements, the sons of the First Man, together with the call and the hearing. They anoint them, living in them. These shall make seven, five plus two.

[ ... the] f[ive s]ons of the Living Spirit who [support] all [the wei]ght of the universe; together with the [Li]vi[ng] Spirit [and the Fir]st Man, their fathers. These are the fourt[een p]raises that he has proclaimed, the praise that is made of fourteen pra[yers], by which the universe is established.

for they are expl[ai]ned [ ... the la]nd that is not revealed. This land [ ... ... is estab]lished. They bore up under the enemy [ ... ... equa]ls them. The hidden plenitude that [ ... ] the Ambassador who came from the Father, the [ ... ] He came and stood firm in the midst of these [ ... ]: he is called the good father of [all] the ae[ons and gods] who are holy and conjoined. He has become leader and a great king after the likeness of the first Father, the lord of all these cou[nse]ls. This universe is held together by their will [ ... ] to the end of all things.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame: disciples question, honorific address (“our master”), request to be enlightened, and explicit anchoring to a named written source (“prayer of Sethel”). This is editorial apparatus wrapping the teaching.
- ¶2: Dialogue attribution (“Then the enlightener speaks…”) plus a promissory interpretive line. No cosmological exposition yet; functions as narrative linkage into the teaching.
- ¶3: Systematic enumeration and degree-structure arithmetic (five plus two) within the Manichaean cosmic system (elements/sons of the First Man/Call/Hearing). Impersonal explanatory voice; no citation formula or institutional application.
- ¶4: Completes the 14-structure (5 sons + 2 fathers) and grounds it cosmologically (“support… weight of the universe… universe is established”). The “fourteen prayers/praises” language reads as an internal cosmological-liturgical mechanism rather than church instruction.
- ¶5: Core cosmological myth/exposition (hidden land/plenitude; Ambassador-from-the-Father; cosmic kingship; universe held together by their will) is present, but it is front-loaded with an editorial move that grounds the teaching in Seth’s written prayer—an external textual anchor characteristic of later compilation and tradition-linking.

## Chapter 11
### [Concerning the Interpretation of] all [the] Fathers of [Light], who are distinguished from one another.

*This chapter is largely a catechetical list built on the formula “The beginning of X is Y,” where X often names human virtues/activities (grace, prayer, builders, deliverers). That “X→Y” mapping crosses from cosmos to lived religious/social language and looks like a later didactic application-layer scaffold. Nevertheless, many lines preserve older cosmological identifications/titles of the Fathers of Light and brief functional descriptions (e.g., Living Spirit with five sons; Light Mind as awakener/gatherer). The opening attribution and the closing beatitudes are later frame/application material.*

the great Father of the [ligh]ts; since [a]ll [graces] are given by his hand.

the Mother of [L]ife, the first Holy Spirit.

the T[h/i]rd Am[bassad]or, the king of the glorious realm that lies in this [wo]rld, [ ... ] of the King of the lights.

the Beloved of the Lights, who is honour[ed.

the First [Man.

[the] Living [Spirit], the Father of Life, who has distribu[ted about] his five sons from place to place.

the Great Builder, who is glorious.

the Pillar of Glory ... [ ... ]

[Jesus the Splendour]; or the one who delivers, as he frees whoever belongs [ ... ] from his words.

the Virgin of Light, [.

the Light Mind; who is the awakene[r of they] who sleep, the gatherer in of the ones who are sc[att]ered.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Clear FRAME markers (“Once again…”, “my beloved ones”) plus a didactic ‘beginning of…’ schema. What can be earlier is the bare cosmological assertion of the Father of the Lights as source/giver.
- ¶2: The ‘blessing/prayer’ side points outward to community piety, suggesting later catechesis; the divine title “Mother of Life” is core cosmological nomenclature, while “Holy Spirit” may reflect later Christianized terminology (uncertain).
- ¶3: Underlying content is purely hierarchical/cosmological identification (Third Ambassador; realm of glory), but the ‘beginning of every good counsel’ formula reads like a later pedagogical scaffold.
- ¶4: Short titular identification likely preserves older list-material; the surrounding ‘beginning of…’ pattern maps into human valuation (“honour”), suggesting later catechetical shaping.
- ¶5: “First Man” is a core cosmological entity; “trappers and hunters” is an obvious cosmos→human-profession mapping, likely later application.
- ¶6: The functional description (distribution of the five sons) is internal to the cosmic system and strongly marks older teaching; the ‘fighters’ label looks like a didactic hook aimed at human categories.
- ¶7: Divine epithet “Great Builder” fits cosmological function-language, but the explicit appeal to “architects and builders” suggests a later explanatory bridge to everyday roles.
- ¶8: “Pillar of Glory” is core cosmological vocabulary; the ‘porters’ framing looks like an application label grafted onto a list of Fathers of Light.
- ¶9: “Jesus the Splendour” here functions as a named Manichaean cosmic being (not a Gospel citation), which can belong to the older teaching stratum; the ‘deliverers’ category is an explanatory human-facing label.
- ¶10: “Virgin of Light” is core cosmological nomenclature; the ‘wisdom of truth’ framing suggests a later pedagogical assignment of abstract qualities to specific Fathers.
- ¶11: The functional descriptors (Awakener/Gatherer) are internal-cosmic roles and read like older substrate. The ‘honoured ones’ category again looks like a later explanatory heading.
- ¶12: Beatitude/exhortation aimed at the hearer (“Blessed is he… lodge this treasure… fasten… in his heart!”). This is application voice rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶13: Continuation of beatitude and exhortation (“continue in their belief… inherit… eternal life”), functioning as pastoral application/closing rather than core system-description.

## Chapter 12
### Concerning the Interpretation of the Five Words that are proclaimed in the Universe

*This chapter fragment is dominated by dialogue-frame and sect-referential setup (Baptists / “Purified Ones”). The oldest recoverable layer appears only as brief enumerative identifications of the “words” with Manichaean cosmological beings (Father; Third Ambassador). The interpretive hook (“they call it by this name”) suggests the teaching is being applied to contemporary sectarian terminology, so the seam likely falls between the sectarian naming and the bare cosmological identifications.*

the Father, the l[ife ...] this unnameable, whom no one [...] his name.

The second [word is the Thir]d Ambassador [...] of life, whom they also call the father of [...] [...] the first Father.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue apparatus: “Once again…”, “disciples… questioned”, “apostle”, plus explicit anchoring to contemporary sects (“Baptists”, “Purified Ones”) and their terminology. This is editorial framing, not substrate exposition.
- ¶2: What remains is a bare cosmological naming (“the Father… this unnameable”) characteristic of core expository diction; the excised lead-in ties the line to external groups’ naming practice, which is a later interpretive application.
- ¶3: Systematic enumerative identification (“The second word is…”) mapping a ‘word’ to a defined cosmological being (Third Ambassador). No explicit institutional/pastoral address or NT citation; reads like a fragment of an older five-fold list, though transmitted here in an interpretive chapter context.

## Chapter 13
### Concerning the Five Saviours, the Resurrectors of Those Who Are Dead, Together with the Five Resurrections

*This kephalaion is chiefly a pentadic, systematic enumeration of five “resurrectors/saviours” with cosmological agents (Father, Light Mind, etc.). The extant text is heavily lacunose at the start (first/second items partly lost) and ends with a generalized beatitude that reads like later exhortatory/pastoral closure rather than impersonal mechanism-teaching. The opening is a clear hagiographic dialogue frame.*

The second resurrector is [...].

And the third resurrector is the Father, the First [...], because by his word shall all lives [...] gather in and go to him, and they receive [...] and rest.

The fourth resurrector is the Light Mind, because [...].

The fifth resurrector to the good [...] is the Father, the First established one [...]. [He ...] all the powers, and he stands firm [...]. He shall unveil the [...] over them [...], and he draws them to him [...], his will [...], and he [...] them, and he stands them up [...] there, and they remain until [...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame (“Once again the enlightener speaks”)—a compilation wrapper, not the substrate teaching.
- ¶2: Too fragmentary to date securely, but it appears to continue the broken opening frame/title line rather than giving a cosmological mapping or mechanism.
- ¶3: Enumerative, impersonal degree-structure (“second resurrector”) consistent with the pentadic teaching substrate; no institutional or citation markers.
- ¶4: Cosmological mechanism: resurrection effected “by his word,” lives gather and rest—cosmos→cosmos description without audience address or institutional pointing.
- ¶5: Pentadic enumeration naming a cosmological being (“Light Mind”) in impersonal explanatory mode.
- ¶6: Sustained cosmological process-description (unveiling, drawing, establishing, standing up powers) within the cosmic system; no institutional application language.
- ¶7: Beatitude-style address (“Blessed is every one…”) shifts from impersonal mechanism to generalized exhortatory blessing; functions as later devotional/pastoral closure rather than the enumerative substrate.

## Chapter 14
### The Interpretation [of] the S[i]lence, the Fast, [the Peace], the Day, [and] the Rest; [what] they are.

*Despite the chapter title’s ritual-sounding keywords (silence/fast/peace/day/rest), the surviving content is mostly cosmological identification/exposition (First Man, ship of living waters, Pillar of Glory, great aeon). The only clear editorial layer is the opening dialogue-question frame and a closing beatitude/doxology. Several paragraphs are heavily lacunose; where a teaching paragraph includes an explicit teacher-attribution (“the enlightener ...”), that phrase is treated as later framing and stripped, leaving the underlying cosmological explanation.*

[...] the gatherer in [... ...] [...] is the Pill[a]r of Gl[or]y [...]

The one they call [...] it is the [Fi]rst [M]an who dwel[ls i]n the ship of living waters [...] li]ght [...] he is c[alled ...] that they have [...] glorious [...]

[...] cal]l it 'day' [...] uni]verse as they shall [...] in the great aeon [...] are spread in the universe, it is [...] established in the [...] and the so[u]ls [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue/Q&A frame: disciples question the enlightener; petition formula (“We implore you, our father...”). This is compiling-community apparatus, not the systematic substrate.
- ¶2: Core is the cosmological identification of a ‘gatherer’ with the Pillar of Glory (a fixed element of Manichaean cosmic mechanics). The adjacent language about bodily lusts reads as interpretive/ascetic application rather than impersonal system-description.
- ¶3: System-internal mapping/identification: a named cosmic hypostasis (First Man) located in a defined cosmic vehicle/domain (ship of living waters). No address to church practice, no citation, no institutional polemic.
- ¶4: Underlying content is impersonal cosmological exposition (great aeon; distribution/establishment in the universe; relation to souls). The intrusion of “the enlightener ...” signals a later dialogic presentation layer wrapped around older teaching.
- ¶5: Beatitude/doxological close (“Blessed is he... remains for ever and ever”), characteristic of devotional/pastoral stitching rather than systematic cosmological correspondence-teaching.
