## Chapter 15
### [Concerning the ... ...] Five [Parts ...] Worlds of [...]

*This chapter is predominantly core cosmological teaching (pentadic structure → doubling into male/female → twelvefold complex with zodiacal linkage). It is framed by a hagiographic dialogue opening and a brief doxological close. The only clear late graft is the end application that moves from cosmic antagonists to the "holy church" and sect polemic (¶8–9), which crosses from cosmos→cosmos mapping into cosmos→institution pointing.*

Did Death arise and stand firm [...] up? Or else did it turn itself from [...] its armour [...] in its [...] but it finished [...] the two [...].

[...] it moved it and [...] they that are in the [...] of the earth of darkness [...] first part.

Yet, after a long time, as there was not [...], it gushed and streamed up [...] the five elements of Death that gushed [...] which are smoke, fire, wind, water and darkness [...] the second time [...] they were sculpted [...] those in which Death was established [...].

The fourth part is the evil fruits [...] in the five trees that are in the five worlds of the land of darkness.

The fifth time it spread itself out to its creatures that exist in the five worlds; the ones that were formed and generated from the five fruits of Death. They are the five worlds of flesh, the five creations of darkness in which Death has been established. It spread itself out to ten parts, five male parts and five female [...].

Now, like the land of darkness, in that its spirit and its [...] they nourished the five elements [...]; this is also [how] the ten parts that are in male and female, the fire and lust of the flesh [...] them, as they yoke them with one another [...] they were established against the First Man. This is [...] of the darkness, it was condemned for ever.

Alongside the mystery of these twelve parts that came about in the darkness [...] against the First Man. The twelve [...] the twelve spirits of error that came about from the twelve signs of the zodiac [...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening scene and disciple-dialogue attribution are compilation frame. The remaining question is a substantive cosmological query about the activation of Death in the two-essences system, likely preserving the older teaching agenda beneath the frame.
- ¶2: Fragmentary but remains within the cosmic system (earth of darkness; a numbered/ordered "part"), without institutional or citation markers.
- ¶3: Systematic pentadic cosmology (five elements named) in impersonal explanatory voice; no appeal to external institutions or moral exhortation.
- ¶4: Correspondential/pentadic mapping (fruits/trees/worlds) stays entirely within the darkness-cosmos architecture; numbered-part structure is a core marker.
- ¶5: Degree-structure teaching: five→ten (male/female doubling) described as a cosmic process of generation; still cosmos→cosmos without present-world anchoring.
- ¶6: Mechanistic explanation of activation (nourishing, yoking) and antagonism against the First Man—classic cosmological process discourse, not institutional application.
- ¶7: Twelvefold extension tied to zodiacal signs remains an internal cosmological taxonomy (parts/spirits/signs) rather than church or ethical address.
- ¶8: Crosses the boundary from cosmic description to cosmos→institution pointing: "dwells in the holy church" anchors the antagonist pattern to the contemporary ecclesial sphere (editorial application).
- ¶9: Explicit church anchoring ("light that dwells in the church") plus sect-polemic functions as contemporary application rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶10: Doxological closure (praise formula) functions as editorial/hagiographic framing rather than teaching content.

## Chapter 16
### [Concerning the Five] Greatnesses who [went forth] against the Darkness.

*Dominantly CORE: a systematic pentadic listing of the five Greatnesses (emanational series) followed by a long, internally-cosmic exposition of the five ‘advents’ via similes (judge, king inspecting works, butter in milk) and eschatological mechanics (Summons/Obedience; Last Statue; great fire). Later layers appear as (a) FRAME dialogue-attribution at the start, (b) a few small audience-address intrusions (MIXED), (c) a Christian salvation-history/tree-of-life segment tied to Adam/Eve (OVERLAY), and (d) a closing beatitude/exhortation (PASTORAL).*

[...] the Darkness, it was defeated by five greatnesses.

The first greatness is the Father of Greatness who exists in calm and hiddenness; as he is established in his light-earth, in his own essence.

Four greatnesses were manifested and came forth of him. They were appointed to ten parts, and established in total twelve.

The second greatness poured out and was manifested. It was revealed from the first greatness. It is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, together with the First Man and his five sons; these are the second greatness.

The third greatness is the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder, the Living Spirit, and the five sons of the Living Spirit. These are the third greatness.

The fourth greatness is the Third Ambassador, and the Pillar of Glory, and all the light powers who were revealed from him. These are the fourth greatness.

The fifth greatness who came from the Father is Jesus the Splendour, and all his powers whom he summoned, his emanations who were [...] They poured out and were manifested from him.

This is, so that they were spread out and [...] fives: these five [sons] of the First Man [...] the entire building; another five are the five sons of the Living Spirit who are spread out to five places, supporting [...]

[...] total in twelve parts that [... There are] another two added to them who are the summons and the obedience. They are the father and the mother whom Jesus the Splendour by his advent has appointed as successors to his place [...] the great earth and the [...] in them.

[The advent ...] of the First Man who came [...] belonging to [...] existing in a kingdom [...] esteem before they who belong to his household [...] his enemies [...] and suffering by their wickedness; and they take from him [...] and a richness; and they imprison [him in] captivity and take from him their companies (?). Yet, he [...] by his humility; and he takes heart and restrains his enemy [...] in silence.

Just as with this man, this noble son whose enemies surround him, this also is how the advent of the First Man happened, [...] rulers, the enemies of the light.

In contrast, the advent of the Living Spirit and the commandment that he received, whereby he came upon all the rulers, it differs from that of the First Man. His advent [...] the advent of the First Man.

The advent of the Living Spirit is like this, when he was sent from the greatness that he might bring up the First Man. He resembles a judge the king would send to overturn the violence to that man, the noble son against whom his enemies sinned. And he, this judge, [...] hard and cruel [...] and he gives the means [...] by his righteousness and his strength; and he also judges all who are condemned according to their rebellious heart, and repays each according to his deserts. They who are worthy of blows he strikes with hard leather whips. Again, these who are worthy of cutting off their limbs, or indeed their [...] and he repays them accordingly. [...] and he binds and fetters them, and hangs by [...] and thus he does to them with a righteous judgement. And he kills them to flay their skin [...] so that they will be killed by a death [...] until they die. Again, [they for] whom it is fitting [...] to banish from their dwellings he may remove according to a righteous judgement; just as they had faulted and sinned against the noble son.

Also, the associates in the mystery and the assistants of this judge, these who belong to him in great strength; in an instant and haste they shall do the will of their master.

Now, just as this genuine judge, who was sent from the king to overturn the violence to this man, the noble son: this is also the case with the Living Spirit. He also was sent from the King of the lights to overturn the vengeance and violence to the First Man, against whom the King of the realms of Darkness had sinned. He faulted and sinned against him, and also his rebellious powers. Therefore, when (the Living Spirit) came, he bore up the First Man.

Also, all the rulers, the powers of Sin who had faulted and sinned against the sons of the First Man, he has judged them according to a righteous judgement. He has bound them in heaven and earth. He put each one to the place fitting for him; he weighed each of them according to his cruelty and oppression. While some of them he enclosed in the prison, others he hung head down. Some [...] are crucified, others are sat down all the time. Some among them are fixed beneath their associates, restricted by a strong chain; others he made the holders of authority over their companions beneath, so that they may do their pleasure amongst these below them.

when the Father of Life established the judgements [...] He was revealed to all the powers of Darkness. He judged them as a true giver of judgement [...] This is the first judgement that the Living Spirit gave the rulers [in respect of the] First Man. Indeed, he gave victory to the First Man corresponding to his victory; but he condemned the King of the realms of Darkness corresponding to that by which he had erred.

[...] this chain, of this sort, became [...] the earth till the end time when the universe will be dissolved; and it perishes in the great fire and is separated. While the light goes to its country, the Darkness remains in the chain and fetter for ever.

The advent also of the Third Ambassador, when he came to inspect they who are imprisoned in the zone: he is like a great king who comes and inspects craftsmen [...] and [...] and [...]

Also, they who are good, of the craftsmen and architects and artisans whom he has there in each place: he shall see that they have worked well, and have fallen to his works in their fashion. He shall rejoice with great joy [...] He shall thank the artisans who have constructed his works [and completed] his orders.

Now, just as this great king who comes and inspects his works and fortifications, so also is the advent of the Third Ambassador. For, when he too came, he saw [his works] established and prepared and [...] series of the rulers prepared and established there, above and below, outside and within, in the great pattern that his brothers had made. They had come before him to the contest, they who had fixed and created the universe before he came. He saw the gods and the angels established and controlling the fortifications [...] guarding the entire ruling-power [...] which his first powers had done. They are the First Man and the Living Spirit and the Great Spirit, the Mother of Life.

Therefore, when he saw them, that they had created everything with great wisdom, [...] as they looked he plundered (?) [...] in his advent, by which he came, he was unveiled to them. He says to the Living Spirit: “The judgement that you have [...] good, what you uttered [...] you gave to them correspondingly; so that they were moved and raised up to that of which they were not worthy”.

Again, this is like this simile of the advent of Jesus the Splendour. It happened like this; because he too set out [...] his power and his wisdom, in his [...]-ness [...] in his glorious wisdom he destroyed them. He bound and crushed them like the fire that shall burn and destroy trees. This is also how he performed his will in the zone, among the many powers,

Then the summons and the obedience, the great counsel that came to the elements, which are set in conjunction: it mixed with them, it was established in silence. It bears up [...] until the end time when it can arise and stand firm in the great fire. It will gather to it its own soul, and sculpt it in the Last Statue. You will also find it sweeps out and casts from it the pollution that is foreign to it. However, the life and the light that are in all things it gathers in to it, and builds upon its body.

The time when this Last Statue will be perfect in all its limbs, then it can become free and ascend from that great struggle through the Living Spirit its father, the one who comes and brings a [...] limb. He brings it up from within this gathering (?), the melting down and destruction of all things.

Now, this counsel, that summons and obedience, resembles thus cooled butter. If a person will take and set it in hot milk, it melts down and soaks in with this hot milk. It melts in another time in silence and quiet to the midst [...] yet also, of the power and the taste of this milk [...] is drawn to (the butter), and (the milk) sets and becomes cold [...] in [...] and the [...] this milk it shall clarify and discard. However, the oil and the grease and the ‘beauty’ of the milk it shall gather in to it.

Again, this too is what the advent of that thought is like, the counsel of life, which is the summons and obedience that came to the elements. This is the one that at the end can arise and stand firm, and will gather in to it all the lives, and the light that has remained behind in everything. It can build them upon its body; it will gather them in to it [...] in its image for ever and ever [...] from this time.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Teaching claim is cosmological and impersonal; the speaker-attribution to ‘the enlightener’ is a later Kephalaia frame wrapper.
- ¶2: System-internal cosmological definition (being→state/place/essence), no citation, no audience address.
- ¶3: Enumerative degree/number structure (pentad, ten, twelve) characteristic of the systematic teaching layer.
- ¶4: Emanational chain stated as ontology (“revealed from”), with fixed cosmic names and a fivefold sub-structure.
- ¶5: Systematic cataloguing of cosmic hypostases and their pentadic entourage; page marker removed per rules.
- ¶6: Continues the impersonal emanational schema; cosmos→cosmos mapping only.
- ¶7: Though ‘Jesus’ vocabulary triggers a Christian register, this paragraph functions purely as a cosmological hypostasis within the emanational pentad, without Gospel citation or salvation-history narrative.
- ¶8: Explicit pentadic correspondential structuring (five→five→five places), purely internal to the cosmic architecture.
- ¶9: Numerical/theological rationalization stays within the system (cosmic entities, roles, places); no institutional or contemporary-world mapping.
- ¶10: Pedagogical simile/narrativized exposition used to explain a cosmic event; still descriptive, not exhortative, and not anchored to contemporary institutions.
- ¶11: Explicitly ties the simile back to cosmic rulers; internal mythic-historical explanation.
- ¶12: Comparative structural teaching of successive divine missions (‘advents’), not pastoral instruction.
- ¶13: Extended correspondential simile (judge/king) explaining cosmic justice mechanics; no citation formulas or ecclesial targeting.
- ¶14: Still within the simile’s cosmic-judicial register; not institutional ‘mystery’ practice but assistants of the cosmic agent in the analogy.
- ¶15: Clear cosmos→cosmos mapping: King of Lights/Living Spirit/First Man vs. King of Darkness and powers; explanatory, not hortatory.
- ¶16: Cosmic punitive ordering of rulers across heaven/earth; ‘Sin’ functions as a cosmic power-category (personified force) within the system.
- ¶17: Core content is a correspondential balancing of judgements/victory/condemnation within the cosmic system; the opening direct address is a later rhetorical intrusion.
- ¶18: Eschatological mechanics (end time, dissolution, great fire, separation of light/Darkness) presented as process, not exhortation.
- ¶19: Advent + simile framed to explain a cosmic inspection in the ‘zone’; remains system-internal.
- ¶20: Continuation of the king/inspection simile; pedagogical but not applied to church offices or ethics.
- ¶21: Cosmic architecture vocabulary (fortifications, pattern, rulers, gods/angels) with explicit internal identification of earlier powers.
- ¶22: Still intra-cosmic discourse (Third Ambassador addressing Living Spirit) evaluating judgement as a cosmic operation (‘correspondingly’), not citing Gospel/NT.
- ¶23: Tree/fig-garden destruction imagery in a ‘Jesus the Splendour’ advent context reads as Christianized salvation imagery and parable-like language; not a closed cosmos→cosmos mapping and plausibly mediated by Christian tradition.
- ¶24: ‘tree of life’ planting and ‘good fruit’ language aligns with biblical/Christian symbolic stock; functions as salvation-history imagery rather than systematic cosmological exposition.
- ¶25: Opening portion is still cosmos-internal (Jesus the Splendour acting ‘in the zone’ among powers) and can stand alone; the Adam/Eve ‘good news’ section is a clear crossing into salvation-history narrative typical of later Christian overlay.
- ¶26: Highly diagnostic core mechanics: cosmic mixture into elements, silent working, eschatological gathering, Last Statue formation—system process language.
- ¶27: Continuation of eschatological process (perfection of limbs, ascent, destruction/melting); slight second-person residue is absent here—still descriptive.
- ¶28: Classic correspondential simile used to explain cosmic mixture/separation: sensory/physical process mapped onto metaphysical gathering and purification.
- ¶29: Summative restatement of the ‘counsel’ as a cosmic collector/building-body principle; entirely system-internal eschatology.
- ¶30: Beatitude-style exhortation (‘Blessed is every one who will believe…’) addresses hearers’ belief and salvation; shifts from cosmological mechanism to audience-directed soteriological encouragement.

## Chapter 17
### The Chapter of the Three Seasons

*This chapter is overwhelmingly cosmological and structural: it defines the three times/seasons by anchoring them to major cosmic events (First Man’s descent/ascent; construction of the universe; Ambassador’s image → final Statue). The only later material is the dialogue/hagiographic container (“apostle…disciples” Q&A attributions), which can be cleanly stripped without damaging the underlying system-teaching. No NT citation formulae or institutional/pastoral grafts appear; “Jesus” functions as a Manichaean cosmic agent within the redemption sequence, not as Gospel overlay.*

From the time when the light came forth and was crucified in the darkness, to the season when the Statue will ascend and the entire light, which was conjoined with the darkness, will be purified and cleansed. From that time on, from that time to the season of the end totals three seasons.

Three seasons: from the time when the First Man went down to the abyss, and waged the war and the struggle against the King of the realms of Darkness and his other powers. He was victorious, while his sons remain behind with them in the worlds [... ...] in the [...] until he shall catch all of them [...] he bound them all up, so that not one of them escaped his hand. He won a great victory [... ...] The First Man spent human lifetimes and generations in the land of darkness, until he prevailed [...] he fulfilled the will of the greatness that was commanded of him.

Then they sent the Living Spirit, the Father of Life. He brought the First Man up from the struggle, he brought him up to the land of light. This is the first period, from the descent of the First Man to his ascent.

The second season is from the time when the First Man ascended. The Living Spirit came, and the Mother of Life. They set in order the things of the universe, they set up [...] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living set in order [...] the universe, up to the moment when they go out [...] it was reckoned to the second season.

[...] the universe [...] the Third Ambassador spread [...] that is in the [...] living; he [...] he separated beauty from all the powers and scooped it up to the heights, he swept the waste to the abyss, and he [... ...] Now, from the moment when the Ambassador revealed his image he purified the light. Sin spurted up towards him. He hid his image. Sin came [...] from the darkness. It sculpted the trees and the abortions fell down. Afterwards they sculpted Adam and Eve in the flesh. Until the moment when Jesus was sent and he [...] as he performed there his will. He gave the hope to Adam, and ascended to the heights.

Now, from the time when the Ambassador displayed his image until the season when the Statue will ascend at the end, shall be reckoned to the third season.

[...] lasts long in the time that the First Man spent in the world of darkness. It is a very great count of time, more than the (other) two seasons!

The first season, which the First Man spent in the world of darkness, is how much longer than the time that this universe has been? While it is established?

If you should say that [... ...] it is small, if you should [... ...] to him [...] and you consult [...] you will tell him: Twenty times! It is greater again in this other one that you will consult: Thirty times! Less also is this other one [... ...] people will receive the of the trees [... ...] the years and the generations [...] earth to the heights. This is [...

This is the third period, from the time when the Ambassador revealed his image until the Statue will ascend from the lump. It again, that time, is greater than the intermediate; but it does not come equal to the time of the First Man. For this intermediate season, in which the Father of Life and the Mother of Life constructed the universe with its orders, is shorter than that of the First Man.

Great is the period of the First Man! It lasts longer than the season in which the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the world and the Statue ascends. From the time too when the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the universe [... ...] when the Father of Life and the Mother of Life shaped the universe.

These are the [...] three periods of time that occur.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Teaching content is a cosmic time-span definition (Light–Darkness mixture → purification/Statue ascent), but it enters through a Mani-community dialogue frame marked by “Once again” and “apostle…disciples,” which must be stripped.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological-historical exposition: First Man’s descent/war/victory and long duration in the darkness—cosmos→cosmos narration without institutional application or citation.
- ¶3: Systematic periodization anchored to named cosmic beings (Living Spirit/Father of Life) and a clean structural definition of the first time.
- ¶4: Core cosmological mechanics: ordering/constructing the universe by the divine hypostases; numbered-season structure continues with no editorial pivot.
- ¶5: Cosmic-agent sequence (Third Ambassador, Image, Sin as a personified power, Adam/Eve fabrication, Jesus as redeemer agent) remains internal to Manichaean cosmology; no Gospel citation markers or church application.
- ¶6: Concise structural definition of the third season using cosmic termini (Image revelation → Statue ascent).
- ¶7: Pure dialogue apparatus (“disciples…apostle”) with no recoverable teaching content; functions as compilation frame.
- ¶8: Underlying teaching is comparative time-measure (duration of First Man’s period vs the other two), but presented inside Q&A frame language.
- ¶9: Substantive cosmological question about comparative durations; frame attribution is late, but the queried structure belongs to the systematic teaching.
- ¶10: Comparative/analogical quantification of the seasons’ lengths (rhetorical second-person but not pastoral/institutional); the speaker-tag is a later dialogue wrapper.
- ¶11: Systematic comparison across the three times; cosmos-only referents (Ambassador/Image, Statue, Father/Mother of Life) and impersonal explanatory voice.
- ¶12: Core reiteration of relative durations and cosmic endpoints (dissolution, Statue ascent) with no institutional or citation intrusion.
- ¶13: Formal closure of the teaching sequence; still internal to the structural cosmology (summary of the three times).

## Chapter 18
### [Concerning the Five] War[s that the] Sons of [Li]ght waged with the Sons] of Darkness.

*This chapter is largely a core cosmological sequence: five ordered "wars" describing major salvific operations (First Living Man; Living Spirit; Third Ambassador; Jesus the glorious one; Counsel of Life). There is a thin hagiographic dialogue attribution at the opening (¶1), a clear Christian overlay via a Gospel-style citation formula (¶2), and an editorial/pastoral beatitude applying the victory-pattern to the "elect" at the end (¶9), consistent with the chapter’s second-half drift.*

The sons of light waged five wars against the sons of darkness. The sons of light humiliated the sons of darkness in them all.

The first war is that of the First living Man, which he waged against the King of the realms of Darkness, and all the rulers that had come forth from the five worlds of [...]. He hunted them with his net, which is the living soul. He [...] in the snare and [...] the face of the [...] his virgin, the living fire [...] they did not find how to escape his net [...] he caught them like fish [...] the five counsels of the five elements [...]. He cut and dug out the root of the five trees, [...] root of the evil tree, like an axe.

The second war is that of the Living Spirit [...]. He has constructed the things and the worlds of light; he spread out the rulers and chained them in the middle [...] the stars in [...] the earths [...] them in the three vessels. For the rulers rebelled and were set against the Living Spirit. They wished to [...] since they knew and realised that he might bind and fetter them with a strong chain. Yet he, the Living Spirit, (appeared to them) with many aspects, with numerous [...], with many hands and great cruelties. He was revealed to them, he imprisoned them with much skill. Not one of them was able to save (himself), and he [...] [...]. Now, since he [...] he trapped them [...] [...].

The third war is that of the Third Ambassador who has displayed his image [...] what is below [...] scooped [...] all light and the property [...] the ones that are consumed [...] the entire ruling-power [...] pride of the light [...] extending it [...]. A person comes, who is powerful [...]; he takes his riches [...], silver [...], his pride and wantonness.

This is what the ruling-power is like [...], all riches [...] as they travel [...] light [...]. He cleansed and purified it.

The fourth war is this that Jesus the glorious one waged against all the rebels who had rebelled above and below. When the light was purified from them by the image of the Third Ambassador, their chains loosened [...]. The glorious one in the fourth war; he came and brought [...] a rebellion of the entire ruling-power. He girded himself [...] all of it another time. He constructed the whole structure and he made [...] a great contest; he humiliated therein. [...] he fulfilled the will of the greatness, [he gave the] hope to Adam [...] to his light ship.

The fifth war is this that the counsel of life [...] which is the summons and the obedience [...] great [...] and it placed [...] upon the [...] and the [...] [...] of death, which [...] with it. And it was victorious over [...] for ever, and it gave the kingdom [...].

[...] the good are victorious over the wicked [...] for ever and ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Teaching statement is impersonal and cosmological (Light vs. Darkness; fivefold structure), but introduced by a late dialogue attribution marker typical of the Kephalaia frame.
- ¶2: Core layer is systematic cosmological mechanism (First Living Man’s net = living soul; rulers from five worlds; five elements/counsels; tree-root imagery as internal correspondence). The final line shifts to explicit Gospel-style authority citation ("As the saviour has said"), marking later Christian overlay.
- ¶3: Impersonal, process-oriented cosmological exposition (construction of worlds; chaining rulers; three vessels). No institutional pointing or citation; reads as core teaching. Page marker and curly-brace artifact removed from extraction per rules.
- ¶4: Cosmic-agent operation (Third Ambassador; image-display; extraction of Light) with a short illustrative parable-like turn that remains explanatory, not institutional—typical of core correspondential pedagogy.
- ¶5: Continuation/conclusion of the illustrative comparison about the ruling-power and the purification of Light; remains within the cosmological mechanism.
- ¶6: Jesus appears as a defined cosmological actor in the Manichaean hierarchy (not a Gospel citation). The paragraph stays cosmos→cosmos (rebels; chains; structure; Adam’s enlightenment/hope), with no NT quotation formula.
- ¶7: Eschatological/cosmic completion act (Counsel of Life; Summons and Obedience) expressed in structural terms; page marker removed per rules.
- ¶8: Brief summarizing dualist maxim consistent with the closure of a five-war cosmological victory sequence; no institutional or citation markers.
- ¶9: Direct beatitude addressed to the community using institutional category "elect" and application language (blessedness for persons who imitate the five wars). This is cosmos→community pointing, characteristic of late pastoral/application grafting.

## Chapter 19

*Dominantly a systematic cosmological list of five "releases" (rescues/redemptions) tied to named cosmic agents (First Man, Living Spirit, Third Ambassador, Jesus the Splendour, Great Counsel/Summons-Obedience), with heavy lacunose transmission. The main late accretion is the final eschatological-institutional paragraph about "apostles," "commandment," and judgment (¶12), plus a brief exhortative beatitude (¶13). ¶1 contains a hagiographic speech-introduction framing the core list; ¶6 contains an institutional seam (“churches”) embedded within an otherwise cosmological description of Jesus the Splendour’s saving work.*

[There are] five [releases by] which the light is saved f[rom] all [the rulers and] powers of darkness!

[The first loo]sening and saving is that of [the] First Man, since when the First M[a]n was freed fro[m the prison of] the rulers by the Living Spirit, who had come [... ...] of the First Man, much light and [much] power was saved from the entire ruling-power. They ascended with the First Man to the aeons [of] greatn[ess]. This [is the first] release.

... ... th]ree wars [... ...] to the time when he [... ...] above [...] them upon the [... ...] universe.

[The third red]emption is that of the Thi[rd Ambassador ... he] displayed [his image ...] he [... ... the r]uling-power; he [... ... ... ...] from them [... ...] So this is [how] the [...] his image that [...] in the Th[ir]d Ambassador.

[Th]e fourth s[av]ing is that of Jesus the Splendour, since whe[n he was] re[ve]aled in the zone he displayed [his im]age in front of the firmaments and purified [the light] that is above. He took the likeness [...] he made himself like the angels in [...] un[til] he travelled and descended to the form of flesh. He set in order [t]he earths and all the fastenings. He also loosen[ed ...] light without measure in the entire structure. He gave [the] summons and the obedience to the elements, he formed [J]esus the Youth. He as[ce]n[d]ed and rested himself in the light [land].

The fifth release and saving is that of the great counsel, w[hich is the s]umm[ons an]d the obedience, since it also [...] the soul from the [land] of greatness.

[...] the Living Spirit [...] as he gave [...] the release [... ...]
The first [... ... he] ascended and he [... ...] as he [... ...] wisdom [... ...]
The fourth [... ...] its wisdom [...]
And also, the fifth [... ...] into it, and it sets it in order [...] its light image in this Last Statue.

These five grea[t] powe[rs ...] were revealed. They came to a profit [... ...] they were established upon [... ...] First Man [...] when he came to/ward [...] from the struggle. He set in order and constructed [...] universe [... ...] the First M[an ...] his advent.

In [t]his way [...] they st[an]d with [...]

... because of the light [th]at had rushed from [... ...] the Amba[s]sa[d]or.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula (“Once again… speaks”) wraps a compact cosmological thesis-statement; the teaching itself is impersonal/systematic (five-fold structure).
- ¶2: Systematic first item in a numbered five-fold cosmological sequence; cosmos→cosmos rescue-mechanics with named aeonic beings.
- ¶3: Continues the enumerated cosmological process (construction of worlds/earths/rulers; saving of light) without institutional address or citation.
- ¶4: Though highly lacunose, it reads as internal continuation of the preceding cosmological sequence (wars/universe), not as church discipline or citation.
- ¶5: Third item in the five-fold series; correspondential/cosmological language (“image,” “ruling-power”) remains within mythic mechanics.
- ¶6: Core mythic description of an aeonic agent (Jesus the Splendour) operating across zones/firmaments/elements is interrupted by an explicit institutional referent (“churches”), indicating a later application seam embedded in the paragraph.
- ¶7: Completes the five-fold list in cosmological terms (Great Counsel/Summons-Obedience; soul; land of greatness) without institutional regulation.
- ¶8: Internal recapitulation/structural linking of the releases (first/fourth/fifth) and the Last Statue within the cosmic system.
- ¶9: Summative cosmological sequencing (five powers revealed/established; struggle; construction of universe) stays cosmos→cosmos; application-score signal is not borne out by explicit exhortation here.
- ¶10: Lacunose connective closure likely concluding the cosmological arrangement; no institutional or citation markers visible.
- ¶11: Fragment still keyed to a core cosmological figure (“Ambassador”) and the movement of light (redemption mechanics).
- ¶12: Institutional/disciplinary and communal authority vocabulary (“commandment,” “apostles,” “last season,” judging souls) shifts from cosmological exposition to church-eschatological administration; consistent with late editorial/pastoral layer.
- ¶13: Beatitude-style exhortation (“Blessed is every person… understand”) is application to the hearer rather than cosmological mapping; functions as a closing admonition, not substrate teaching.

## Chapter 20
### The Chapter of the Name of the Fathers

*After a brief dialogue frame (¶1–2), the chapter gives a compact, systematic cosmological definition of why multiple divine beings can be called “father”: each is “father” relative to his own “greatness” (a corresponding realm/vehicle in which he is established). No pastoral/application turn and no NT citation overlay; “Jesus the Splendour” functions as a cosmological entity.*

Now, the Father, who is the first established thing, shall be called ‘the Father of Greatness’. His greatness is the great earth where he lives, he being established in it [...] the essence of the light that swathes all the rich gods and the angels and the dwellings [...] is set over it.

Furthermore, they shall call the Third Ambassador ‘father’. His greatness is the light ship of living fire wherein he lives, he being established in it.

Once again, Jesus the Splendour shall himself also be called ‘father’. His greatness is the ship of living waters where he lives, he being established in it.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic dialogue frame (“Once again the enlightener speaks to his disciples”) introducing the question.
- ¶2: Petition/Q&A apparatus (“We beseech you, our master…”) characteristic of the compiled Kephalaia frame, not the underlying teaching exposition.
- ¶3: Core correspondential/cosmological exposition: “Father” defined by ontological primacy (“first established thing”) and by his “greatness” as a realm (“great earth”) with other cosmic beings/dwellings ordered under an “essence of the light.”
- ¶4: Systematic extension of the same internal cosmos→cosmos mapping: title “father” + corresponding “greatness” as a specific cosmic vehicle/realm (“light ship of living fire”). No institutional pointing.
- ¶5: Continues the same structured teaching. “Jesus the Splendour” is used as a Manichaean cosmological being (not a Gospel citation), with his “greatness” defined as a cosmic ship/realm (“living waters”).

Only the opening dialogue-attribution is later frame.*

The fif[th] is his great glorio[us ... ] that [ ... , w]hich [has been ma]nifested all the tim[e], whi[ch contin]ues [ ... ] all of them. It was sculpted in them [ ... ] sealed in the [m ... ] sealed [ ... ] as they send [ ... ] in [ ... ... ] they too receive nourishment [ ... ] immediately [ ... his] living voi[ce ... ] joy [ ... ] set up anew. **Temporal observations:**

- ¶2: Systematic cosmological structure (five limbs/storehouses; twelve limbs/wisdoms) in impersonal expository voice; no institutional or citation vector.
- ¶3: Enumerative degree-structure language (five-fold repetition; internal cosmic imagery like springs of praise). Fits substrate mapping style.
- ¶4: First limb defined by function/emanation (light that enlightens; gushing/pouring into aeons). Cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶5: Second limb given as sensory correspondence (perfume) with cosmic pervasion across aeons; classic substrate register.
- ¶6: Third limb as "living voice" operating within aeons—impersonal mechanism, no address/polemic.
- ¶7: Though lacunose, it continues the five-limb enumeration with the same emanational mechanics (gushes forth), indicating the same core sequence.
- ¶8: Continuation of the fifth-limb description using internal-cosmic processes (manifestation, sealing, nourishment, living voice, joy, renewal) without shifting to institutional application or NT citation; remains within the same substrate enumeration.

Should they be poured [...] the body of the four mountains and the three vessels that surround the universe; if they shall all be poured out [...] these fine granules.

Happen you know that the grains of the earth particles are measurable, [and those of] the [entire] universe. It is possible to count the particles of the earth each year [...] the sand grains of the entire universe; but the duration of the time that the Great Spirit spent in the Father, [the] first established thing, one will not be able to make a count of it!

He first sculpted her like this. He established her in his inner storehouses in quiet and silence. When [they had] need of her she was called and came forth of the Father [of Greatness]. She looked at all her aeons of light!

Once again, from the time when she was called to [...] go to [...] she gave [...] upon his [bod]y [...] all the aeons [...]; she also [...] all of them. This [is also the case] with the First Man.

This is how the Man spent a long time being anointed in the Mother, set fast in her inner storehouses [...]; from when the First Man went down to the contest, till the time when the [Statue (?) co]mes i[n ...] this time [...] he appeared [...]

Again, from when he was called and came forth, till the time when he [himse]lf called the five shining [gods] who are his sons; they themselves also spent a long time existing in their father! It is the time that occurred from the coming down of the First Man till the going up of the Last Statue. The five gods spent this much duration being anointed in the First Man. Yet, [at the] time when they were awaited, they were called and came forth.

At the time when the Mother of Life was called from the Father of Greatness, when she expelled herself and came down by her own wish, from the heights of the Father to the earth, [she would spend thousands of] years and many ten thousands until she reached the earth. [Fo]r there is no measure nor rule to the heights of the Father. Rather, [just as] he had called her in his Great Spirit, so he set her upon [the] land of greatness through one of these three that are counted to the twelve hidden ones within the light veil, the ones [esta]blished after the [...] region of the south.

[The] matter of the Mother [of L]ife is like this. When [the Father called her he established her in the place he pleased,] to establish her there [...]; and she examined everywhere that [...] a blink of an eye, or like a [...] bird [...]

This also is lik[e ... the] Mother of Life in [...] whenever [...] cast her out [...] upon her to the place [...] him. He perfected her in his Great Spirit and set her in [the place he pl]eased, according to [his will].

[Th]is [also is how] they called the [five g]ods from the [Fi]rst Man. They were set before him, as he wished that [...]-ness [...] down, that they would rule in them [...] one another. He ordered and distributed them [...] the son. He stripped himself of them [...] and he [...] he girt and bound himself to [...] in a [...] of silence upon the border of the earth [...]

Again, he too, the First Man, spent a long period and [time] until he gave power to his sons; until he assu[m]ed them. After he came to wear them he stood fast in the heights between the borders. Again, he spent a duration and time like this, corresponding to such amount and measure as the raindrops and dew and falls of mist that come down in the universe. This is how he spent this duration also, standing between the borders, until the hour when he cast himself down to the contest!

Whenever the Father of Greatn[e]ss called the Great Spirit and the First Man and the gods of the glorious one, who are provided to enable them to reveal him; at that very time [he] sculpted the Great Builder in the [land of] light, [and] the Living Spirit with the mighty and active gods who came [from] him. Also, at that time, he sculpted the [Amb]assador, with Jesus the Splendour and the Virgin of Light and the Pillar of Glory, and the gods who came from them. [All these the Father of Greatness sculpted at] a single time. They came forth of one another, one after one; the time when [...] and the work.

[...] to recount it to you [...] from the time when the Darkness raised itself up to [...] essence of the light [...] these three emanations [...] from the will of the Father against the Enemy [...] he has [...] Darkness [...] according to the will [...] the universe [...] in it [...] separate out [...] rest him in which way, and he rejoices [...] in his kingdom; until [the time when the face] of the Father will be unveiled over [all] the gods of glory, and all the rich ones of [...] the em[anations] who came forth against the Enemy.

[From] that time when he sculpted these three [...] till the time when the Father would unveil his face, [and they] go in to his hidden storehouses, and he calls them [...] establishes each one of them to his place. There is no measure nor rule to this period until that time.

... about this Matter, this thought of death [...]; for [this] is the way that [...] came up to its storehouses. It has [...] in its five elements.

Again, it also [...] the period and the time that it spent, until it revealed itself to its elements, before it came to the trees. It spent a great period and a long time established [...] before it came up to the trees. It was [...] in the trees. It spent a long time and duration [...] existing in them, before it sculpted the fru[its ...] and sculpted the fruits.

Again, it spent a long [time] in the fruits. Not little is the period and the time that it spent existing in the fruits, before it sculpted [...] after a long time [...] the d[emon]s of its five worlds in various forms [can not (?) ] be measured [...] in p[erfec]tion of the fruits in the demons and fiends. They fell upon their earth and [...] were nourished. Not little is the period that they [spent in the wo]rlds, until they were nourished amongst [...] to make [...] that land which shines, and the great [King] who dwells there.

[You] know that [...] that in [...] at the time when he [...] its essence. It assumed [...] Matter [...] to the war [...] the Perfect Man. [From the time] when the war occurred [...]

[The first is ...] he came to be in some [...] when they ensnared a [...]

[The second is ...] that he built [...] the universe was established. The zone [...] down until the time when it will be dissolved.

The third is the [...] the things and the universe will [...] from the time when they were established there, till the time when their end will be complete and their existence ceases [...]

[The fourth is the dissolution when they will dissolve the universe] there, and all things are destroyed. Obliteration reaches them in that great fire, [which will] burn them for fourteen hundred and sixty-eight years.

[The fift]h is the way that the Last Statue will be sculpted [from] the remnant of all things. The light separates out to its place, and goes up and rules in its kingdom. However, [the Da]rkness [...] and they take it in to the tomb [...] and they are bound with it in an eternal chain.

Now, from [the tim]e when this first war occurred, till the time of this chain [...] these five things [...] light.

[The six]th: From the time when it stirred and raised itself up in its land, till the time of its chaining,

[From the time] when it will be taken in to the chaining and bound in the lump [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Fragmentary but in the impersonal cosmological register (“revealed in the Father… first established thing”), with no dialogue attribution or institutional application.
- ¶2: Emanation/abiding-in-the-Father duration language; internal cosmic beings only.
- ¶3: Core “measurement” analogy using universe-architecture (four mountains/three vessels) as the correspondential scale.
- ¶4: Contrast between measurable cosmos and unmeasurable divine duration; still cosmos→cosmos (no institutional pointing).
- ¶5: Systematic emanation mechanics (sculpted/established/called/came forth) and storehouse imagery typical of the substrate.
- ¶6: Continuing emanation/process description; no frame attribution.
- ¶7: Core time-span mapping (descent→contest→Last Statue) is present, but a first-person recounting seam suggests later pedagogical voice.
- ¶8: Numbered/systemic duration spans anchored in cosmic events (First Man/Last Statue) and emanation order.
- ¶9: Strong core cosmological geography/numbering (twelve hidden ones; light veil; south region), but prefaced by later didactic address.
- ¶10: Underlying core contrasts of divine placement/speed (“blink of an eye”) remain, but the explicit ‘instruction of the people’ points outward from cosmos to audience.
- ¶11: Return to impersonal emanation mechanics (perfected/set according to will) within divine economy.
- ¶12: Systematic ordering/distribution of the five gods; border/earth imagery stays within cosmic architecture.
- ¶13: Classic correspondential “measure” similes (raindrops/dew/mist) used to quantify cosmic duration; impersonal expository voice.
- ¶14: Systematic cosmological list of emanations/functions (not NT citation); “Jesus the Splendour” here functions as a named cosmic being within the hierarchy.
- ¶15: Core dualist process-history (Darkness’ uprising; three emanations; final unveiling) expressed as cosmic mechanics, not moral exhortation.
- ¶16: Impersonal duration/measurelessness and storehouse-placement formulae typical of core cosmology.
- ¶17: Shifts from cosmic description to institutional authority talk (“teachers… saviours… apostles of greatness”) about what can be proclaimed.
- ¶18: Continuation of proclamation-limits framed around “apostles and teachers,” a later editorial/theological reflection rather than substrate mechanics.
- ¶19: Fragmentary Q-style setup (“If a person shall ask…”) functioning as a rhetorical frame rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶20: Core dark-cosmology (Matter; storehouses; five elements) embedded in a didactic answer-frame.
- ¶21: Parallel evil-emanation process rendered through staged correspondences (elements→trees→fruits) within the system.
- ¶22: Internal dark-world generation (demons/five worlds) and duration-logic; no institutional pointing.
- ¶23: Cosmic combat schema (Matter vs Perfect Man) in mythic-process mode; fragmentary but still internal.
- ¶24: Begins a numbered/ordered list (even though lacunose), consistent with systematic teaching structures.
- ¶25: Cosmic construction and temporal span of the established universe; list-structure continues.
- ¶26: Further list item defining a cosmic time-range to “end,” still internal to system.
- ¶27: Eschatological chronology with quantified duration; still cosmological mechanics rather than institutional exhortation.
- ¶28: Core end-time separation and chaining of Darkness; purely cosmic resolution.
- ¶29: Summative time-bracketing (war→chain) tying the numbered items together; no external application.
- ¶30: Core time-span marker preserved; later editor adds a reflection on who may ‘speak and recount’ (apostles/chosen/teachers).
- ¶31: Only a minimal cosmological clause survives; the remainder appears to belong to the later ‘who can speak’ frame-thread.
- ¶32: Explicit ‘apostle and teacher’ capability statement; late frame reflection, not substrate teaching.

## Chapter 25
### [Concerning the Advent of Five Fathers from the Five Limbs of the Father]

*A very short chapter with a clear hagiographic/dialogue frame opening, followed by a compact core correspondential teaching: a fivefold emanation/advent sequence mapping five cosmic figures to five internal faculties/limbs of the Father (mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration). No NT citation formulae; “Jesus the Beloved” functions here as a named cosmological being within the system, not as a Gospel overlay.*

The [Ambas]sador [came] from his mind; the Beloved of the Lights [from his thought]; the Mother of Life from his insight; [Jesus] the Beloved from his counsel; the Virgin of Lig[ht f]rom his consideration.

So in this way were summone[d these five] fathers. These five advents came from the five limbs [of the Father]. One came from another and was manifested in its season.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic editorial opening (“Once again the apostle speaks… He speaks thus:”), marking the dialogue/hagiographic frame; “apostle” is a compilation-layer attribution rather than substrate teaching.
- ¶2: Systematic fivefold correspondential mapping wholly within the cosmic architecture (emanated beings ↔ internal faculties/limbs of the Father). No institutional application or citation; impersonal structural voice.
- ¶3: Continues the same structural exposition: fivefold advents from five limbs, sequential manifestation “in its season” (process description, not exhortation or polemic).

## Chapter 26
### Concerning the First Man and the Ambassador and the Ship; what they resemble.

*A chain of five similes mapping major cosmic redeemers (First Man, Ship of the Day/Great Spirit, Living Spirit, Ambassador) onto human analogies is the dominant layer. The only clear later addition is the closing exhortation to disciples in direct second-person application voice. The opening paragraph contains a brief hagiographic dialogue-attribution that must be stripped to recover the older teaching unit.*

The First [Man] is like a great distinguished man whose treasure has been taken away from him. He came to pursue his treasure. This is how the [First] Man too came after his sons from the heights, so that he might redeem them and retrieve his treasure, which had been scattered amongst his enemies.

Once again, [he is like a man] whose two sons were taken away from him by [ ... He] came out toward them to redeem them. Again, this is how the First Man [ ... ] shines in the heights because of his two sons, who were separated from him. They are his living soul that exist in two powers, which makes four.

Once again, the ship [of the day] is like a great spear [ ... ... The Great Spirit too, which is in it, is like a wise [crafts]man who might beautify and set aright [all] the armour for war.

Once again, the Living Spirit is like a combatant who might go towards a king's son who has been taken prisoner of war, and he redeems him from the hands of his enemies.

[The Amba]ssador too is like a great man [ ... ] because of the treasure [ ... ] hand of his prisoners [ ... ] these light powers [ ... ... the] sons and their soul, so that they would [ ... ... (the soul) might] be brought forth in the land wherein it was imprisoned.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The simile itself stays within the cosmic system (First Man, sons/treasure, enemies, redemption) in impersonal explanatory mode; only the prefixed speech formula marks later framing.
- ¶2: Systematic cosmological accounting (sons/living soul/powers, numerical relation) presented as a simile without institutional targeting or exhortation; no citation or pastoral address.
- ¶3: Cosmic entity-to-object correspondence (Ship of the Day/Great Spirit ↔ spear/craftsman; beautifying/ordering) remains internal to the mythic-cosmological system.
- ¶4: Pure redemption simile (Living Spirit’s function) with no shift into contemporary institutions or audience instruction; characteristic core explanatory voice.
- ¶5: Despite lacunae, the sentence structure indicates another cosmological redemption simile (Ambassador, light powers, soul’s release) with no frame formulas or pastoral application.
- ¶6: Direct second-person exhortation (“You yourselves must…”) and goal-oriented moral application (be purifiers/redeemers, be counted worthy) shifts from describing cosmic mechanisms to instructing an audience—typical later pastoral/application layer.

## Chapter 27
### Concerning the Five Forms that exist in the Rulers of Darkness.

*The chapter is dominated by systematic cosmological-correspondential description of the Ruler of Darkness (five body-forms sourced from the five dark worlds, plus additional triads of qualities). It opens with a dialogue/"apostle" frame (later compilation layer) and ends with a clear exhortatory application to the audience (pastoral/application drift), consistent with editorial fatigue in the second half.*

Consider the ruler, the leader of all the powers of darkness. Now, five forms exist in his body, corresponding to (the archetype) of the five creations that exist in the five worlds of darkness.

Indeed, his head is lion-faced, which came about from the world of fire. His wings and his shoulders are eagle-faced, after the likeness of the children of the wind. His hands and his feet are daemons, after the likeness of the children of the world of smoke. His belly is dragon-faced, after the likeness of the world of darkness. His tail is the form of the fish, which belongs to the world of the children of water. These five shapes are in him, and they came from the five creations of the five worlds of darkness.

Should he wish, he shall walk on his two feet [ ... ] world of smoke. Also, when he wants, he shall [ ... ] four [ ... ] on his hands and feet [ ... ] like the children of fire. Should he wish, he shall rise up on his wings, like the children of the wind. Also, should he wish, he shall plunge down to the waters like the children of water. Again, should he wish, he shall crawl on his belly like the children of darkness. These five shapes are in him.

Also, there are three other (qualities) in him.

The first: [ ... ] his powers [ ... ]

The second: Shall a [ ... ] his magic arts. When he wishes, he shall make an invocation over himself, and hide from his powers. When he wants, he shall show himself to them and strike a death-blow by his magic arts. Any word he might utter at that moment, he shall [wreak magic] by it.

The third: His body is strong, a[1]] [ ... ] nails and claws of his powers are not able to penetrate it. No iron nor copper body will equal him [ ... ]; they will not be able to destroy him, because he was formed and fashioned from the hard-hearted counsel of Matter, the mother of demons and fiends.

Again, there are three others in him.

When he wants, his fire shall blaze, and his entire body be made like the [ ... of] fire.

Also, when he wishes, he shall send out frost, and his entire body freezes like a [ ... of] snow.

The third: Whenever his powers are stood before him, he shall look at them and understand; for [whatever ex]ists in their heart he shall understand, what is in their heart and their face so long as they are stood before him. When they retreat before him and distance themselves from him, he shall not understand what is in their heart. There is no life in him whatsoever, but his 'life' is the bile of anger that is apparent on his face and in his fear. Shall a [ ... ] destruction that is establ[ished be]fore him.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core teaching begins with impersonal system-description (five forms ↔ five creations ↔ five worlds of darkness), but it is introduced by a later Kephalaia dialogue frame using 'apostle' and a staged setting.
- ¶2: Classic substrate mapping: body-part/shape correspondence anchored entirely within the five dark worlds and their 'children' (cosmos→cosmos; no contemporary application).
- ¶3: Impersonal functional exposition (shape-shifting capacities) continuing the five-form system; lacunae do not obscure that this remains internal cosmology.
- ¶4: Enumerative structural marker introducing a triad; typical systematic teaching connective without exhortation/citation.
- ¶5: Fragmentary, but it preserves the numbered, schematic teaching format (triadic qualities) characteristic of the core layer.
- ¶6: Describes an internal faculty/operation of the dark ruler (magic arts as mechanism), not a polemic about contemporary practitioners; remains cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶7: Mechanistic description of composition/indestructibility (metal comparison) and origin from personified Matter; fully within the mythic-cosmological system.
- ¶8: Second triad-introduction in the same schematic voice; indicates structured teaching units.
- ¶9: Cosmic-physical transformation property stated impersonally; correspondence stays within elemental/cosmic register.
- ¶10: Continues the internal, descriptive catalogue of powers (heat/cold); no audience address or institutional reference.
- ¶11: Cognitive/affective mechanics of the ruler (limited perception; 'life' as bile of anger) in impersonal exposition; still system-internal.
- ¶12: Direct exhortation and audience address ('my beloved ones'; imperatives 'do not lay up... Hold yourselves secure') applies the cosmology to behavior—late application layer, matching the chapter’s second-half drift.
- ¶13: Continues exhortation ('do you become zealous and perfect...') aimed at the hearers; soteriological promise ('inherit life') rather than system-description, indicating editorial/pastoral application rather than the impersonal teaching substrate.

## Chapter 28
### [Concerning the T]welve Judges [of] the Father

*This chapter is largely a systematic cosmological list of the Twelve Judges (a numbered emanational schema) with brief narrative functions for each—typical of the older correspondential-teaching layer. A late drift appears at the end: an explicit hortatory application to the audience (¶16), and an institutional intrusion (“holy church”) within the otherwise cosmic description of the Light Mind (¶13).*

Twelve judges exist; the great mighty givers of judgement! They were sent and came from the exalted greatness to this place. They were assigned to different works according to the command that the Judge, who belongs to the greatness, entrusted to all these judges. Now he, the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges, who exists and is established in the aeons of greatness; he is the Father of Greatness.

The first judge is the First Man, he who humiliated the ruler of Darkness in the beginning. He judged him according to a judgement of righteousness; for he had exalted himself against a kingdom that was not his own.

The second judge is the Living Spirit, he who came and bore up the Man. He too made a judgement on all the powers of Darkness; which had wounded the garments of the First Man.

The third judge is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, she who beautified and set up the heavenly part of the universe, according to her pleasure; which [...] her. She judged the rulers above; she fettered and set them firmly in the place that is fitting for them.

The fourth judge is the Great Builder, he who built the new aeon of joy. He judged, according to a judgement of righteousness, the storehouses of the Enemy; that death may not well up from this time on. He has constructed a prison for the Enemy. Also, upon the crown of the building he has constructed a throne for the First Man and all the fathers of light; they who engaged the struggle with the Evil One and were victorious over him.

The fifth judge is the great King of Honour, he who dwells and is established in the seventh firmament. He is the judge of all the firmaments who gives a true judgement, according to a judgement of righteousness, upon all the powers and all the kingdoms of the firmaments.

The sixth judge is [the Th]ird Ambassador, he who came and displayed his image. He purified the light [...] which his own [...] also of the enemy, he has [...]

The seventh judge is ... ... the worlds [... as he gives the victory to] they who are steadfast; but he condemns the ones who are convicted ... ... he shall become for them the steps of the way (?) [...]; but the convicted he sweeps and casts out to the depths.

The eighth judge is Jesus the Splendour; because after the Ambassador had displayed his image and the light was taken [...] a mass of rulers rose up from above and below. Then they sent Jesus the Splendour. He came ... ... he descended and made a judgement in the firmaments. He separated the steadfast from the convicted.

The ninth judge is the Virgin of Light, [she who took the heart] of the powers by her image, gathering her own ones into her. She makes a judgement on the ruler of the moist, and the ruler of the dry.

The tenth judge is the Judge who dwells in the atmosphere, judging all mankind. He makes a separation between they who are good and they who are evil, setting apart the righteous from the sinners.

The eleventh judge is the Light Mind, he who shall come and appear in the world. And he unveils [...] and separates light from Darkness, and sets the truth apart from lawlessness.

The twelfth judge is the great counsel, which is the summons and the obedience, which is appointed in the elements. It is the one that makes a separation between the good and the evil ones. At the end also it can gather itself together and sculpt its own self in the Last Statue. And it separates light from darkness.

These are the twelve great and mighty judges who were sent. They came from the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges. He is the Father that is hidden and immeasurable, the one who exists; who is established in the aeons of greatness upon the great throne of honour, which is the King of Honour [...] in his kingdom.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogic frame (“Once again… disciples”), characteristic of the editorial/hagiographic wrapper.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological exposition establishing a numbered structure (twelve) and the transcendent Judge (Father of Greatness); no citation, no institutional application.
- ¶3: Cosmic-historical function of a named emanation (First Man) within the Light–Darkness conflict; internal-to-cosmos mapping.
- ¶4: Systematic continuation of the numbered list; cosmological agents and actions, no audience address.
- ¶5: Cosmic construction/ordering language (setting up the universe; fettering rulers) typical of the older teaching substrate.
- ¶6: Cosmological mechanics (new aeon, prison, throne) and emanational actors; teleological clause remains within cosmic process (not moral exhortation).
- ¶7: Degree-structured cosmic geography (seventh firmament) and systematic judging function; no institutional referents.
- ¶8: Named cosmological being (Third Ambassador) with image-display/purification function; lacunae do not obscure its systematic placement in the list.
- ¶9: Despite heavy lacunae, the voice remains functional/cosmological (victory/condemnation; casting to depths) rather than direct exhortation to the audience.
- ¶10: Core is the cosmological dispatch and judging action of Jesus the Splendour in the firmaments. The phrase about “apostles” signals a later community’s institutional vocabulary grafted onto a cosmic name.
- ¶11: Correspondential-cosmological language (“image,” rulers of moist/dry) and systematic list continuation; entirely within the cosmic system.
- ¶12: Eschatological separation is stated as the function of a cosmic Judge “in the atmosphere.” Moral terms appear, but they operate as categories in a cosmic sorting mechanism rather than exhortation to the audience.
- ¶13: Light Mind as an eschatological cosmic agent fits the numbered emanational schema; “holy church” is an institutional referent that crosses the boundary from BEING (cosmos) to POINTING AT (community structure).
- ¶14: Abstract-but-systematic cosmic hypostasis (“great counsel” appointed in the elements) with eschatological transformation (“Last Statue”); internal cosmological mechanics.
- ¶15: Formal recapitulation closing the cosmic list by returning to the transcendent Father; no exhortation/citation.
- ¶16: Direct audience address and imperatives (“my beloved ones… you yourselves may walk… Judge… May a brother…”) with practical soteriological application—classic late pastoral/application layer, consistent with the chapter’s second-half drift.

## Chapter 29
### Concerning the Eighteen Great Thrones of all the Fathers

*The chapter is largely a systematic, numbered cosmological list (18 thrones: 9 in the outer aeons, 9 in the zone) — this is strongly CORE in voice and structure. It is wrapped by a hagiographic/frame opening (¶1) and concluding doxological/summarizing material (¶11, ¶18), and it culminates in an explicitly institutional-hagiographic identification of the ninth throne in the zone with the present apostle in the holy church (¶17), consistent with the chapter-level editorial-fatigue drift.*

Eighteen [great thrones for all the fathers] exist and are appointed, which are established in eighteen places! While there are nine among them [appointed in] the outer aeons, they exist [i]n all the worlds of the damage.

The first throne is the throne of the Father, the God of [trut]h, the King of the aeons of greatness, the one who exists; who is established [...] he alone, in his own essen[c]e, [...] apart. This is the first throne [...] the one that surpasses all the thrones.

The second throne is that of the [gl]orious Mother of Life. She is the origin of all the emanations that have come to this world.

The third throne is constructed for the father, the First Man. He is the one who humiliated the entire first Enmity, which existed in the worlds of Darkness.

The fourth throne is that of the Beloved of the Lights, the great honoured Beloved of the aeons of greatness.

The fifth throne is that of the glorious Great Builder; the great architect who built the new aeon for a newness, for [a ...] for [the] fathers of light; also for a place of binding and a prison for the Enemy and his powers.

[The sixth thro]n[e] is that of the Living Spirit, the [glorious] mighty one, he who bore up the First Man out from the [land of Darkness]; who also set in order and constructed the world [s ...] both for a cleansing of the light, [and] for a binding [of the] powers of the Enmity.

The seventh th[rone is] that of the Third Ambassador, the king of the zone, also the master of all advices.

The eighth throne is that of Jesus the Splendour, who [is] the releaser and redeemer [of] all souls.

The ninth throne is the throne of the great M[ind, he to whom] … and all the [life] that will be purified from the world returns to him.

In contrast, these are the other nine thrones that are appointed in this world of the zone.

Three thrones in the ship of [the d]ay: one is that of the Ambassador, the second i[s that of] the Great Spirit, the third is that of the Living Spirit.

Once again, there are another three in the ship that [belongs to the night]: the first is the throne of Jesus the Splendour, [the second is that of] the First Man, the third is tha[t of the Virgin of] Light. These six thrones are established in the two ships.

Also, the seventh throne is established in the seventh firmament. The great King of Honour sits upon it.

The eighth throne is established in the atmosphere. The Judge of truth sits upon it, he who judges all mankind. Three paths shall be distinguished before him: one to death, one to life, on[e] to the mingling.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with a formulaic dialogue attribution (FRAME), then immediately shifts into impersonal, numbered cosmological architecture (18 thrones; 9/9 division), which is characteristic of the older systematic teaching.
- ¶2: Systematic enumeration and ontological description of a cosmic principle (Father/aeons), without audience address or institutional pointing.
- ¶3: Numbered throne list; emanational cosmology in impersonal expository voice. (Page marker removed.)
- ¶4: Core Manichaean/Iranian cosmological actors (First Man) in a functional, mythic-cosmic register; no ecclesial application.
- ¶5: Continues the numbered cosmological list; ‘Beloved of the Lights’ is treated as a cosmic hypostasis within the aeons.
- ¶6: Process/function description (building the new aeon; prison/binding for the Enemy) stays entirely within cosmic mechanics.
- ¶7: Strongly core: standard Manichaean cosmological role-description (rescue; ordering; construction; cleansing/binding) in impersonal explanatory voice.
- ¶8: Numbered throne list with cosmic titles/functions (‘king of the zone’) — internal to the system.
- ¶9: Although flagged ‘christian’ by vocabulary, ‘Jesus the Splendour’ here functions as a defined cosmological being (releaser/redeemer) within the Manichaean hierarchy, not as a Gospel citation.
- ¶10: Core throne enumeration is present, but the phrase ‘all the churches shall gather’ shifts the mapping toward institutional referents; likely an editorial application grafted onto a cosmic identification.
- ¶11: Summative/doxological closure (‘for ever and ever, amen’) reads as editorial framing rather than the bare teaching list.
- ¶12: Structural transition within the same enumerative teaching (9/9 division); remains inside cosmological geography (‘world of the zone’).
- ¶13: Systematic triadic placement (ships/day) — classic correspondential-architectural listing.
- ¶14: Iteration formula (‘Once again’) continues the internal cosmological mapping (night-ship; named hypostases). (Page marker removed.)
- ¶15: Firmament-location mapping remains cosmological; no institutional application.
- ¶16: Eschatological mechanics (judge + three paths) presented as cosmic process/structure, not moral exhortation or church rule.
- ¶17: Explicit institutional and time-anchored application (‘apostle in the holy church’, ‘has come to you at this time’) is hagiographic/editorial self-identification, not substrate cosmology.
- ¶18: Fragmentary concluding summary with honor-language reads like an editorial wrap-up to the list rather than a needed element of the cosmological enumeration.

## Chapter 30
### Concerning the Three Garments

*Short chapter dominated by cosmological-mechanical teaching about the Living Spirit’s three ‘garments’ (wind, fire, water) and their deployment across the zone. Only the first paragraph contains a clear dialogue/frame attribution and direct address to disciples, which appears secondary to the underlying teaching narrative.*

that when the Living Spirit came he brought the three garments; that of wind, that of fire, and that of water. He assumed them from [ ... ] he has [ ... ] in them in those three garments [ ... ] he established [them] for all the things of [this univers]e, both above and below. He arranged them in the [ ... ] he was displayed. Afterwards [ ... ] his works [ ... ] the [ ... h]e stripped them off before [the ri]ghteous ones.

Now, when he was about to loose [n them, ... he] divested himself of them from their (clasp) and he [ ... i]n them, and thus these three g[arme]nts are arranged [ ... ] from below the edges of the zone as they clothe i[t al]l [ ... ] up [ ... ] above [ ... ] up [ ... ] above them strips them of the zone from that place at its topmost point; and it is placed below them and ra[ised u]p to the heights above, from which it had been sent o[u]t at their beginning.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The paragraph opens with a formulaic frame (“Once again…to his disciples”) and direct address (“my beloved ones”), but the remaining content is internal cosmological narration about what the Living Spirit did with wind/fire/water as cosmic ‘garments’—system description rather than institutional application.
- ¶2: Impersonal, mechanical cosmography: the garments are ‘arranged’ and stretched over the zone from its edges to the topmost point. No citation formulas, exhortation, or institutional identifications; mapping remains cosmos→cosmos.

## Chapter 31
### Concerning the Summons, in which Limb of the Soul it descended to the First Man.

*The chapter is dominated by systematic cosmological teaching about the First Man as head of the living soul’s limbs, with internal correspondential analogies (head/limbs, air/breath, tower/Pillar of Glory) and a fivefold noetic set (mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration). The only clear late layer is the opening dialogue-question frame with an appeal to “scriptures.” One paragraph (¶7) is more mythic-narrative in tone but still stays cosmos→cosmos (no institutional application), so it is retained as core with mild uncertainty noted.*

Indeed, the First Man dispersed himself and he [...] limb of the living soul as I have written for you [...] of the living soul; he has [...] he did not know [... ...] but the blessed, glorious Man came [in] secret; in his image; in his shape; in his love; in his holy virgin, she who is the virgin of light, the soul of the father.

“After the fashion of this fleshly body: as the root of all the limbs hang upon the head, so that should one of the person's limbs be cut off, while the head exists he has hope for [...] but if his head should be cut off, the entire body will die and he is lost.

“This is also the case for the Firs[t] Ma[n]. He is the head, while his sons attach to it the limbs [of] his soul. Or, conversely, like the living air, on which all flesh entirely lives as it breathes and [...] therein. His head is placed on the body of the Pillar of Glory in the heights of the universe.

“[This i]s also the case [for] the father, the blessed Man; as he is like the head that is upon the body and the air that upon the Pillar is set i[n ...] being made strong by his light virgin, [she] who is his soul that he clothes [...]

“[ ... ] t[h]ose [...]: the mind, thought, insight, counsel and c[on]sideration that he produced and sent forth from hi[m to] do his will. He sprang and travelled behind them. [...] of his living soul, which is entwined among the rebels; as they are like the limbs of its body, and [...] universe.

“And when they were sent, at [that ti]m[e] he was found with the Virgin of [Lig]ht and [he] stood up, asking and entreating for a p[o]wer. [ ... he] gave him peace and a ki[ss ...] he [gave] him goo[d] tidings [...] Man. The Man him[s]elf gave [his ...] his limbs and gathered his soul i[n ...] he built it in its place like this tower [...]

“[...] shaped it and beautified it skillfully [...] for[m ...] the voices that he sent [...] he might sink in and quench [... ...] so[u]l that was crushed by the enemy. They were gathered in. They came, set firm once more, in the image of their father.”

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue frame (“Once again… questioned the enlightener… He says to him”) plus an appeal to written authority (“it is written in the scriptures”), marking editorial compilation rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Core cosmological identity-language (First Man; living soul; Virgin of Light) presented as what the Man IS/does within the cosmic system, but introduced by a dialogue attribution typical of the later Kephalaia frame.
- ¶3: Impersonal correspondential analogy (body→cosmic structure) with no citation, exhortation, or institutional targeting; mapping remains internal to the system.
- ¶4: Systematic cosmos→cosmos mapping (First Man/head; sons/limbs; Pillar of Glory as cosmic body) in explanatory voice, characteristic of the substrate.
- ¶5: Continues the structural correspondential mapping (head/body/air/Pillar; Virgin as soul) without institutional application; despite lacunae, the register is cosmological and systematic.
- ¶6: Classic substrate marker: a numbered/clustered noetic set (mind/thought/insight/counsel/consideration) treated as hypostatized powers sent forth, then re-anchored to the living soul’s cosmic scattering—cosmos→cosmos throughout.
- ¶7: Mythic-narrative presentation (request for power; peace/kiss; gathering/building) but still entirely within the cosmological dramatis personae (Virgin of Light; Man; limbs/soul; tower/Pillar motif). Slight uncertainty due to heightened narrative/‘good tidings’ diction, but no NT citation formula or institutional targeting.
- ¶8: Process-description of restoration/gathering of soul via sent ‘voices’ (Summons/Answer complex implied) with no audience-address or church regulation; despite a minor pastoral-register signal, the mapping remains cosmic and functional.
