## Chapter 45
### Concerning the Vessels

*This chapter is largely a single impersonal cosmological sequence: fore-prepared containment-places are constructed by the Powers of Light for specific Dark vessels/beings before they descend or arise, binding them until the end/dissolution of the universe. No NT citation, hagiographic dialogue frame, or church-institutional application appears. One brief didactic imperative at the start (¶1) reads like a later instructional bridge but does not alter the underlying substrate teaching.*

Before these vessels were discharged and swept down from heaven, first three ditches were fashioned and constructed for them, in which they would be bound and fettered.

Before the sea giant was discharged from the sphere, the Father of Life constructed a place for [him i]n this great sea that is within the walls, whereon the ships sail, and where the fullness shall [ ... ... ]

Also, before the [ ... ] was displayed to the worlds [ ... ] the earth [ ... ... f]ell to the sea and its thought shaped it, the Father of Life constructed a place for it in [the ... ... ] below the four mountains, above the mountains of the heavens above [ ... ] that [ ... ] a great plain it digs in Matter in the midst of the worlds. When the Adamant of Light was sent to it, he hastened after it to that place, smote it and threw it between mountains in the place made ready for it [ ... ] is set fast upon it till the end of the universe.

Before the waste and dirt of the Darkness were swept out from creation, gehennas were constructed in which the waste might be covered till the dissolution of the universe.

Before the rebels rose up, they whose corps rebelled in the ruling-power above and below, they [ ... ] outside, and also the tyrants in whose heart it lay [to ty]rannise amongst the murk and the clouds and winds and spirits and storms; for them were constructed in the great outer sea seven hostels, places whence they are thrown into the depths and the Darkness [ ... ]

Also, before the abortions [fell ... ...] to the earth, there was set apart [ ... ]: they are gathered in and dwell therein.

Again, before the watchers rebelled and came down from heaven, a prison was fashioned and constructed for them in the depths of the earth, below the mountains.

Before the children of the giants were born, they who had [no] knowledge of righteousness in them nor divinity, thirty-six cities were assigned and constructed for them wherein the children of [the giants would] live; they who would come to beget from each other, they who shall spend ten hundred years alive.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core-like ‘before X, Y was constructed’ containment schema; but the opening second-person imperative sounds like a pedagogical/editorial lead-in rather than the older impersonal teaching voice.
- ¶2: Purely intra-cosmic mapping: a named cosmic being (sea giant) is assigned a prepared cosmic location by the Father of Life; no institutional or exhortative pivot.
- ¶3: System-internal cosmography (four mountains; heavens; Matter; prepared place) plus functional action of a named cosmic agent (Adamant of Light) subduing and fixing a monster ‘till the end of the universe’—classic substrate process narration, not moral application.
- ¶4: Cosmic-waste mechanics and eschatological containment (‘till the dissolution of the universe’) remain entirely within the cosmological system; ‘gehennas’ functions here as a cosmic receptacle, not a moral exhortation.
- ¶5: Degree/containment logic continues (‘before… for them were constructed… seven hostels’), with the ‘rebels/tyrants’ located among cosmic media (clouds, winds, spirits, storms) rather than any contemporary institutions—substrate-style correspondence within cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶6: Brief but structurally consistent: pre-allotted place for a class of beings (‘abortions’) and their containment/dwelling—no later frame or moral address.
- ¶7: Intra-cosmic geography and containment (‘depths of the earth, below the mountains’). ‘Watchers’ tradition is mythic but not introduced via NT citation or ecclesial application here.
- ¶8: Still functions as cosmic-historical logistics (assigned ‘thirty-six cities,’ lifespan specification). The evaluative phrase ‘no knowledge of righteousness… nor divinity’ could be a later moral gloss, but it does not pivot to church practice or present-world polemic; overall remains system-internal.

## Chapter 46
### Concerning the Ambassador.

*This chapter is largely a compact, impersonal sequence of pre-temporal appointments within the Manichaean cosmic plan (Ambassador, ships, Judge, astral fate, destroyers, Builder). The only clear late/polemical reach beyond the closed cosmic system is the clause about "the sects" (¶5), which applies the ordination of Christ to the editor’s contemporary religious landscape.*

Know that before night and day were differentiated in the universe, the Third [Am]bassador was summoned. And afterwards, he was sent to this void and outer waste that he might purify the light, and separate night and day from each other.

Before the light was released and freed and ascended through this zone, the Father of Life made beautiful for it the ships of light. He constructed them so that it might be purified in them, and be sculpted and fashioned in good order.

[Bef]ore Sin had multiplied and had made a kingdom in the flesh, before the acquitted and the condemn[ed] were set apart from each other, the Judge [w]as sent forth and revealed. His throne was created and placed [i]n the living atmosphere, so that he would be a just Judge, so that there the righteous and the sinners would be set apart and tested and separated from each other.

Also, before the error and offence of the sects were displayed in the universe, the blessed Christ was ordained and brought forth

Again, [befo]re mankind had been engendered, some were named ['rich' and] others 'poor' on earth [ ... ] upon them. Before they die, they who will die are marked out by the stars and the signs of the zodiac in the sphere. They are appointed for them; in them are their births. And their root is bound up with their zodiacal signs; and they are compelled by them and brought to an equal judgement in accordance with their deeds and their sins.

Again, before the universe is dissolved, [and the] entire earth, the destroyers were appointed and instructed for its dissolution.

And before the ne[w] aeon was [built and] made be[auti]ful and marked out, [the] Great Builder [w]as summoned, the first architect and the angels who are with him, who would build and make beautiful the new aeon.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Direct audience address and lesson-introduction formula; functions as editorial/homiletic frame rather than cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological sequencing (“before X… afterwards Y…”) describing a pre-temporal salvific action within the system (no citation, no institutional application).
- ¶3: System-internal salvific mechanics (Light purification via cosmic ‘ships’), in the characteristic expository voice of the teaching substrate.
- ¶4: Still framed as pre-temporal appointment and cosmic architecture (Judge, throne, living atmosphere). Although moral vocabulary (“righteous/sinners”) raises the pastoral register, it remains a system description (eschatological sorting mechanism) rather than an exhortation or church application.
- ¶5: The pre-temporal ordination formula is consistent with the chapter’s core sequence, but the explicit targeting of ‘sects’ is an editorial/application move beyond cosmic-to-cosmic mapping. ‘Christ’ here may be the Manichaean cosmic figure, but the anti-sect framing is late in function.
- ¶6: Deterministic astral-zodiacal mechanism stated as structure (“marked out,” “appointed,” “root… bound up”): cosmos → human fate within the same system, no exhortation/citation.
- ¶7: Cosmic eschatological logistics in the same iterative ‘before… appointed’ style; no institutional or devotional overlay.
- ¶8: Closed-system cosmology (new aeon construction; Builder/architect/angels) expressed as pre-temporal planning; typical substrate scheduling motif.

## Chapter 47
### Concerning the Four great Things

*Nearly the entire chapter is systematic cosmological exposition: a fourfold apportioning of the "ruling-power" across cosmic structures, followed by a mechanical rationale for suspending the leaders on the stellar wheel and an extended king/kingdom analogy to express proportional balance. Only the opening dialogue attribution is hagiographic frame; there is no NT citation or institutional/pastoral extension here (despite the chapter-level fatigue warning).*

The wheel of the stars, which is displayed to you, is a great mighty thing! Also, it is one among the four great things; since the whole ruling-power is assigned to four parts.

The first part is the powers who occur in the ten firmaments that exist above the wheel, and the ruling-power that is bound in them, as it is imprisoned within them in the heavens that are above.

The second part is the eight earths that are below; the four that are composite, and the four places of darkness. The second part extends from this earth, upon which mankind walks, towards the underside.

The third part is the walls and the vessels that surround the worlds. They are the four mountains and the three vessels; with what is in them, and the ruling-power that is trapped within them, and the powers who are masters over them.

The fourth part is the sphere of the stars that is appointed with great diligence, as it is ordered in a [ ... m]any [ ... ]; that of the Father of Life, [and that of the Mother of] the living.

Its [ ... ] very [ ... ] with diligence, because of the great and cruel powers, and the titanic figures, [who are] in it; they being suspended from it. So, all the first-born and the leaders of all the rulers, they who were existing in the three parts, these were garnered. They were collected together, seized, bound and assigned on this wheel. Each was apportioned by these three parts. They were distributed, entwined, and weighted against their companions of the same size.

Still, for its part, the wheel is equal to them in its strength; the powers of the enmity who are bound on it. Indeed, they were suspended on the circling wheel because of their cruelty; so that, due to the wind and the motion in which they go round, they comprehend not the place wherein they are set. For if they had been bound in the heavens, the heavens would not have had the strength to support them. Again, if they had been assigned on the earths, on the fixtures that are below, they also would not have had the strength to support them. Rather, they were suspended from the wheel of the circling stars; so that the power and the light that should come up from below can be received, and given to the heavenly ones above them. Conversely, whatever will fall from above, whether cold or hot, the wheel of the sphere will also take it and cast it out upon the entire earth, upon all the wood.

Behold, the ordinance of the wheel is in this form. For the powers who are entwined on the wheel are like this: a king mastering his kingdom by this mighty legionary force of all the leaders of the kingdom; and with all the armour of the soldiery that is collected together for him; and also with the mass of the property of the kingdom; and the beautiful lustful aspect of the women who are collected together for him; together with [ ... ] property [ ... ] and those cattle [ ... ] that have been distributed about among his retinue. Yet, if you ask about that king, he is matched with all his retinue. His retinue, for its part, is matched against his entire kingdom. Judgement shall come forth from him, vindication and condemnation, to his entire kingdom; by his wisdom, skill and strength.

Now, just as for this king, who is matched against the measure of his entire kingdom by his wisdom, skill and strength; so also is it for the powers who are bound on the sphere as compared to the ruling-power, and the powers who exist in the entire zone. Like the king towards his retinue; so too is it that this totality is gathered to the sphere, to seven leaders, to twelve signs of the zodiac. As all the cities are gathered to the king, again they gather to the leaders of the universe. All that is done in the universe above and below: the battles and the disorders and the captivity and the hunger and the lust and the property; so shall they increase and diminish through these leaders. They are the motivaters of the whole created order; the totality being gathered in to them the way that I have told you. I have opened your eyes!

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with a standard Kephalaia frame attribution (later editorial container), but the content after the colon is a compact structural thesis (fourfold apportioning of the ruling-power) consistent with the impersonal cosmological teaching layer.
- ¶2: Numbered, enumerative cosmography ("first part"), mapping cosmic regions (ten firmaments) to bound powers—systematic, non-citational exposition.
- ¶3: Continues the fourfold partition with fixed cosmological geography (eight earths; four composite + four Darkness-places) and spatial extension—still internal to the cosmic architecture.
- ¶4: Characteristic substrate-style cataloguing (four mountains/three vessels) plus containment mechanics (trapped ruling-power; masters over them).
- ¶5: Cosmic administrative ordering attributed to primary divine hypostases (Father of Life/Mother of the living) without Gospel citation or ecclesial application; lacunae preserved.
- ¶6: Process-mechanics for cosmic restraint (garnered/collected/bound/assigned/weighted) in an impersonal explanatory voice; no institutional referents. Page marker removed per extraction rules.
- ¶7: Classic cosmological engineering rationale (why the wheel, not heavens/earths; balancing forces; transfer of powers/light; distribution of cold/hot). Entirely cosmos→cosmos causal description.
- ¶8: Didactic analogy (king/retinue/kingdom) used to express proportional equivalence and governance; although it draws on human imagery, it functions as internal explanatory correspondence rather than pointing to contemporary institutions.
- ¶9: Returns from analogy to explicit cosmic structure (seven leaders; twelve zodiacal signs) and causation within the created order. The closing direct-address flourish is rhetorically late-sounding, but it does not pivot to church discipline or Gospel citation, so the paragraph still reads as continuous cosmological instruction.

## Chapter 48
### Concerning the Conduits.

*The chapter is overwhelmingly a systematic cosmological-mechanical exposition of three ‘conduits/roots’ linking heavens, earths, trees, flesh, and the wheel of stars. Later material is mainly the dialogue/hagiographic apparatus (speaker attributions, disciples’ petitions). No NT citation overlay appears; the few frame formulas are detachable without disturbing the internal cosmos→cosmos mapping logic.*

There are three conduits that exist through the entire zone, from the abyss to its heights.

The first conduit is the root of all the powers above, they that exist in all the heavens. They are cast off and fettered on the earths below; because the earth below is the ‘stripping off’, the garment and the body of the powers that are above the heavens.

For when the Father of Life constructed the heavenly worlds, he divested them of their bodies, [he] threw them to the earths below.

So, the upper worlds are of the soul and the spirit; but the worlds below are of the body and the carcass [...] Now as for this, he [...] the powers of heaven; he sealed it on their bodies and their carcasses that are on the earths, so that when life comes up from the fullness of these earths, it can be entirely drawn up to the heights in the roots of what is heavenly, that are fastened in their bodies. And also, all life can be purified in that place.

However, the waste that will be swept from the purified one who ascends to the heights will be let fall to the ground through the conduits of these conduits, and will be poured down [...] and thrown to the underside.

This is the first conduit, which comes from the powers of the heavens to their bodies and their carcasses that are upon the earth; also, from their bodies and [their] carcasses that are on the earths to the powers of the heavens.

The second conduit is the one that comes from the temples and dwellings and cities that exist in the heavens down to the ground, to the five shapes of tree that grow upon the earth. The life comes up from the trees to the temples and cities. Life too, and the residue of the heavenly ones, comes down to the trees in a conduit.

And the third conduit is all the powers and householders who dwell in all the firmaments, whose roots come down from them to the five worlds of flesh that creep upon the earth, being fixed to one another. Now, the power and the life that shall be gathered in from the worlds of flesh are dispersed among them in various shapes. The powers above shall draw them to the upper side through conduits.

[The was]te too, and the lust and the evil-doing and the anger that will be greater in the powers of [heave]n, shall be poured to the ground through their various conduits. They shall be discharged upon mankind and the other remaining animals. When what is heavenly will wash the waste and the stench and the poison down on the creations of the flesh below, in their turn the creations shall be greater in lust and anger and evil-doing against each other through the action of their fathers who are on high.

Still, look at the wheel of the stars! It has no [root in this earth], but its roots are bound in the totality. The wheel of stars, then, receives life through the power and the life that ascend from the earths through conduits and the firmaments above. It (also) takes life from those conduits that receive up to the firmaments and the heavenly worlds above it, it takes life out from that place.

Look, in that the wheel has no root in this earth, from where did the stars and signs of the zodiac find this authority? They became masters over these five fleshes and five trees. Even though you have told us that the wheel has no root there! For you said to us that every thing, if it can be reduced from the earth, the heavenly powers above draw it through a conduit. Also, we see that every thing that a person will attain, whether it be wealth that comes into his possession or poverty that will accrue to him, or his sickness and health; he attains it through the signs of the zodiac and the star under which he shall be born.

Nevertheless, what I proclaimed to you, namely that the wheel has no root therein, I have recounted to you properly. It is set aright in its position. For when the Living Spirit, the Father of Life, constructed the heavens and the firmaments above, he bound their roots into the earths below; in their carcasses that he had stripped from them. Yet, when he constructed the wheel, [he] did not bind its root into the earths, but he bound the root of the wheel by the sea giant.

And when [the] Ambassador came, he displayed his image and purified and brought the light out from all [the] heavens. Matter, however, which is present in all the rulers, spurted out against the image of the Ambassador. It went up in order to reach it, but reached it not. It dropped back and fell to the [under]side. Now, when it had fallen from above, it separated out into three parts.

The first part fell to the wheel; another part fell to the earth; an[other part fell to the se]a. [By means] of the part that fell to the sphere, [the r]oot was bound in the trees and flesh. Therefore, because of this, they received authority and became masters in the five worlds of the flesh and the five worlds of the tree.

Through the nature that came down [upo]n the wheel the root was bound in the trees. Also, by means of the abortions that came to the ground, another root was bound in the fleshes.

Therefore, because of this, the stars and signs of the zodiac received authority over the trees and the fleshes: through the root of Sin that fell down; and [the ro]ot of the abortions that were aborted there, they fell to the ground.

However, the[se] other conduits that [the] Living Spirit has bound in the e[a]rths, they that the soul of the elements comes up through to the heavens; those (conduits) are not bound to the wheel. Rather, they are plundered from them by the powers on the wheel, that hang from it.

The first is the one that comes from the firmaments to the earths, and the one that comes from the wheel to the sea giant. That is only one. So, the other root is the one that comes [fr]om [the temples] to the trees. The third is bound by means of the abortions in the f[leshes]; also in the fleshes the fathers of the abortions who are in the heavens [...] are bound and twined, and the [y] that [...]

You have inquired about a [...] and you have asked about this lesson in superiority, great [...] the earth from which much life ascends [...] the Father of Life has drawn them. He has fastened them from the powers [...] he has bound them in the earths. (These conduits) are the roots of the ent[ire] ruling-power that the life of the living soul is drawn up through, it coming up from the whole earth, from all the mountains, fields and islands.

After that first great root is the root of the trees, which is bound to the temples and the buildings above. For there also is much great life coming up from the tree and the entire Cross, being drawn up from them to the upper side like a great host.

What, however, is lesser [...] of their conduit [...] he takes up [...] the roots [...] the earths, they [...] the whole earth in all the worlds. Again, these of the trees are present in the whole earth, whether small or great; but the root of mankind [...] is not present in the whole earth, other than only this world of the south. Therefore, even now these fleshes do not make [...] in the entire world of the south [...] there is a small part of them in [...] of the upper side of the world of the south in [...] in number than the [...]

I have instructed you about the roots [...] them, [I] have taught you that they are bound by their [roots ...] with one another. They stir one another, they draw life from one another through the action of the powers above, which stirs the nature that dwells in them all.

Now, when the Matter that is in them will be crushed and despoiled, then that Matter too shall [...] its members and overturn them and [...] each other [...] stir [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula introduces a compact, impersonal numbered cosmological claim (three conduits spanning abyss→heights).
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological mechanism: conduit/root linking heavenly powers to their stripped bodies on the earths (cosmos→cosmos).
- ¶3: Mythic-cosmological construction act (Father of Life) explaining the earlier mechanism; no institutional or exhortatory pivot.
- ¶4: Degree/correspondence pairing (psychic/pneumatic vs somatic/ptomatic) plus a purification-transport mechanism through roots—internal system description.
- ¶5: Cosmic circulation schema (ascent of life, descent of residue) stated as process, not moral exhortation.
- ¶6: Recapitulative definition of conduit as a bidirectional link between cosmic strata (heavens↔earths).
- ¶7: Systematic second member of a triad: heavenly ‘cities’ to five trees; still entirely cosmographic (heavenly temples/cities, not contemporary institutions).
- ¶8: Third member of triad; fivefold structure (five worlds of flesh) and impersonal transport mechanics mark old systematic layer.
- ¶9: Cosmic causality of passions as descending ‘waste’ from heavenly powers; despite ethical vocabulary, it functions as cosmological mechanism (no direct exhortation).
- ¶10: ‘Apostle’ attribution is compiling-frame vocabulary; the content is a structural claim about the wheel’s roots/life-supply within the cosmic system.
- ¶11: Disciples’ question is substantive and cosmological (zodiacal authority/fate mechanics tied to roots), though wrapped in dialogue frame.
- ¶12: Attribution is late frame; the explanation itself is core cosmological construction logic (Living Spirit; roots bound to carcasses; exception of wheel bound to sea giant).
- ¶13: Mythic-cosmological episode (Ambassador’s image; Matter in rulers; threefold division) functioning as etiological mechanism inside the system.
- ¶14: Threefold distribution (wheel/earth/sea) and binding of roots to trees/flesh: classic structural cosmology (cosmos→cosmos).
- ¶15: Etiology for two bindings (trees vs flesh) stated as internal causal mechanics (nature; abortions) without pastoral address.
- ¶16: Astrological authority grounded in a cosmological ‘root’ event; ‘Sin’ functions as a substantive falling entity/root rather than a mere moral label.
- ¶17: Clarifies what is and is not bound to the wheel; again a mechanical relation among cosmic structures (Living Spirit; soul of elements; wheel-powers).
- ¶18: A late Q&A wrapper encloses a compact structural recap of the three conduits/roots; the recap itself preserves the internal system mapping.
- ¶19: Pure petition formula (“We entreat you, our master…”) without independent cosmological exposition.
- ¶20: Speaker tag is frame; the response is structural ranking logic grounded in cosmic ‘roots’ spanning the whole earth system.
- ¶21: Rank-order within the triad (earth-root then tree-root) plus ‘Cross’ as cosmic structure; impersonal cosmological comparison.
- ¶22: Comparative extent of bindings (earths vs trees vs mankind) framed as cosmic geography (‘world of the south’), not institutional application.
- ¶23: Concluding systemic statement: mutual stirring/life-transfer among bound roots via powers above—process voice characteristic of the substrate.
- ¶24: Fragmentary but still internal cosmological process involving personified Matter acting on ‘members’; no frame/pastoral cues.

## Chapter 49
### Concerning the Wheel and the Conduits

*This chapter is overwhelmingly core mechanical teaching: a brief Q&A frame introduces a cosmological problem (wheel vs. conduits), followed by an impersonal explanation using analogies (ship/water; bird/air) to describe why pneumatic conduits are not cut or entangled. Only minimal dialogue attributions appear as later framing.*

How does the wheel circle over the conduits, which extend from the heavens to the earths, and it not cut them? Or why shall the conduits not get entangled in with the wheel as it circles and drives [ ... through the u]niverse?

The reason that the conduits shall not be cut, for they are not cut, is because they are spiritual. Thus the conduits are like the waters through which ships sail. You shall find the prow that is in position in the front part of the ship divides the waters, casting them back and forth. So, when it parts the waters and that ship opens up the water with its cleaving prow, immediately and without delay the waters mingle with each other again behind the ship; and the path of the ship shall not be discernible in the midst of the waters.

Just as when a bird flies up in the air, for its path is not visible in the air, and in the way of mankind, in the sun within the conduits. They also shall not be tangled up in them, and neither shall you find their path through the air known.

This is also the case with the wheel. It turns through the conduits and the air. The wheel shall not cut the conduits, nor are the conduits themselves entangled in the wheel.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue attribution and honorific address mark later framing, but the question itself is a technical cosmological problem-statement typical of the teaching substrate.
- ¶2: Core-style causal explanation of a cosmic mechanism (pneumatic conduits not being cut) with natural analogy; no institutional/ethical pivot. The opening speaker tag is the later Q&A container.
- ¶3: Continues the mechanical analogy (bird/air) to explain invisibility/non-entanglement of paths—cosmos-to-nature explanatory mode without citations or church application.
- ¶4: Concise concluding statement of the cosmological rule (wheel passes through conduits/air without cutting/entangling), in impersonal explanatory voice.

## Chapter 50
### Concerning these Names: God, Rich One, and Angel; who they are.

*The chapter is largely a systematic, internal-to-the-cosmic-system taxonomy of three naming-levels (god/rich one/angel) across successive emanational sequences. It is wrapped in a hagiographic Q&A opening (¶1–2) and contains two dialogue-attribution seams (¶3, ¶10) that must be stripped. The late/Christian-leaning vocabulary in ¶9 (“apostolate”) reads as Mani-community diction applied to a still-cosmological mission sequence rather than an NT citation or institutional graft.*

There are these three archetypes [in the land of light, and there is no measure to] them!

So, the ones who are called 'god', are the gods whom the Father has summoned from himself. He has established them after the likeness of his greatness.

Conversely, these who are named 'rich [ ... ]', are the evocations of the first rich gods of the Father; because, when the Father had summoned (the first), they themselves summoned evocations. They called them 'rich'.

They too, the rich ones, have summoned their evocations. They call them 'angel'. These are the three archetypes who occur in the land of light. There is no measure to apply to them!

Once again, listen! Other persons who are named 'god' are the emanations who have come from the Father; the evocations of the Father whom he summoned forth. They came out to the contest and humiliated the enmity.

Conversely, the 'rich ones' are the evocations of these first living words. They too, the rich ones, have come and performed and fulfilled the pleasure of the greatness, in the worlds that are above and below.

On the other hand, the ones who are named the 'angels' are the evocations of the rich ones, who had come from the three living words. They have been sent in an embassy and an apostolate to this building. They have come to the entire divinity, which is established in silence and in hiddenness. And, also, they have come to all the souls who have been entangled in the enemy. They have brought them hope and confidence.

Again, they are called 'god', all the gods who belong to the household of the Great Spirit.

Conversely, the ones who are called 'angel', are all the angels who belong to the household of the Beloved of the Lights.

The ones who are called 'rich', are all the rich ones who belong to the household of the glorious Ambassador.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic opening (“Once again the enlightener speaks”) plus meta-linguistic framing (“people pronounce it with their mouths”) and interrogative setup; functions as editorial container rather than impersonal substrate exposition.
- ¶2: Disciples’ request formula (“Tell us, our master”) is classic dialogue frame; no cosmological mechanism expounded.
- ¶3: Underlying line is impersonal cosmological classification (three archetypes in the land of light; immeasurability), but introduced by later dialogue framing.
- ¶4: Systematic emanational definition within the cosmic system (Father → summoned gods → likeness of greatness); no citation or institutional application.
- ¶5: Continues the triadic degree structure (second-order evocations) with internal causality; lacuna preserved; no reach into contemporary institutions.
- ¶6: Completes the three-step emanational chain (god → rich one → angel) and reiterates the archetypal/immeasurable register—hallmarks of the substrate’s structural voice.
- ¶7: Second sequence applies the same naming taxonomy to a temporal/cosmic phase (contest with enmity) but remains cosmos→cosmos; the exhortative “listen” here reads as didactic rhetoric, not institutional address.
- ¶8: Continues systematic tiering (“first living words” → “rich ones”) with functional description spanning cosmic regions; no contemporary referents.
- ¶9: Still an internal cosmological mission description (angels as third-order envoys to the cosmos/souls). The term “apostolate” may reflect later Mani-community diction, but there is no Gospel/NT citation and no cosmos→institution mapping.
- ¶10: Teaching content is a further systematic application of the naming taxonomy to a cosmic ‘household’ series; framing formula indicates later editorial packaging.
- ¶11: Cosmic-internal classification (“household of the Beloved of the Lights”); despite computational ‘application’ flag, there is no second-person exhortation or institutional identification.
- ¶12: Completes the third series by mapping the naming-term to a cosmic household (glorious Ambassador); impersonal, structural closure of the triad.

## Chapter 51
### Concerning the First Man.

*This chapter is overwhelmingly a compact cosmological battle-teaching about the First Man’s five garments (living Fire plus four auxiliaries). The only clear later material is a brief hagiographic dialogue/frame opener. Despite the chapter-level fatigue signal, the surviving paragraphs after the opener remain cosmos→cosmos exposition; paragraph 5 is flagged by the pipeline for “application” but reads as internal cosmological continuation (air strengthening the four).*

When the [First] Man came forth against the ene[my ...] garment [...] the Darkness with the garment of [of] living fire. With that garment he withered the tumescence (?) of the enemy; he brought down i[t]s foolery and its vanity.

Now, this one alone was sufficient for them. [Y]et, si[n]ce the living fire was weakened, because of this he added [to it another fou]r garments: that of the wind, that of the water, that of the light, and that of the a[i]r. And this, (why) has he given the four? He gave them s[o] th[a]t they would be helpe[r]s to t[h]at god, wh[ich i]s the living fire; so that [...] not with [in] the living f[ire], and penetrate it; and [the po]ison and the [enmi]ty cut it off.

While the fire received a bl[ow and] a wound in its body; yet still, [...] all the powers of da[rk]ness because of it [...] It [...] necessity [...] brothers, to cause them to receive a blow and a woun[d to]o. The power of the en/emy mixed in with them. Rath[er, ex]cept [the air]. The Darkness did not mix in with it, nor was the burning of the enemy able to penet/rate it.

Never-theless, even the one that remained behind without the wound, which is the air, it stripped off garment and power. It gave it to its [fo]ur brothers in its place; so that by that [ga]rment of the living air they would become strong, and [...] profit, and endure, namely its other four brothers. And it [be]came for them [...] life.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue attribution (“Once again … speaks”) is the Kephalaia’s editorial/hagiographic container voice, not the impersonal teaching layer.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological narrative of descent/battle using internal correspondential register (garment/element as armour) with no institutional or audience-directed application.
- ¶3: Systematic, explanatory mechanics (“why… given the four”) detailing the fivefold armour as a functional structure; entirely within the cosmological system.
- ¶4: Continues internal cosmological process-description (wounding/mixing/penetration). “brothers” functions as intra-system kinship language among the garments/elements (confirmed by ¶5), not audience address.
- ¶5: Although computationally flagged for “application voice,” the content stays cosmos→cosmos (air’s power redistributed to the four elements). No exhortation, citation, or institutional mapping; likely continuous core exposition, though phrasing may reflect later smoothing.

## Chapter 52
### Concerning the [ ... ] of the Light

*This chapter is largely an editorial moral/application unit (unity among hearers) built on a short cosmological premise: the Light’s inherent concord versus the Darkness’ self-division. The core substrate is preserved mainly as statements about internal harmony/order in the Light-realm and internal disunity in the Darkness-realm; the shift to analogy with "the kingdom of this world" and direct second-person exhortation marks later application.*

[Now as to what affects the] Light, behold, at the beginning the Darkness was strong against it; [ ... ] no disunity [ ... ] exists in the Light.

[ ... ] how far [ ... the lig]ht that came forth from [ ... ] in that place in a [ ... ] alone [ ... ] the greatness of the Light with one another for ever, and nor did one of them transgress the command and order that his brother had entrusted to him forever.

However, the Darkness, the enemy, was doomed and overcome; because of the arising of disharmony it was divided within itself. Even the kingdom was divided within itself; [and] ev[en] the [powers, who had] left their abyss behind and come up fo[llow-ing] it, were doomed because of the disunity that was present in all of them. The Darkness was destroyed, and its other powers were humiliated,

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Begins with a formulaic frame attribution; underneath it is a concise cosmological claim that stays within the Light/Darkness system (harmony in Light), consistent with older impersonal teaching.
- ¶2: Impersonal exposition about the internal order of the Light-realm (no second-person address; no institutional referents). Harmony/obedience is described as a property of the cosmic community itself, not as an exhortation.
- ¶3: Core mechanism preserved: Darkness’ defeat is explained by internal disharmony (cosmos→cosmos). Final clause pivots to an illustrative comparison with worldly kingdoms (cosmos→this world), signaling later applicative editing.
- ¶4: Illustrative material framed in terms of a "kingdom" gaining glory when its "associates" agree; the language functions as a didactic analogy rather than cosmological system-description, and it no longer anchors explicitly in the Light/Darkness mechanics.
- ¶5: Continues the applicative analogy of one kingdom condemning another; lacks systematic cosmic correspondences and reads as moral-political exemplum supporting the later exhortation.
- ¶6: Direct second-person exhortation (“you yourselves…”) using the teaching to command communal unanimity; this is application voice, not impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶7: Continues exhortation with warning and optative aside (“may it not happen!”). Even where Sin is personified, the paragraph is wholly a behavioral application to the community.
- ¶8: Imperatival paraenesis (“Neither be divided… and you will conquer…”), focused on community conduct and promised victory; no independent cosmological mapping is being expounded here.

## Chapter 53
### Concerning the First Man

*This chapter is overwhelmingly core cosmological teaching: First Man’s descent and the stripping of the storehouses, followed by correspondential analogies (marsh; human body/head and limbs; corpse). The only clear later material is the formulaic dialogue-attribution opening in ¶1, which functions as hagiographic frame around an otherwise core paragraph. Despite the chapter-level ‘fatigue’ signal, no explicit institutional/pastoral or NT overlay appears in the surviving paragraphs.*

At the time when the First Man was sent to the war in the first garment of [living fire], which he gave to the first nature that exists [...] in that single garment. [...] he hunted Matter, the thought of Death, [...] and all its other powers.

When the First Man came up from the abyss, [...] the Living Spirit brought the rulers whom he had hunted [...] element, that [...] of [the land of] Darkness, that he might construct from them this universe and fashion everything appropriate for the Greatness. From that time on, the place of the storehouses of the elements [...] he destroyed and laid waste the [...] all that were in it.

Just like a marsh, of which the fish and birds are snared in a net, and only the waters are left behind and the reeds and the plants that grow in it, this is also the case with the storehouses of Death. The [First] Man snared their counsels and [their k]in that were [in them], and the storehouses were left behind in their [dwelli]ng place.

So then, the case of the land of Darkness is like this, as is that of the storehouses that were in it.

Just as the body of man has many limbs in it, [the land of Darkness] has [storehouses from] its beginning [...] all [the limbs] that hang from the great head of the [body]. A single root is the root of them all, because they all hang from the great head that is on top of the body. Should a limb be cut from the tip of [the]se small [h]eads, the great head that is on top of them [al]l stays in its place the way that it is [...] you find this person alive. Al[l] these sm[all h]eads are firmly set in it [...]

Wh[en] they will cut from them [...] all of them, these little [heads] shall [...] the corpses, because they might see [...] be firmly set [...] in the body, which they joined wi[th it ...]

In exactly this way, it is also the case that the storehouses of Darkness are the same, the things from which the [Darkness gushed] forth.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening is a classic Kephalaia frame formula (“Once again…” + speaker attribution), but the remainder is impersonal cosmological narration about First Man’s garment of living fire and the hunting/snaring of Matter and Death-powers—internal to the Manichaean cosmic system.
- ¶2: Systematic cosmological process-description (ascent from abyss; Living Spirit’s construction of the universe from captured rulers; devastation of storehouses). No citation, exhortation, or institutional mapping.
- ¶3: Correspondential natural analogy (marsh/netted fish and birds) used to describe a cosmic condition (barren storehouses). The mapping stays cosmos↔nature without pointing to contemporary institutions.
- ¶4: Brief summative closure in the same impersonal expository voice; still purely cosmological.
- ¶5: Extended body-cosmos correspondence (storehouses as limbs depending on a great head/root). No pastoral ‘you should…’ application; the brief ‘you find’ functions as explanatory rhetoric within the analogy, not audience exhortation.
- ¶6: Lacunal continuation of the head/limbs analogy, still within the same correspondential explanatory register (corpse/body imagery) and without later institutional or citation markers.
- ¶7: Concluding applicative phrase (“In exactly this way…”) refers back to the internal analogy and restates the cosmological claim; it does not pivot to church/institutional application, so it remains core.

## Chapter 54
### [Concerning] the Quality of the Garments

*This chapter is overwhelmingly cosmological-architectonic teaching (mixture of Light/Darkness; ordering of garments/firmaments; artisan metaphors for the Living Spirit/Father of Life; final separation). The only clear later layer is a brief dialogue-attribution frame at the start of ¶1, which must be stripped. The extended carpenter/architect metaphors (¶3–¶4) remain within cosmos→cosmos exposition (no institutional/NT anchoring), though they are rhetorically elaborated and could be a secondary expansion within the teaching tradition.*

At the time when the F[ir]st [Man ...] he gave the leadership to his five [sons ... the] Living Spirit and the Mother of Life [...] as the entire ruling-power is entwined and crucified [...] they were poured out and mixed with each other, the light with the darkness and the darkness with the light.

At that time the Living Spirit called one of his sons, one rich with power. He gave to him the leadership of the garments. Now, he himself [...] of [...] he hung them and set them up [...] of the Father of Life [...] he set up all things [...] he opened [...] ten firmaments [...] the wheel of the stars [...] them and the [...].

The Living Spirit is like this: Just as [...] and an architect who might build a dwelling for the king, and divide it up into separate houses and [separate ...] everything that is required for beauty [...] and he cleans them. And also the stones [...] them for beauty and loveliness, and he arranges them and makes them so that should the king come and inhabit that palace, he might glorify him.

Furthermore, the Father of Life resembles a wise and skilled carpenter [...] before it was worked [...] before it was established and made beautiful and [...] carpenter [...] works in it, but [...] in him in his [...] in his understanding, and he brings the saw and the adze, and cuts it into separate pieces [...] it and he pulls the [...] it with the plane and he smooths it. He shall make many [...] wooden implements, arranging them shape by shape; he cuts and produces wooden implements from it, wooden implements of different kinds, (such as) dishes, bowls. This wood, that [...] since previously it had not been worked before it was sealed by [the ski]ll and wisdom of this dextrous carpenter. He cut it up into different sorts of implements useful to his master.

So is it also with the Father of Life. In his wisdom he has [...] in limbs [...] palace [...] in it or in a [...] useful [...] the ships of light [...] all the other works [...] in the [...] so that when the great king comes, he who is the Third Ambassador, who [...] he might dwell in this universe [...]

... live. The light, however, that is in it [...] wise, and he takes it up to the land; but the waste and the darkness will be gathered and thrown down to the abyss.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The opening speaker-attribution is a late compiling frame; the remainder is impersonal cosmological mechanics (mixture/crucifixion/entangling of powers) consistent with the older substrate.
- ¶2: Systematic cosmological construction account (sons, garments, ten firmaments, wheel of the stars) in expository voice; no citation, exhortation, or institutional pointing.
- ¶3: Didactic artisan simile used to explain a cosmic function (Living Spirit as world-builder preparing a palace for a king). Despite worldly imagery, it does not anchor to contemporary institutions and stays within the cosmic analogy.
- ¶4: Continuation of the craft-metaphor sequence (carpenter/tools/shaping) functioning as cosmological explanation by analogy; no explicit editorial seam into church practice or present-day polemic.
- ¶5: Returns explicitly to internal cosmological beings and structures (Father of Life; ships of light; Third Ambassador) and reads as the doctrinal referent of the preceding metaphors.
- ¶6: Cosmic sorting/separation mechanism (light raised; waste/darkness cast to abyss) in impersonal process language; no institutional or NT overlay markers.

## Chapter 55
### Concerning the Fashioning of Adam

*This chapter is predominantly cosmological teaching about why the Third Ambassador displayed his Image, how the rulers copied it to fashion Adam and Eve without the consent of Greatness, and how other composite life-forms arose without sexual intercourse. The only clear later layer is the disciples’ framed question (¶1) and a brief pedagogical closing (¶8). No NT citation formulas appear; the long “free woman” simile functions as an internal correspondential exposition rather than an institutional/pastoral application, though it may be a later rhetorical elaboration within the teaching tradition.*

Regarding the Ambassador, the occasion when he came displaying his image, he [d]id not come so th[a]t he might reveal his image to the ru[l]ers in the universe. Rather, he came and displayed to the universe be[cause of h]is soul and his son, who is [ ... ] every [ ... ], that he might enable him to live [ ... ] and make for him a release; and to free (the soul) from every bond and all the fetters, in which it is entangled and bound.

Still, you, look and see! The rulers and the authorities, in that they had no form in [ ... ] him, they lusted for his image. They reflected upon themselves, that [they] had no [one] like him in their creation. They sealed hi[s im]a[g]e in their heart, within their soul. Afterward[s, they] built it according to his likeness; they formed Adam and [Ev]e. They sealed the image of the exalted one in their [soul]. So, they [co]pied with an imitation; but they have not [copied i]n truth! Rather, just as [ ... ... ] it is made as these, for [i]t came [ ... ] but it is not l[ik]e him in truth. [Again, so too] is this figure of ma[nkind. They copi]ed it against the image of the exalted one [ ... ]

Similarly the [t]ree, for while it is like the [ ... it is] not [copi]ed in truth.

This matt[er is comparable to] this: As a great, fre[e] woman [ ... ] within her [ ... ... ] rich in wealth [ ... ] po[ss]essions; she is also beautiful in her beauty, and is virtuous in her modesty. She walks in a free and reputable position. The fame of her beauty has spread in every c[i]ty. She sur[passes ... ] Behold h[er] chamber [ ... ] one who is mad, because [of] the nobles and the great ones. Al[so], they lust for her beautiful face. They did not [ ... ] her, because she is hidden in her palace [ ... ] within in a [ ... ] meet that woman [ ... ] his so[n]; or rather the head [ ... t]hat garment. This free woman shall [ ... ] leave the cha[mber] behind her and come to the street [ ... ] her head [ ... ] and she reveals her [face and her beauty ... ] because of her beloved broth[er]. This wom[an], on whom no man ever looked, nor did they ever see her fa[ce], as she neither desires nor rejoices [ ... ... ] leave her chamber behind her, and come in the midst of mankind [ ... ] and everyone view her. The hones[t] men and the nobles, even the servants too, and the [ ... ] look at her.

In accord with this simile: Again, [this] too [is] how [ ... ] the Ambassador becomes [ ... ... ] as this free woman [ ... not] according to wantonness, in pride, nor [ ... she co]nsented to the outer world, she [ ... ... ], she revealed her face and her [beau]ty [ ... ] but only due to the constraint of grief [about] her [bel]ov[ed one].

Now, this too is the way of the Third Ambassador, when he came and display[ed his] image with a show towards all the powers. [He did] not [co]me so that all the rulers and the author[it]ies would sculpt a form [after h]is likeness. R[ather], he came because of his son being crucified in the totality; [fo]r he would release him, free him, and rescue him from oppres-sion. Yet, when the rulers looked o[n] him, [th]ey lusted for his image. They fashioned after his [like]ness Adam and Eve, without the consent of the gre[at]ness.

[Understa]nd also this other (matter) that I will teach you. For, [a]s with Adam and Eve, whom the rulers fashioned and [se]aled after the image of the exalted one, so too this [i]s how a manifold divinity and many powers and angels [ ... received] the stamp of that image, which was displayed to them. For, in the majesty of the l[ig]ht and (the image's) love, they glowed with their desire and they were undone. Th[ey] were eager for their lust; like a m[an who looks] to the majesty of a lusted after face, and [ ... ] and he is eager for the [...] of the desire.

This is [als]o the case with those powers, when they looked on that majestic and beautiful [imag]e, they () [ ... ] a first image. They received the stamp of th[at image], which was displayed to them. They copied [ ... wh]ich th[ey co]pied out in a likeness, it being made [ ... ... ] the ones that they had put forth, which had received a [stamp of this im]age; but inward even to the rocks [ ... ... ] which occur from place to place. The [...] of that image, which was displayed in the universe [ ... ] they received a stamp, it being revealed there, and an impress.

Now, this new birth that they begot in a new [ ... ] in the world, is Adam and Eve. [There is not a] form in their world since the beginning like it; because that [im]age [occ]urs not in the worlds b[el]ow.

Not all the figures that have been fashioned were fashioned by birth from displayed intercourse. Rather, there are other figures fashioned [ ... ]

One of them is the sea [gi]ant, the 'sweeping' from the sphere, which the [Father] of Life discharged into the sea. Its own fire and thought fashioned it [in] a figure.

The second figure is the one [that] fell to the earth. It made the sea its womb. Its own l[us]t became its sculptor. It alone fashioned it to a nature, which is the root of death. Yet, when it came up from the sea, it [ ... ] that it might destroy the works [ ... ] of Life. They immediately sent against it the Adamant of Light, the great 'instruction of vigour'. He humbled it in the districts of the north. He has trampled underfoot and set h[im]self upon it, until the end of the world.

[The] third figure is the nature that fell on wh[at] is dr[y. It] fashioned the tree and set itself there. [ ... the] new figure that was fashioned [ ... d]isplays.

So, in accord with the essence they [ ... the Dark]ness; but yet th[ey] did not come with the King of Dark[ness to the wo]rld. Rather, their fashioning [came] about [ ... ] they are composite and have been displayed in the world.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue framing: disciples questioning the ‘glorious one’ with a report of intra-group opinion. Even though it rehearses cosmological facts, it is embedded as a framed dispute-question rather than impersonal exposition.
- ¶2: Core cosmological mechanics (purpose of the Image-display as a salvific operation freeing the soul) embedded under a clear dialogue-attribution frame marker.
- ¶3: Impersonal cosmological causation (lust → sealing image → fashioning Adam/Eve) with ‘copy vs truth’ ontological distinction; no institutional pointing.
- ¶4: Brief correspondential analogy supporting the ‘likeness but not truth’ doctrine; stays generic and non-institutional.
- ¶5: Extended simile/parable used to explain a cosmic act (voluntary display under constraint). Though it uses mundane imagery (woman, city, nobles), it functions as correspondential exposition rather than mapping to contemporary institutions.
- ¶6: Directly ties the parable back to the Ambassador’s cosmic action; explanatory, not exhortatory.
- ¶7: Core cosmological narrative: Third Ambassador’s Image-display as rescue operation; rulers’ lust produces Adam/Eve. ‘Crucified in the totality’ is the Manichaean cosmic Crucified One, not an NT citation.
- ¶8: Pedagogical wrap-up in second person (‘I have enlightened you… commit it to memory’) functioning as editorial/teacherly closure rather than cosmological mechanism.
- ¶9: Systematic extension of the ‘Image-stamp’ mechanism to many cosmic beings; still internal to the cosmos. Contains simile language but not institutional application. Curly-brace artifact normalized to lacuna.
- ¶10: Impersonal ‘stamping/copying’ cosmological process extended through grades of beings and even ‘rocks’; strongly expository. Curly-brace artifact normalized to lacuna.
- ¶11: Cosmic ontological claim about the unprecedented form and its origin in the displayed Image; ‘new birth’ is descriptive, not institutional/pastoral.
- ¶12: Programmatic cosmological thesis introducing non-sexual generation of forms; impersonal and classificatory.
- ¶13: Cosmic exemplar with named being (Father of Life) and internal faculties (‘fire and thought’) as formative forces—substrate-style mechanism.
- ¶14: Fully cosmological combat-mechanics and personified agents (Adamant of Light) with geographic cosmic locale (‘districts of the north’); no contemporary referents.
- ¶15: Third cosmological exemplar (vegetation/tree) framed as another non-sexual ‘fashioning’; remains within cosmic process language.
- ¶16: General doctrinal conclusion: composite forms not simply imported by the King of Darkness but arising in-world; impersonal, ontological register. Capitalized Darkness as a cosmic principle/agent.

## Chapter 56
### Concerning Saklas and his Powers

*Chapter is largely dominated by systematic cosmological/anthropological teaching: (a) how Sin/Matter retained the seal of the Ambassador’s image through its descent and generative transmission, and (b) a long correspondential microcosm-teaching of the senses as ‘storehouses’ with guards at bodily gates, culminating in Light Mind/Living Spirit taking control from Sin. Strong late drift appears at the end: a clear exhortation paragraph, and an ecclesial-sounding ‘psalms/prayers/hymns/charity’ paragraph that applies the sensory doctrine to community practice. Several paragraphs are MIXED only because of dialogue-attribution formulas (‘the enlightener says/speaks’), which are stripped from the extracted core_text.*

All [these a]bortions, amongst them are Saklas and his consort and the ones whom they have served [ ... ], they are the ones that shaped Adam and Eve. How did they find this beautiful image laid over their shape, even though when it was displayed to their fathers they were not in existence? They never saw the image of the Ambassador! And how did they take the seal of the image of the Ambassador? They added it to [the] shape of Adam and Eve.

The enlightener [speak]s to them thus: The Sin that spurted out from the rulers, which is Matter, shot up [tow]ards the image of the Ambassador. It was cut off from [tha]t place and came down to the earth, for they did not accept [it wi]thin the firmaments. When it came do[wn to] the earth, it formed the tree. It was established within the wood and formed the fruits. And when [the] abortions fell [to] the ground, to the earth, at the [second] time [ ... / ... ]

[... in the] fruits, it assumed the[m] and spoke in the ruler, their leader. He says to his companions: Come! Give me your light, and I will construct for you an image aft[er] the likeness of the exalted one. What he said, they did: they gave it to him, and he constructed [ ... ].

Again the enlightener says: The Sin that s[purted out ... / ... ] which is the Matter that saw the im[age of the Ambassa]dor and that formed the tre[e and was established i]n [i]t; afterwards it came up in the [fruits ... ]. It went in to the rulers [ ... ] they formed Adam and Eve after the [likeness of the] exalt[ed one]. Through the energy of the Sin that had s[een the image of the] Ambassador, it went into the (rulers) [through the fruits. It] assumed them and they [t]oo sculp[ted ... they] begat each of them according to his destiny [ ... ]; one he resembles, one he does not resemble.

The moulder placed in the form of Adam and Eve limbs, outside and within, for perception and activity. He was apportioned house by house. For everything that his perceptions and elements will receive externally there are internal storehouses and repositories and cavities; and what is received in to them is stored in them. Whenever they will be questioned about what is deposited in their internal storehouses, they bring out what they have received within and give it to the questioner who requested it of them.

In this way his faculty [ ... ] outer [limbs] to look at [ ... ] every type with [in ... ]

Also [the fa]culty of the eyes [has] houses and cavities and repositories and stores within, so that every image it might see, whether good or evil, whether loveable or detestable or lustful, it can receive into its storehouses and repositories. Also, when the faculty of the eyes is pleased to send out the image that it saw and took in, it can go in to its storehouses [at the] time and think and seek [ ... ] and it brings it out [and] gives it to the questioner who requested it and the one who wan[t]ed it—whether it be something from lu[st ... or a]n image of love [or ... / ... something] hateful. And thus shall that faculty (of the eyes) produce and do what it does in each category.

The faculty of the ears has its own storehouses also. Every sound it might receive, whether good or evil, shall be taken in and placed in its houses and inner repositories, and it is guarded in its [ ... ] for a thousand days. After a thousand days, if someone comes and asks that faculty about the sound that it heard [at] this time and took into its storehouses, immediately it [sh]all g[o] into its repositories and seek and review and search after this word, and send it out from where it was first put, the place in which it was kept.

[In] like order, the faculty of scent [s]hall function just as that of the eyes and that of the auditory organs. Every [o]dour it shall smell it shall take in [to it] and [d]eposit in its inner storehouses. Every time it will be [a]sked by a questioner, it shall go in [ ... ] and [ ... ] storehouse and remember [ ... ] only these things.

However, even the mouth and the tongue within it, and the taste organ, have a faculty dwelling in them. Again, that faculty too, of taste, has thus cavities and repositories set apart for it. It too receives these tastes and gathers them in. A[nd] at any moment when someone will ask of a taste, if [ ... ] it shall send it out and remember tha[t] taste. It shall snare and give even the mar[k of] th[a]t taste; give its memory to the qu[est]ioner who asks for it.

Again, the fac[ul]ty of touch by the hands is also s[o]: When it might touch, touch shall [receive i]ts memory. And it [takes] it in to its inner repository [until] som[eo]ne will ask this faculty for the memory. [Immediately], it shall go in again and bring out the memory of this touch that it made, and give it to whoever asks for it.

And the faculty of the heart that rules over them all is much the most like this. Every thing that these five faculties will receive and put in store for the faculty of the heart it shall receive and guard. Any time that they will ask for their deposit it shall send out and give every thing that they gave to it.

For the counsel of the heart is like the king. All the ad[v]isers of his city and the leaders of his a[r]my are placed with him. Any time if his genera[ls] will entreat him for whatever they wish, he shall give them the ad[v]ice and opinion by which they move.

Furthermore, it is like a cellarman: [Eve]ry thing that will be entrusted to it by them, and given to it, it shall receive and put in separate vessels and places. Any time [ ... / ... / ... ]

... in the place that it put it.

This also is the way of the faculty of the body. The vision that the eyes will see and give to it, and it receives it in, it shall bring out again from the storehouses wherein it had placed it; and give it to whoever asks for it.

Again, [the] sound that the ears will hear and give to it, and it receives it from them. At any time when someone will ask it for that sound, it shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked it for that sound. It shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked.

Again, [the] faculty of smell in this same pattern; and that of taste is of the same [ty]pe. The touch by the han[ds ... / ... ] they shall bring it out in to [ ... / ... ] And whenever they will as[k it] for it, it shall bring it out and gi[ve it].

Indeed, watchmen are a[t] these doors guarding them, and bolts are fastened on the doors at the hands of the guards that guard them!

Just like watchmen guarding a camp with towers and watch-posts. As the holding rooms come out from there and the bolts are upon the gates, the guards control the bolts of the gates while they sit at the entrance [of] the watchposts. So, someone who belongs to their city as a fellow [cit]izen of them, belonging to their country, for him they shall open [the ga]te and receive him in by their will. However, the foreigner who comes they shall prevent, and leave him standing at the gate. They shall not yield to let him enter. Again, another one whom they will receive in, with great trouble before [they] take him in, you shall find many holding rooms [ ... / ... ] that [ ... ] there are a mass [ ... / ... ] them. The number of d[oor] bolts [ ... ] are [ ... ] Whenever someone will wish [ ... ] the bolts at his hand, opens the gate and brings out whoever is hidden within.

This body too is like the mighty camp. And the gates of the camp with their guards are like the orifices and organs of the body. Now, the orifices of the body are of sight, hearing and smell; and they that send out words. There are many sentinels and a mass of guards placed over the limbs of the body, guarding their orifices. And the faculty in it is the directive of the body, the queen of the entire camp; so that whenever it wishes it can open, or when it wishes it can shut.

However, if and wh[en] the Light Mind comes, it shall tu[rn i]n to the orifices of the body. The rulers, who [a]re the door gua[rds], shall h[in]der and restrain it from en[ter]ing. They shall bind the circu[it] of the body so that the foreign[er] can not enter it.

Nevertheless, the Light Mind by its wisdom and awe and diligence shall hum[ble] the guards who are set at the body's orifices. When it finishes humbling them it shall take fr[o]m them bolts to all the orifices of the body. Now, the orifices that had been opened before to the parades of Lust, to receive in the sights of pleasure, to transfer the [wor]ds of the practice of li[f]e's concerns, to receive in the t[astes] of the different foods and unclean meals together with the Sin therein; whoever stops receiving th[is] in, his heart and mind shall follow after.

So, now, bec[ause] the b[olt]s to the body of the righteous person are in the hands of the Light M[in]d within, he is open [to receive] in all that is pleasing to God. He is op[en to take] in by his eyes the visions of lov[e ... ] and righteousness. Yet, now, by the power of the Light Mind that has suffused him and lived in the body he has humbled Lust, the goddess of the body. The parades of Lust have become loathsome in the presence of the righteous person. How-ever, the aspects of divinity and righteousness and humility and scorned clothes are pleasing to him.

[S]imply, all orifices in similar fashion were opening at one time to wickedness, but now have opened to the good. Sin was ruling in the body this time, doing anything it liked, having none in command.

Yet, [n]ow, authority has been taken from the Sin in the org[ans] of the body. The Living Spirit has become lord over all its limbs. It has bound them with a chain of peace, and sealed them with the seal of truth. It has opened the orifices of the body to the good, and because of this the good enters them through the ears and the eyes. It dwells in the heart. The H[o]ly Spirit reigns. It does all that it pleases. It annuls [the] will of [S]in, the begin-ning and the end. [In contrast], the will of the [Holly Spir[it] comes about, and the purpose of the Light Mind.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Substantive cosmological question (Saklas/Adam-Eve/Image of the Ambassador) is internal to the mythic system; the surrounding Q&A attribution is later compilation frame.
- ¶2: System-internal causal chain (Sin/Matter → image → descent → tree/fruit → abortions) in impersonal explanatory voice; no citation or institutional pointing.
- ¶3: Mythic-cosmological narrative remains within the rulers’ world (light used as substance for constructing an image).
- ¶4: Repetitive, mechanical transmission-explanation (Sin/Matter as carrier of ‘seal’) characteristic of older teaching style; stays cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶5: Direct-address closure (“Behold, I have taught you…”) functions as didactic/applicative wrapping rather than impersonal substrate exposition.
- ¶6: Core is systematic microcosm teaching (limbs/senses as ‘storehouses’); only the speech-introduction is compilation frame.
- ¶7: Fragmentary continuation of the same correspondential ‘faculty/limbs’ exposition; no editorial address.
- ¶8: Highly systematic faculty-mechanics; ‘storehouses/repositories’ schema is classic Manichaean microcosm teaching.
- ¶9: Impersonal, procedural description of sensory retention/retrieval; no institutional or Gospel overlay.
- ¶10: Continuation of the same systematic sensory schema.
- ¶11: Systematic ‘faculty + repository’ mechanics; correspondential focus (taste/mark/memory) stays within the anthropological microcosm model.
- ¶12: Completes the five-sense ‘storage’ set in a uniform technical register.
- ¶13: Hierarchical governance teaching (heart ruling five faculties) in impersonal explanatory mode.
- ¶14: Pure simile used for structural correspondence (heart ↔ king/government) without contemporary institutional anchoring.
- ¶15: Second simile (cellarman/storage) continues the same ‘repository’ doctrine; internal pedagogic imagery.
- ¶16: Fragment closing the cellarman simile; no layer markers.
- ¶17: Continues the technical ‘faculty/storehouse’ mechanism (body as integrator).
- ¶18: Repetitive procedural language typical of the underlying teaching register.
- ¶19: Formulaic completion of the sensory set; no editorial present-time anchoring.
- ¶20: Core image introduces the ‘gates/guards’ correspondential model; attribution is later frame.
- ¶21: Extended structural simile (camp/gates/foreigners) serving the body-sense doctrine; despite mild ‘you shall find’ rhetoric, it remains explanatory rather than institutional.
- ¶22: Clear microcosm mapping (camp→body; gates→orifices; guards→powers; ruling faculty→queen). Attribution is a later dialogue wrapper.
- ¶23: Cosmic-soteriological mechanics (Light Mind vs rulers/guards) expressed as process; stays within the system.
- ¶24: Still mostly internal: Light Mind ‘takes bolts’ and reverses openings from Lust/Sin; some ‘practice of life’s concerns’ edges toward application but remains generic and embedded in the mechanism.
- ¶25: Core mechanism (Light Mind controlling bolts; Lust/Sin subdued) is substrate-like; the excised segment points outward to concrete elite clothing as temptation, suggesting later applicative expansion.
- ¶26: Entire paragraph applies the sensory doctrine to communal religious practice (psalms, prayers, hymns, charity) and moral instruction; this is cosmos→community ‘pointing at’ rather than system-internal exposition.
- ¶27: Brief doctrinal summary in cosmic-agent terms (Sin ruling the body); rhetorical ‘at one time/now’ but not institutionally anchored.
- ¶28: System-internal cosmological agents (Living Spirit, Light Mind, Sin) governing the microcosm; ‘Holy Spirit’ appears as an internalized reign-title here without NT citation formula, so treated as part of the doctrinal system rather than Gospel-overlay.
- ¶29: Direct exhortation with audience address (‘my brothers…’), imperatives (‘make yourselves secure… be watchful…’), and practical guarding instruction: late applicative layer consistent with chapter-end editorial fatigue.

## Chapter 57
### Concerning the Generation of Adam.

*The oldest teaching substrate here is the astrological-cosmological mechanism explaining changing human stature/longevity via a fivefold hierarchy of zodiacal time-authorities (year/month/day/hour/moment) (¶2–6). Dialogue attributions are later frame (¶1, ¶8, ¶12) and are stripped where they introduce otherwise core material (¶3, ¶10). The second half shows clear editorial fatigue: a homiletic/end-times application and moralizing critique of contemporary humans (¶7, ¶11) plus renewed Q&A framing (¶8–9).*

Now, no[te] that Adam is mighty in his stature, and great is his size. Even his years are many! He spent many years living in the world. And he is not the only one we have found to be mighty; but all the others who were begotten in his generation, many are their years too. Still, these people who are born today, their stature has been diminished in power; even their years are lessened. Also, why is the birth today, of [the]y who are born, altered compared with that of these first [ ... ] place to place? See, even the stars and signs of the zodiac continue in their positions. Why now have the age and the years of these last ones diminished, and he has also become smaller, compared to these ancient ones who belong to these first generations?

They increased, have turned, and diminished; because there are five types of authorities and leaders appointed in the sphere of zodiacal signs, and the heavens above it. They have names they are ca[lled] by: the first name is the year; the sec[on]d is the month; the third is the day; the fou[rth] is the hour; the fifth is the moment. Now, these five positions, and these five houses, exist in the sphere and the heavens. These places have five powers, and they are made masters over them. The master of the year exists; the master of the month exists; the master of the day exists; the master of the hour exists; the master of the moment exists. In turn one is above his companion; and in turn the one above is made the master over the one below him.

So, mankind is begotten, and [the ani]mals. They are born in these powers. And, thus, these powers have received authority since the beginning of creation till the end of the world. Now, in the beginning of the generations the powers who were masters over the years reigned. Therefore, because of this, the offspring born in their generations and their lifetime: their years were found to be greater. They attain a long life span in the generation of Adam, and [tha]t of Sethel his son, and that of the ones who came after him.

After they ceased to reign, namely the powers who are masters over the years; the powers who are masters over the months themselves reigned. Like the months, for they are less than the years; again this [is] also the case with the offspring w[ho were] born in them. Their life span is less [than the life span of the ones born] in the generation of the powers who master the years!

When the authority of the powers who master the months was completed; the powers of the days received authority after them. Therefore, like the days, for they are less than the months, this also is the case with the offspring who were born in them. Their life span is less than the life span of the ones born in the powers who are masters over the months. Again, even so the offspring who were born in the hours, and of the moments. Their conduit is less than that of the months and the days.

For the manner of these first generations is that they are richer in their light and purer in stature. Even their years are greater. Thus, again, even their offspring is much more than the offspring of this tim[e]. They were spending a longer time [ ... ] in [the w]omb of their mother. Afterwards, they [ ... ] a number in a single womb. There are times five were brought forth from a single womb, and times six were brought forth from [a] single womb! There are some lesser than this; again, some greater than this. Indeed, because of this the offspring of Eve multiplied, and of her children. The earth was filled by reason of them alone; because the offspring from the generation of the people of old, who they bore into the world, differ greatly from those of today. Their conception and their birth is not like latter births, brought forth nowadays from the woman's uterus.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue setup ("Once again", catechumen questioning "the enlightener"), with honorific address; functions as hagiographic/Q&A container rather than teaching substrate.
- ¶2: Introduces the problem in cosmological terms (stars/zodiac as stable determinants). Although it uses present-time comparison (“today/now”), it stays within a cosmic-astrological explanatory frame rather than institutional/pastoral application.
- ¶3: Core content is a systematic fivefold hierarchy (year/month/day/hour/moment) mapped to ‘masters’ within the zodiac/heavens—classic substrate-style degree structure. The removed opening is a clear compilation frame marker ("our enlightener" + encomium).
- ¶4: Continues the impersonal mechanism: beings are ‘born in these powers’; longevity varies by which temporal power rules. Remains cosmos→cosmos (heavens/zodiac/time-powers → human lifespan), without institutional targeting.
- ¶5: Iterative degree-descent logic (year→month) applied to lifespan; characteristic schematic exposition of the substrate.
- ¶6: Completes the fivefold cascade (months→days→hours→moments) in a purely structural way; no citation, exhortation, or institutional application.
- ¶7: Homiletic/application turn: direct address (“Do you see”), end-times imminence, and generalized ‘today’ moralizing. Even though it uses cosmological language (‘life and light in the world’), the paragraph functions as an exhortative lesson rather than the schematic substrate.
- ¶8: Renewed Q&A framing with entreaty and summary back to the teacher; strongly formulaic (‘Once again’, ‘I entreat you, my master!’).
- ¶9: A transition question in the catechumen’s voice (no mechanism given), serving the dialogue progression rather than preserving a self-standing schematic teaching unit.
- ¶10: Underlying content gives a causal account rooted in ‘light’ quantity and embodied condition (purer stature → greater fertility), still within the cosmological system; the stripped opening is explicit pedagogical framing with imperative address.
- ¶11: Ethical/moralizing denunciation of contemporary humans (‘wickedness’, ‘evil’, ‘ugly’, ‘death catches them’) without a new cosmological mechanism; fits later pastoral-application register.
- ¶12: Closing homage/thanksgiving formula to the teacher; classic hagiographic frame concluding the unit.
