## Chapter 58
### The Four Powers that grieve

*The chapter opens with a formulaic dialogue frame, then preserves a substantial core cosmological teaching (Mother of Life; First Man) describing divine grief within the Light–Darkness conflict. The second half shows strong editorial fatigue: it shifts to Mani’s self-presentation and the suffering of the institutional “holy church,” then to a Christianized eschatological warning (gehenna/repentance), suggesting later community expansion beyond the older substrate.*

The first who grieves is the first Mother of Life. She is sad because of her children who are set in affliction, for they were conjoined with the Darkness and the [poi]son. They have been bound with the entire ruling-power. For what was she grieving, [wa]tching over them taking these afflictions? Whenever she might [s]ee them and how suffering is brought upon them, she shall grieve and be unhappy on account of them.

[The s]econd one who is troubled of heart is the First Man, whose [great]ness and kingdom is established in the li[ght] ship [...]. And he too is troubled of heart because of his five sons who are set in the midst of danger. For any time he may see that they are given trouble, always he might hear the sound of their weeping as they cry out because of the blows and wounds of the enemy who assaults them daily; and as he sees them like this constantly, he shall g[ri]eve and mourn on account of his [s]o[n]s.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic opening (“Once again… speaks to his disciples”) is characteristic hagiographic/frame apparatus; the whole sentence functions as an editorial setup for the chapter’s theme.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological exposition: a named Light power (Mother of Life) and her children’s entanglement with Darkness/ruling-power; no institutional application, no citation, no audience address.
- ¶3: Core cosmology continues: First Man and his five sons (a systematic Manichaean structure) situated in cosmic geography (“light ship”); suffering described within the cosmic conflict rather than mapped onto church life.
- ¶4: Autobiographical Mani-first-person plus institutional identifiers (“holy church,” persecution by enemies, choosing the church) indicates later hagiographic/editorial self-positioning rather than the older impersonal substrate.
- ¶5: Soteriological warning framed in Christianized eschatological language (“gehenna,” repentance, reconciliation/peace-mercy). This reads as a later Christian overlay rather than a closed cosmological description of how the system works.

---

## Chapter 59
### The Chapter of the Elements that wept

*The chapter’s dominant layer is a coherent, systematic fourfold cosmological teaching (the elements/garments of the First Man weeping at four fixed cosmic moments). It is wrapped in a brief dialogue-frame opening and ends with an editorial/pastoral turn that applies the teaching to the apostolic mission and a moral admonition—consistent with the strong chapter-level “editorial fatigue” drift in the second half.*

The garments, the sons of the First Man, wept bitterly three times. Then they were silent. There is a fourth time of weeping, for they will weep at the end. And then their crying can stop, and they will never again weep from this time on.

Now, the first time of their weeping is when they had seen the Darkness, the enemy being about to burst out against them. They understood everything that would happen to them. They were aware of the one that would rise and come upon them, but by necessity they hastened to him and fought with him. They mastered him, they detained him. They bound him with their body and their limbs, they overcame his death and his fire and his Darkness.

[The] second time that they wept is [when the]ir father, the First Man, [ascend]ed from the [abyss] of Darkness and left them behind; that is the time when he stripped [them off] and left them below. Indeed, when th[ey] s[aw] how the First Man had risen out of the abyss and [l]eft them behind, and that they were abandoned in the enmit[y, th]ey wept and were sore of heart. Still, what could they do, wishing [ ... / ... ] their heart to every affliction. They [remained b]ehind and grew [s]ick under the pressure of the weighty burden of all the works, until the end time.

[The] third time that they wept is when the Living Spirit stripped off the three garments of activity, placing them beneath all things. He gave them a sign to lift their heads and rise to the heights from that place; and to pass and journey through all the earths until they should reach the land of tranquilli[t]y and peace, the place of rest prepared for them. So, when they had seen him, that he had separated himself from them, while they themselves remained behind after him and had not ascended; at that moment when they stayed behind these three garments wept. After their crying, they turned their [hear]t to all the heights that were concealed from them, and were of one mind. For [they would] soar up and come to the heights; and pass over all things until the time that was ordained for them all [ ... ] at the end of the worlds.

[The f]ourth time when they will weep is when the [S]tatue will be taken up on the last day, and they will weep [for] the souls of the liars and blasphemers; for they may give [ ... ] because their limbs have been severed [ ... ] of the Darkness. And also [th]ose souls, when the Statue will go up and th]ey are left alo[n]e, they will weep in that they will rema[in] behind in affliction for ever. For they wi[ll be cut off] and separated from the Last Statue. An[d it is] a necessity to take these souls who are ready for loss as retribution for the deeds that they have done. They go i[n to] this [darkne]ss and are bound with the Darkness; just as they desired it and loved it, and placed their treasure with it. At that very moment, when the Last Statue rises up, the[y wi]ll weep. And then they will scream out loud because they will be severed from the company of this great Statue. And they remain behind for ever. This great weeping [ ... ] is terrible, it occurs in front of the souls and [ ... ] who are ready for loss in accordance with their deeds. And they [will] weep with that crying, they will never be silent; because they will not attain rest from this time on.

So, they too are the garments of these elements. This fourth time of weeping is heart-rending for them. It is much worse for them than these other three times of weeping before. This fourth time the weeping is not for themselves. Rather they weep and are heartsore for these soul[s] who will be cut off from peace, because rest will not come about for them, to allow them to rest from torment for ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue-frame incipit (“Once again… disciples”), typical of the later Kephalaia editorial apparatus.
- ¶2: Impersonal, structural fourfold schema (counted weepings) stated as cosmological mechanism, without audience-address or institutional anchoring.
- ¶3: Cosmos→cosmos narration of conflict and binding (elements vs. Darkness/enemy), with no shift into contemporary institutions or exhortation.
- ¶4: Continues the numbered cosmic sequence (second weeping) tied to a specific cosmological event (First Man’s ascent/leaving garments in mixture). Emotive language remains internal to the mythic mechanics.
- ¶5: Numbered third moment, with systematic cosmological geography/process (“beneath all things… through all the earths… end of the worlds”). No institutional application.
- ¶6: Fourth and final cosmic moment anchored in eschatological machinery (Last Statue taken up; souls cut off/bound to Darkness). Though moral descriptors appear (“liars and blasphemers”), the paragraph remains within the system’s end-time mechanics rather than pointing to contemporary institutions.
- ¶7: Internal interpretive gloss on the same cosmic event (why the elements weep), still cosmos→cosmos (elements/garments ↔ souls’ final loss), without direct audience exhortation.
- ¶8: Application turn: explains apostolic/prohetic labour in the world (“apostles and the fathers… prophets…”) with soteriological exhortative logic (“so that the souls might be saved”), matching second-half pastoral drift; institutional/hagiographic vocabulary marks later layer.
- ¶9: Closing admonition (“wise person must… be attentive”) functions as moral application rather than cosmological exposition; likely appended as a concluding pastoral gravity-marker.

---

## Chapter 60
### Concerning the Four Fathers; what they are like.

*This chapter is overwhelmingly a systematic correspondential teaching (body-faculties mapped to cosmic beings/structures). The only clear late layer is the dialogue/lecture framing: the opening attribution to “the apostle…to his disciples” and the closing “Behold, I have shown to you…”. No Gospel citation formulae or institutional (church) applications appear; “Jesus the Splendour” functions as a cosmological being, not NT overlay.*

Just as the corporeal body has limbs that are hidden, so it also has others that are visible. They can be seen upon it, they can be grasped and receive blows and wounds, whilst others can neither be grasped nor receive blows.

So it is also with this entire greatness, as we resemble it. Some of it is hidden, whilst some of it is visible; some of it is far away from the blows and suffering, whilst some of it receives blows and wounds.

Now, thus the directive of the body that lives within, hidden in the storehouses inside the body and unseen. So it is also how the Father, the God of truth, exists; as he too is hidden in his kingdom, not displayed before this outer desolation.

Thus, too, the body has two eyes that are visible and seen, and are even the body's source of illumination; so there are also these two light ships that shine and are seen in the entire universe and the whole of creation. The light has never been hidden, just like the two eyes of the body.

The living soul too is like this; it is established in apparent silence. In its apparent silence it is grasped and receives blows from these five fleshes, which destroy (the soul) and strike it. It can be likened to the mystery of the corporeal body, as it can be grasped and mastered, can receive blows and wounds.

Also, thus this tongue that speaks, proclaims, reprimands and reveals about everything happening in this body, inside and outside, also the considerations and bodily knowledges. The tongue, which is outside, makes it manifest. It also proclaims about the honour and shame of the body. And it speaks, unveiling the pain of the body that is sick from its sores.

This is also what Jesus the Splendour is like, who shall be sent from the greatness. He manifests and reveals about everything, both the external and the internal, both what is above and what is below. Be it about the outer aeons that are hidden and not displayed, or about the ships of light that are seen: he is the one who unveils about them. He is also the one who reveals about their glory, and the greatness that dwells in them.

And regarding this beating and wounding of the living soul, Jesus is the one who reveals it. He also preaches about (the soul) and its peace. He reveals about its cleansing and healing.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core teaching begins immediately after the frame attribution: impersonal analogy of hidden/visible limbs as a structural setup for later cosmos↔body mappings.
- ¶2: Impersonal, system-internal analogy: ‘greatness’ (cosmic totality) mapped to hidden/visible states and susceptibility to ‘blows/wounds’ (suffering mechanics), without institutional application.
- ¶3: Direct correspondential mapping (body’s inner directive faculty ↔ the Father hidden in his kingdom). Both sides remain within cosmic/anthropic system; no audience-address or ecclesial identification.
- ¶4: Classic substrate pattern: organ-function correspondence (eyes/illumination) mapped to cosmic structures (two Light Ships in the universe). Impersonal, systematic exposition.
- ¶5: Cosmic suffering mechanism stated as process (soul receiving blows from the five fleshes) and mapped to bodily vulnerability; remains within the cosmological drama, not moral exhortation.
- ¶6: Still the correspondential substrate: functional description of the tongue as revealer/manifestor, preparing the next mapping to a cosmic revealer. No NT citation or pastoral address.
- ¶7: ‘Jesus the Splendour’ functions as a defined cosmological revealer (tongue-correspondence continued), not as a Gospel-cited Jesus. The mapping remains cosmos↔faculty↔cosmos.
- ¶8: Continuation of the same cosmic function: revelation of the soul’s wounding and its restoration. ‘Preaches’ appears as functional vocabulary (revealer role), without NT quotation or institutional discipline.
- ¶9: Second-person summative closure (‘Behold, I have shown to you…’) typical of editorial/homiletic framing that wraps the teaching rather than expounding new cosmological correspondences.

---

## Chapter 61
### Concerning the Garment of the Waters: how great is its Measure.

*This chapter is a hagiographic-historical riverbank scene used to introduce a dense cosmological teaching about the elemental garment of Water. The core substrate concentrates in ¶6–10 (with a small fragment in ¶8): a systematic account of First Man’s deployment of elemental garments against the rulers, including the sevenfold river-measure and the binding of the giants. The opening/closing (¶1–3, ¶12) are clearly editorial frame; ¶11 is a later devotional/exhortative wrap rather than impersonal system-teaching. One intra-Manichaean citation (“Great Gospel”) in ¶6 is a later compilation marker embedded inside otherwise core cosmology.*

How great is the power of the garment of the waters? How far shall [it extend]? That it should enter with the roar of its flood and fill this river Tigris in its vastness like the mighty sea! It has carried waves from river bank to bank, and run from wall to wall. The source from which all these waters burst forth, how great will it be? For they come and come each year at their appointed season!

[At] the time when the First Man assumed the elements, and stood [fi]rm against the first enmity that had come about in the darkness, he defied these great and powerful rulers. They are immeasurable, greater than the extent or size of thousands and ten thousands of parasangs. At the start, when he spread the fight against them, he gave them fire and wind; and afterwards he gave the garment of the waters to the rulers of death. So they, the rulers, drank in and consumed those waters. Many [waters] poured forth from the garment of waters [...] the rivers flow. The mouth of the rulers [...] those [...], the warriors of destruction [...] laboured and brought them forth.

There were seven (rivers), like the great river Tigris [that you] see, flowing in to the mouth of each one of them, as they shall not [...] to a single stomach, to their fill; because in turn the descent to its stomach shall burn them with its fire and heat. Those rulers were like the [...] of [...]; they were not emptied; rain and [...] their existent days.

The measure of that river was expanded seven times, as they flow [...] of the body of the [...] waters [...] now.

[...] is how great a struggle, since it has [...] these creatures of destruction, the mighty giants. Behold, they are entwined, bound, and [...] in every place. They are hidden, not revealed before the eyes of every flesh that exists on the land of death [...]. These rulers [...] revealed. The entire universe of flesh will be destroyed and dissolved [...]; all pass away [...]. They will be destroyed with their angry voice, which is mightier than the thunder.

Now, as this small stream is reckoned [...] before the vastness of these waters. For only one river from thousands of rivers of water, from the secrets that are not revealed, is the river Tigris upon the earth.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/biographical scene-setting tied to King Shapur and the title “apostle”; this is compilation frame, not impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Extended historical description of the Tigris flood with calendar detail (Phar-mouthi) and city terror; narrative staging rather than cosmological system-mapping.
- ¶3: Narrative continuation with institutional marker “leaders of his church” and the title “apostle”; this is editorial scene-setting around the teaching.
- ¶4: Core-style cosmological inquiry (measure/extent/source of an elemental ‘garment’) embedded in later dialogue narrative; the narrative voice and speaker tags are later compilation frame.
- ¶5: Dialogue-transition with the title “apostle” and audience-direction (“listen to what I am about to tell you”); functions as rhetorical bridge into the teaching rather than the impersonal substrate itself.
- ¶6: Predominantly core cosmological mechanics (First Man, elements, garments, immeasurable rulers, ingestion/production of waters). The explicit appeal to an authoritative written source (“Great Gospel”) is a later compilation marker embedded in the paragraph.
- ¶7: Systematic numerical structure (seven rivers) and internal cosmic physiology (mouth/stomach/descent/burning) stays within the cosmological system; no institutional or citation overlay.
- ¶8: Contains a compact, structural measure-teaching (“expanded seven times”) characteristic of the substrate, but embedded in later dialogue framing and expanded by exhortation.
- ¶9: Cosmic binding/entwinement of the giants and eschatological dissolution of the flesh-universe: still cosmos→cosmos description, not institution-pointing or NT citation.
- ¶10: Underlying teaching is a cosmological magnitude-comparison (earthly Tigris vs hidden ‘thousands of rivers’). The first-person ‘I…to you’ is a later speech-setting trace.
- ¶11: Devotional/exhortative wrap (“See… be amazed… Marvel… understand… how great is that god”), shifting from impersonal mechanics to audience-directed piety; not institutional rules, but still a later homiletic application layer rather than core substrate.
- ¶12: Closing disciples’ reverence/thanks formula (“We revere you, our master… opened our eyes…”): classic Kephalaia hagiographic frame closure.

---

## Chapter 62
### Concerning the Three Rocks

*This chapter is predominantly a compact cosmological-cosmic stability teaching (three "rocks" fixed into the universe). The second-half pastoral drift is localized in ¶5, where the third rock is redefined as a support of the "holy church"—an institutional application that breaks the cosmos→cosmos mapping and reads like an editorial extension of the otherwise systematic triad.*

Three great rocks, which exist, were appointed by the greatness. They were set in these outer worlds.

So, the first rock is the [Pillar] of Glory, the Perfect Man, which was summoned [by the] glorious Ambassador. He set it in this zone; [ ... he] brought forth its base (?) from the earth to heaven. It bore up under the [en]tire uni[verse], and became first of all the weight-carriers in [it]s powers. In its firmness it has been set upright, and has made fast every [t]hing above and below.

[The] second great and glorious rock is the new earth of light, which was set upon the earth of Darkness. It has squashed the fastenings of the five storehouses of Death. It has established firmness, with great firmness, over and beyond the earth of Darkness. It has flattened, crushed and fixed the root of Death.

[These] are the three great rocks, they that have come forth in great confidence, in three great and honoured places. They were appointed and distributed in three great places of these [self]same worlds. They were made fast according to the will of the greatness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue-frame incipit (hagiographic/editorial container): "Once again he speaks to his disciples:"
- ¶2: Impersonal, structural statement establishing a numbered triad fixed into cosmic geography; no audience address, citation, or institutional anchoring.
- ¶3: Systematic cosmological exposition: named cosmic beings (Perfect Man, Ambassador) and cosmic mechanics (bearing up the universe; fixing above/below) in an impersonal descriptive voice.
- ¶4: Cosmos→cosmos mapping continues: light/dark earths, storehouses, and Death as a cosmic principle. No pivot into church practice or exhortation.
- ¶5: Editorial application: the triad is extended/redirected into an institutional referent ("holy church"), shifting from cosmic emplacement to community anchoring. Even if "Jesus, the glorious one" can be a cosmological figure, the decisive marker is the cosmos→church identification.
- ¶6: Summative recapitulation in the same impersonal, distributive-cosmic register (three places in the worlds), without institutional or citation language.

---

## Chapter 63
### Concerning Love.

*Core teaching dominates the middle and end with a cosmological definition of Love (as the Father of Greatness) and Hate (as the first Death/Darkness-principle) operating in the time of mixture. The chapter opens with a standard dialogue/frame question. A major later graft applies the definition of love to the holy church and the apostle and then anchors it with an explicit written Gospel saying (NT overlay).*

Love is the Father of Greatness, who dwells in his glorious land. The entirety of the divine has been revealed [there]in! These two are a single living body, the Father and his love; because he has given himself for every thing, as he exists in his aeons. Indeed, due to this, they called the Father, the Lord of the totality, 'love'. For he gave the victory to his aeons and his limbs.

Hate is the first death that welled up. It revealed the land of darkness. This one called 'hate', because it has ruined all its perdition's offspring; it sinned even against a power foreign to it, and ruined it. Due to this they shall call it 'hated'; for it has caused destruction, both wounding in flesh, and what is not its own.

Today again it causes wounding and destruction in all flesh. It kills them all with diverse deaths; these that are displayed today in every thing, in every type. Even the Cross of Light, the living soul, likewise too it ruins and kills them; as it did at the start. Indeed, due to this have they called it 'hated', this one from the hated; the envy from the envy; the devil from the devil; the bad that came about from the bad.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame (“Once again he speaks to his disciples: What is…”) with lacunae; functions as the compiler’s container rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological-identificatory teaching: Love is defined as a hypostatized cosmic/divine reality (Father of Greatness) within the aeonic system, without institutional application or citation.
- ¶3: Editorial application of the concept to the holy church and the apostle (cosmos→institution), culminating in an explicit ‘it is written’ Gospel quotation (entry via NT), marking this whole paragraph as later Christian overlay despite some cosmic terms (e.g., Mind).
- ¶4: Core substance is a systematic cosmogonic definition of Hate as an originating Death/Darkness-principle acting within the cosmic drama; the only late element is the hagiographic/compilational speech-introduction formula.
- ¶5: Continues the same cosmological agency-description (Hate harming flesh and the Cross of Light/Living Soul) without pivoting to church discipline or exhortation. The repeated ‘today’ could reflect a mixture-era horizon, but it remains internal to the Light–Darkness system rather than pointing to contemporary institutions.

---

## Chapter 64
### [Concerning Adam]

*Apart from a brief formulaic dialogue opening (¶1), the chapter is a continuous, systematic teaching sequence: a threefold distinction in Adam followed by a sevenfold statement of the rulers’ intent in forming him. The diction stays cosmos→cosmos (image of the Ambassador, elements, zodiac/stars, rulers, ascent of light) without pivoting to institutional or contemporary-world application; thus nearly the entire chapter reads as teaching-core rather than later pastoral extension or NT overlay.*

Three great things that are distinct were revealed in Adam the first human. Indeed, due to this, he was found to be better and more outstanding than the conjoined powers that are in heaven and on earth.

The first: The image of the exalted one was placed upon him. The creators and moulders of his body sealed him after the shining form of the image that had been displayed to them above. Indeed, due to this, the image of Adam was found to be surpassingly beautiful beyond any conjoined power above and below.

The second thing: Adam was shaped in his structure through the light of the first-born of heaven and [earth ... ] the five sons. Indeed, due to this, his structure was found to differ from the structure of even the other creations; [sin]ce the form of all creations is different. As for Adam, the formation of his soul fits over the correct distribution of the elements. Therefore, he has intelligence surpassing that of the other creations and beasts.

The third are the teachings and counsels and the seal of all the powers above and below. The creators, who set him in order, gathered them and sealed them in him. Adam and his consort Eve became a dwelling and home for the signs of the zodiac and the stars; and the months, the days and the years. For the seal of the entire universe is stamped upon Adam. Indeed, due to this, heaven and earth moved because of him; trouble and disorder arose on his account between they that are good and they that are evil. So, the good induce him to life because of their image and shape placed upon him; while the [wi]cked were drawing him to death, so that they would have power through him and they would conceive the kingdom, and through him humiliate the entire universe.

Furthermore, understand that the rulers determined and formed Adam so that they would be masters through him and conceive the kingdom because of seven things.

The first: He became the enlightener in their creation; instead of the light of the image of the Ambassador who had displayed himself to them above. For they all desired the likeness of the image of the Ambassador. Their heart was drawn after it.

The second: So that they would scoop up the light through him and restrain and obstruct it from its ascent to the heights.

The third: So that because of the image of the Ambassador that is placed upon him the entirety of the divine that is above and below would be humiliated; on account of the likeness of the splendour, of the beauty of his superiority. For he is superior in his appearance to all creatures.

The fourth: He is a seal-ring and a new impression, so that all births who will be begotten might be brought forth after his seal.

The fifth: That might be displayed through him all the skil[ls] of the powers, they above and below, secret and manifest; and that they might determine through him the totality, and every thing be revealed in him.

The sixth: That there would come to be a great protection for them through the image of the exalted one that was placed upon him; for as the heavenly would [ ... ] even rulers; and no evil thing would be done to them on account of Adam.

The seventh: That he might become chief and king and master over all creatures; and they might conceive the kingdom through him.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic/dialogue opener (“Once again… disciples”), functioning as editorial frame rather than teaching content.
- ¶2: Impersonal, enumerative teaching voice introducing a structured triad; no citation formulae or institutional application.
- ¶3: Cosmological mechanics (creators/moulders; sealing after an image revealed ‘above’) presented as system description; stays within the mythic-cosmic architecture.
- ¶4: Systematic degree-language (first-born, five sons, elements) and explanatory causal chain; the brief evaluative conclusion (intelligence surpassing beasts) remains within cosmology/anthropogony, not pastoral exhortation.
- ¶5: Strong correspondential-cosmological mapping (Adam/Eve as ‘dwelling’ of zodiac/time; universe ‘stamped’ upon Adam). Conflict of good/evil is still narrated as cosmic mechanics, not moral instruction to an audience.
- ¶6: Bridge into a numbered heptad. Although it includes mild address (“understand”), it functions as internal structuring of the teaching sequence rather than an institutional application seam.
- ¶7: Cosmic agents and motives (rulers desiring the Ambassador’s image) stated in expository form; page marker removed per extraction rules.
- ¶8: Classic Manichaean light-mechanics (capture/obstruction/ascent) in purely cosmological terms.
- ¶9: Continues the heptad with cosmic valuation tied to ‘image/splendour’; no polemic against contemporary groups or institutions.
- ¶10: Correspondential register (seal-ring/impression) used as cosmic generative principle; impersonal schematic style.
- ¶11: Totalizing cosmological claim (all skills/powers; secret/manifest; totality revealed) consistent with systematic anthropogony teaching.
- ¶12: Stays within the rulers’ strategy and the power of the ‘image’; lacuna preserved as given.
- ¶13: Concludes the heptad in the same expository register; remains within the cosmic plan of the rulers rather than turning to community instruction or Gospel/NT citation.

---

## Chapter 65
### Concerning the Sun.

*The chapter contains a substantial enumerative teaching core (seven benefits of the Sun; seven evils of the Night; five archetypes; three further archetypes corresponding to the mystery of the Father). It is wrapped in a hagiographic dialogue frame and repeatedly interrupted by editorial application/polemic against “the sects” and by a late Christian overlay at the end (a ‘saviour’ saying; ‘children of the day/light’). The second-half application drift matches the fatigue signal: the cosmological-cosmological correspondences remain, but are increasingly turned into exhortation and sectarian contrast.*

it is the gate of life and the vessel of peace, to this great aeon of light [...].

Every day, would this great light-giver shine its light, it shall do seven good things for them. Shall they not see them?

The first good thing that it does for them is its light, which it shines upon them. It opens the eyes of all mankind. They see underfoot as they walk by it. It takes away the blindness of the night, which made them blind with its darkness.

The second: When (the Sun) is come to the world, with its coming it shall bring calm and peace to the entire world. With its coming it shall also take away from them all the fear and trembling, which their heart is filled with the whole night; for it shall take away the darkness present by its light, and sweep it out. Also, this is how it extinguishes fear by its calm.

The third is its vigil; because when (the Sun) comes to the world all sleeping mankind shall arise and awake. And the watchmen too shall cease from watching. The guards who sit at the gates of camps, they break vigil at the watchposts, because of its light that has shone upon them in the world.

The fourth: It shall nourish, and give strength, taste and scent, to the trees and fruits and vegetables, and all the herbs, and the flowers and grasses, which are upon the entire earth.

The fifth: When (the Sun) shines in all the world, the evil reptile and the sharp-toothed beasts that are filled of wickedness, they shall flee to hiding in their dens.

The sixth: When it shines its light, the wound of any person is eased from pain. Its light shall melt away the evil arts of every doer of wickedness.

The seventh: (The Sun) shall display and manifest to the world the sign of the splendour of the aeons of light, from which it has come out; it here in this world being their sign. It brings light to the whole of creation.

The first wickedness that the Night shall do in the world is the darkness, with which it shall fill the entire world; because when the sun sinks from the world and draws to it its rays, at that instant the shadow of the night shall spread out over the whole world. The eyes of the people fill with blackness. They neither see nor wonder with their eyes, because of the darkness of the night which shall fill the whole world.

The second evil thing that the Night shall do is its fear; because when its shadow looms forth, its fear and its trembling shall possess all of mankind. The entire world shall fill of the night.

The third: When the Night falls, all creatures take up revolt. They rebel in their heart, to do wickedness and perdition.

The fourth: The heaviness of sleep shall lay upon them, and they fall asleep; they slumber and doze, and they are like these corpses in the night.

The fifth is its ugliness; again, when the Night would fall, the form of the people shall be hidden in the darkness and the ugliness of the night. The beauty and the form of men and women shall not be displayed before one another, because of its darkness and its ugliness, which it shall spread out over all their forms.

The sixth occasion is when the Night would fall on the world. At that instant evil people shall come out to do wickedness; the adulterers, and the robbers and poisoners. The evil beasts shall come out, and all the reptiles that are filled with wickedness, from their dens. And they stalk in the night. Also, the demons and fiends find power to do wickedness in the night. Again, the blows and buffets and wounds and discharges shall torment people in the night; because in it the whole of evil shall find strength.

The seventh: The Night shall display the sign of the Darkness, its father. It has arisen out of its essence; because this night that came out of this first Darkness has been displayed in the world. Note this night, the shadow of this first Darkness, the one that is entwined and bound in all things above and below.

There are five archetypes occur in the Sun, it making them [...] as it finds the world.

The first is its light; for it illumines by its light the world, and all the creatures who exist in it.

The second is its beauty; because when it shines, it shall flood beauty and loveliness upon all creatures and everything.

The third is its peace; for, again, when the sun would shine on the world, all people shall receive affection through it, and give peace to one another.

The fourth is the life of the Living Soul, which it shall release from all the bonds and fetters of heaven and earth.

The fifth: It gives a strength to the elements; and also it gives a scent and a taste to the entire Cross of the Light.

Once again, there are another three archetypes made apparent by the sun, in respect of the mystery of the first greatness.

The first is the filling up of the disk of its ship; because its ship has filled up every season, and shall not wane at all, the way that the ship of the moon wanes. This continuous filling, by which it is filled, displays the mystery of the Father, the great greatness; from whom all the powers and the gods have come forth. He shall never wane, nor shall lack ever exist in him!

The second: (The sun's) light surpasses the light of all the stars, and all the lights that occur in the universe; corresponding to the mystery of the light of the Father; for his light surpasses and is greater than the light of his aeons.

The third: This sun is very high above all else. It surpasses the height of all the mountains and hills that exist upon the entire earth, corresponding to the mystery of the Father of Greatness; for he is high above all the household of his light-earth. Also, because the Father himself is high above all the heights and the mountains of his light-earth. He is 'filled up' in his image [all] the time and every hour from eternity to eternity!

The mystery of the first great light is the sun, which is this second light-giver, the one that comes daily to the world. It displays all the marks of its graces, displaying and signifying on its own behalf that it is good, out of the essence of the good. It came from the good Father and appeared in this world.

In contrast, this Night that is made of fear exists after the mystery of the Darkness. Behold too the mystery of its essence! It displays it in the world in all these wickednesses. It displays this mystery daily in the world,

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic opening scene with ‘the apostle’ seated among disciples; hagiographic narrative container rather than systematic teaching.
- ¶2: First clause is a cosmological definition (Sun as ‘gate/vessel’ within the aeon). The remainder pivots into editorial polemic anchored in ‘his law’ (Deuteronomy tradition) and worship-prohibition.
- ¶3: Application/polemic register (‘people are blind… even today’), addressing contemporary ignorance rather than expounding the cosmic system.
- ¶4: Enumerative structuring move (‘seven good things’) introducing a systematic list; largely impersonal despite rhetorical question.
- ¶5: Numbered, impersonal exposition of a fixed set (1/7); operates as day/night correspondence without institutional anchoring.
- ¶6: Numbered correspondential effect-list (2/7), describing mechanism (light dispels darkness/fear) rather than exhorting.
- ¶7: Continues the enumerative sequence (3/7). Uses human-nightwatch imagery as the natural expression of the day/night power shift, without institutional church markers.
- ¶8: Correspondential register (‘strength, taste and scent’) and impersonal listing (4/7).
- ¶9: Numbered list (5/7), presenting light’s dominion as a cosmic-natural deterrent.
- ¶10: Numbered list (6/7) with light’s therapeutic/exorcistic efficacy expressed as natural effect; still expository rather than imperative.
- ¶11: Closes the sevenfold sequence (7/7) with explicit cosmos→cosmos signification (Sun as ‘sign’ of aeons of light).
- ¶12: Sect-polemic and evaluative address (‘foolishness… sects of error’) applying the teaching to opponents.
- ¶13: First-person instructional pivot (‘hear also what I teach you’) functioning as audience management rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶14: Begins a mirrored sevenfold negative sequence (1/7) describing the Night’s operations in impersonal, structured form.
- ¶15: Numbered list (2/7) continuing the night/day correspondence as a mechanism.
- ¶16: Numbered list (3/7), still impersonal description of effects attributed to the Night as power.
- ¶17: Numbered list (4/7), correspondential description (sleep/death-likeness) rather than exhortation.
- ¶18: Numbered list (5/7), aesthetic correspondence (beauty/ugliness) tied to light/dark mechanics.
- ¶19: Numbered list (6/7). Though it names social evils, it remains descriptive (what the Night empowers), not a direct prohibition or church rule; possible later moral coloring but presented as part of the fixed sevenfold schema.
- ¶20: Closes the sevenfold night-series (7/7) with explicit dualist cosmology (Night as ‘sign’ of primal Darkness): cosmos→cosmos correspondence.
- ¶21: First-person summary plus sect-polemic (‘I have taught you… the sects have not seen…’)—application layer.
- ¶22: Underlying substrate is the fivefold archetype structure; the paragraph is introduced by a typical Kephalaia dialogue marker.
- ¶23: Fivefold schema (1/5) stated impersonally.
- ¶24: Fivefold schema (2/5), correspondential aesthetic register.
- ¶25: Fivefold schema (3/5) presented as effect of the Sun’s appearing; still expository rather than imperative.
- ¶26: Fivefold schema (4/5) with explicit Manichaean cosmological entity (Living Soul) and liberation mechanism.
- ¶27: Fivefold schema (5/5) using correspondential registers (‘strength… scent… taste’) tied to cosmic structures (‘elements’, ‘Cross of the Light’).
- ¶28: Second-person address (‘you yourselves see’) and rhetorical superlatives comparing to ‘beauties of mankind’ and ‘powers and guards… in the world’; functions as homiletic amplification rather than structural mapping.
- ¶29: Introduces a threefold schema linked to the ‘mystery of the first greatness’; structural teaching move.
- ¶30: Cosmos→cosmos correspondence: solar ‘ship’ fullness maps to the Father’s inexhaustibility; systematic analogy rather than citation or rule.
- ¶31: Explicit correspondence formula (‘corresponding to the mystery…’) kept within cosmic degrees (Sun/stars ↔ Father/aeons).
- ¶32: Third correspondence in a threefold set (height of Sun ↔ transcendence of Father of Greatness); strongly substrate-like, systematic and cosmological.
- ¶33: Direct address (‘O my beloved ones’) and sect-polemic; applies the mysteries to opponents rather than developing the system.
- ¶34: Cosmological identification of the Sun as ‘mystery/sign’ of the Good/Father; remains within cosmic ontology (origin/essence/sign).
- ¶35: Opens with a cosmos→cosmos contrast (Night ↔ Darkness) in the same ‘mystery/sign’ logic as the Sun section, then pivots into audience-facing polemic about ‘the sects’.
- ¶36: Explicit ‘the saviour’ allusion plus a Jesus saying (Thomas tradition); this material entered via Christian/Gospel overlay.
- ¶37: Apostolic speech tag plus NT/Pauline-style identity formula (‘children of the day/light… children of the night/darkness’), indicating Christian overlay rather than the oldest substrate.
- ¶38: Concluding beatitudes/blessings and soteriological exhortation (‘inherit life eternal!’) addressed to ‘my beloved ones’; functions as editorial homiletic close, even though it reiterates the two-essences doctrine.

## Chapter 66
### Concerning the Ambassador.

*The chapter opens and closes with hagiographic dialogue frame, while the central teaching (¶2–¶6) gives a systematic correspondential exposition: daily solar rising/setting as the cosmic ‘first’ and ‘last’ moments (First Man’s descent/battle; Last Statue’s ascent/consummation). A clear late pastoral/application graft appears in ¶7 (direct address + imperative exhortation to preach/repent).*

Two great mysteries are apparent in this shining sun, at its rising and setting. Now, at the moment when it rises and is visible in the universe, all creatures of flesh shall lift up their heads fr[om their sleep] towards the splendour of the sun, and open their doors. While at evening time, when the sun sinks from the universe, all people from every place and even all beasts shall go in to their dark hiding places. The population of the entire universe shuts their doors and dark night overcomes them. They go inside and stay within the dark night in their hiding places.

Very great is this mystery revealed by the sun! For when the sun rises and [be]come[s] visible at the beginning of the day all people open their doors, and all of humanity and every creature emerges onto the ear[th] to destroy this Light Cross that is established i[n] silence.

This pertains to the mystery of the First Man, because when he came out from the grea[t]ness he shone forth over all the children of darkness. At that mome[nt] all the children of darkness came forth from their storehouses, its powers and its armies, abandoning their caves and their black abyss. They destroyed the [outer] body of his five sons, who were swathed over hi[s b]o[dy, in t]hat first struggle.

Again, when the sun sinks from the universe and sets, and all people go in to their hiding places and houses and conceal themselves; this also pertains to the mystery of the end, as it presages the consummation of the universe. For, when all the light will be purified and redeemed i[n] the universe at the last, the collector of all things, the Last Statue, will gather in and sculpt it/self. It is the last hour of the day, the time when the Last Statue will go up to the aeo[n of] light. The enemy too, Death, will go in to bondage; to the prison of the souls of the de[ni]iers and blasphemers who loved the darkness. They will go in with it to bondage and the dark night overcomes th[em]; and thenceforth this name 'light' will not shine upon them.

Behold the two mysteries made visible daily in the sun, which is this splendrous light-giver.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue attribution (‘Once again he speaks to his disciples’) and attention-command used as a frame, not cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Impersonal correspondential setup: a twofold solar pattern (rising/setting) presented as ‘mysteries’ within the cosmic order of the universe/mixture, without institutional address or citation.
- ¶3: Cosmic mechanism in correspondential form: the daily emergence of embodied life is interpreted as assault on the Light Cross (a cosmological entity/process), staying within the Manichaean system rather than pointing to contemporary institutions.
- ¶4: Systematic cosmological narration keyed to a structural correspondence (‘this pertains to…’): First Man’s descent and the five sons in the first struggle—core mythic-cosmic teaching; no frame markers or exhortation.
- ¶5: Closes the twofold correspondence with eschatological cosmology (purification, Last Statue, Death/prison). The mapping remains cosmos→cosmos (solar setting → consummation), without institutional/pastoral application.
- ¶6: Brief impersonal recapitulation of the two-mystery solar correspondence; still expository, not hortatory or framed as dialogue.
- ¶7: Direct audience address (‘my brothers and loved ones’), imperatives (‘Take trouble… preach…’), repentance/time-to-do-good language—application to community practice, consistent with late pastoral graft (also matches the chapter’s second-half drift).
- ¶8: Narrative reaction formula (‘When his disciples heard…’) serving as hagiographic/dialogue frame closure rather than cosmological teaching.

## Chapter 68
### Concerning Fire.

*A compact five-fold, impersonal classification of the operations of fire. Aside from the brief dialogue/frame attribution in ¶1, the chapter is a self-contained systematic teaching sequence with no NT citation, no institutional application, and no cosmos→contemporary-world pointing.*

This fire, which burns and is apparent today in the world, it does five works.

The first work that it does is this: It incinerates and burns wood, and everything that shall be placed upon it.

The second: It separates four aspects of the wood, which are these: fire and light, ash and smoke.

The third: It gives a taste and an aroma to whatever will be cooked on it. It also gives heat and cheer to whoever will be close to it.

The fourth work that [it] does: All craftsmen shall work the utensils that they will fabricate in it.

The fifth: It shall display light, and the people see by [it].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The opening speech-attribution is a classic Kephalaia frame marker; the remaining sentence initiates a numbered five-fold teaching list in an impersonal, classificatory voice.
- ¶2: Numbered, impersonal expository style continuing the five-work structure; no citation or exhortation.
- ¶3: Systematic analytic division (four aspects) embedded inside the larger five-fold; characteristic of classificatory substrate teaching.
- ¶4: Continues the numbered list with sensory/affective effects (taste/aroma/heat/cheer) stated descriptively, not applied to church life or ethics.
- ¶5: Still within the same five-work classification; practical effect described without present-tense polemic or institutional address.
- ¶6: Concludes the five-fold sequence; closes naturally as a complete classificatory set, consistent with a core teaching unit.

---

## Chapter 69
### Concerning the Twelve Signs of the Zodiac and the Five Stars

*The chapter is largely systematic cosmological teaching (zodiac and planets derived from the five worlds of Darkness; distribution by fours; effects on creatures). The main secondary layer is the Kephalaia dialogue/lecture frame (apostle/disciple setup and first-person recap formulas). No NT citation overlay appears; no explicit church-institution seam is present.*

Whether these twelve signs of the zodiac that are fixed on the sphere, or these five stars that circle upon them; those and these are rulers, all of them, according to their essence. They are all enemies and rivals to each other ⟨one oppresses another, they threaten each other⟩. They plunder one another by the action of the great craftsman who has formed all the worlds. He has gathered [them] and bound them on the sphere above, which circles constantly. Now, while it circles and tumbles them, so they shall do everything that they desire, above and below, according to their pleasure. Nevertheless, a guardian is over them, a debt-collector above them; compelling, extorting, and taking what is theirs away from them!

Now, this is how it can rightly be understood. They were [s]eized from the five worlds of darkness, and bound on the sphere. Two zodiacal signs were taken per world. Gemini and Sagittarius belong to the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aries and Leo belong to the world of fire. Taurus, Aquarius and Libra belong to the world of wind. Cancer and Virgo and [P]isces belong to the world of water. Capricorn and Scorpio belong to the world of darkness. These are the twelve rulers of depravity, the ones that wicked[ness] shall not [ ... ] For they cause all the evil and [ ... in the wo]rld, whether in tree or in flesh.

And understand also about these five stars, the leaders: from where were they formed? The star of Zeus (Jupiter) was generated from the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aphrodite (Venus) came about from the world of fire. And then Ares (Mars) belongs to the world of wind. Hermes (Mercury) belongs to the world of water. Cronos (Saturn) belongs to the world [of] darkness.

And also, the two ascendants are counted to fire and desire, the dry and the moist. They are the father and the mother of all these.

And also, the sun and the moon, which are counted in with them as a rule so that they would reign over them. You will discover they subjugate them at every moment.

These seven we have named, the five stars and the two ascendants: They are the doers of wickedness that do every wicked and bad act in every land, the whole of it, above and below, in every creature, in the dry and the moist, in tree and flesh.

[They] are distributed, appointed on four sides, three per angle in these four places. And they are fixed to this revolving sphere. Aries, Leo and Sagittarius, they three belong to a single side. In contrast, Taurus, Capricorn and Virgo, these other three belong to another side. And then, Gemini, Libra and Aquarius belong to another side. Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer belong to another corner. Now, they are placed like this, appointed to these four parts, and distributed on the sphere.

So, when the side of Aries, Leo and Sagittarius will be plundered by the guardian who is over it, who extorts from it and the leaders who move upon it; at that instant shall affliction strike all the four-footed creatures below. However, when [the side] of Taurus, Virgo and Capricorn will be plundered; [affliction] shall at once befall the herbs, together with the vegetables and all the fruits of the trees. Yet again, when the side of Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer will be plundered; scarcity shall befall the waters upon the earth, and drought be from place to place. Conversely, should the side of Gemini, Libra and Aquarius be plundered; deformity and stuntedness shall befall the form of mankind from place to place.

They were fashioned from the five worlds of darkness, and bound on the sphere.

They too came about from the five worlds of the land of darkness.

They are set to the mystery of fire and desire. They are the dry and the moist, the father and the mother.

They are strangers to them. Still, because of the constraint of the arrangement, in that the thing receives light, and as they belong and plunder them; because of this they were counted in with them in relation to the calculation of the number. For, the sun and the moon are from out of the greatness, not belonging to the stars and the signs of the zodiac.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Classic Kephalaia question frame: ‘apostle’ among the congregation, disciple inquiry, entreaty formula, and editorial setup; functions as container rather than teaching.
- ¶2: Core cosmological mechanism (binding rulers on the revolving sphere; guardian/debt-collector constraint) embedded in a dialogue-attribution wrapper.
- ¶3: Systematic pentadic-to-dodecad mapping (two signs per world, with one triad adjustment) stays entirely within the cosmic architecture—typical substrate correspondential-cosmological exposition.
- ¶4: Pentadic provenance list (planet-by-planet from the five dark worlds) is a clean cosmos→cosmos distribution teaching.
- ¶5: Concise degree/correspondence statement (ascendants ↔ fire/desire ↔ dry/moist ↔ father/mother) remains internal to the cosmological register.
- ¶6: The underlying line is cosmological reconciliation (sun/moon counted among rulers to dominate/subjugate them), but a scribal/questioning gloss intrudes mid-sentence.
- ¶7: Global causal attribution (astral rulers’ operations across realms and creaturely degrees) remains descriptive rather than exhortative or institutional.
- ¶8: Core is a structured fourfold distribution (triplicity-by-corner) within the cosmic machine; the removed opener is a rhetorical delivery marker characteristic of the compiled discourse setting.
- ¶9: Pure correspondential causality: astral-side events ↔ terrestrial consequences (animals/plants/waters/humans). No pivot to contemporary institutions; internally systemic.
- ¶10: The factual cosmological claim is core-like, but the self-referential summation is a compiled-lecture rhetorical seam rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶11: Same pattern as ¶10: brief core proposition preserved, embedded in a didactic recap typical of the redactional presentation.
- ¶12: Underlying mapping (ascendants ↔ fire/desire ↔ dry/moist ↔ father/mother) is internal-cosmic; opener is rhetorical redaction.
- ¶13: Core cosmological clarification: sun/moon are of ‘greatness’ yet numerically counted with rulers by ‘constraint of the arrangement’—an internal-system reconciliation; the opener is compiled discourse framing.

## Chapter 70
### Concerning the Body: It was constructed after the Pattern of the Universe.

*Core layer is dominant: systematic microcosm–macrocosm correspondences (body↔cosmic geography; five watch-stations; four bodily worlds with 7 rulers each; zodiacal body maps; demonic rulers causing illness). Clear later strata: (a) hagiographic dialogue frame (¶1, ¶30, ¶37); (b) an ethical/self-mastery application section explicitly keyed to the Elect and moral subduing (¶18–22), plus minor didactic asides inside otherwise core material (several MIXED cuts). No NT/Gospel overlay appears here.*

This whole universe, above and below, reflects the pattern of the human body; as the formation of this body of flesh accords to the pattern of the universe.

Its head is like the first-fruits of the five garments. And, from its neck down to the site of its heart, it resembles the pattern of the ten firmaments. And again, the heart accords to the wheel of the rotating sphere. And, from its heart down to its intestines (?), is like this atmosphere that extends from the sphere down to the earth. The [male] part of the body corresponds to this great earth. And also, from its intestines (?) down to its loins is like the three earths. And also, its shinbones to the pattern of the space in which the Porter stands. Its footsoles to the great earth upon which the Porter is stood; and the four fastenings that are under his feet. Its liver to the vessel of fire. Its flesh to the vessel of darkness. Its blood to the vessel of water.

Now, this is how the small body corresponds to the macro-cosmos in its firmaments, in its orderings, in its mountains, its walls and its vessels.

There exist five great camps in this great outer zone, of which the five sons of the Living Spirit are masters.

So, in the first watch-station above, of which the Keeper of Splendour is master, on the battlement of all the works, is found his authority over the three heavens. Then the ones below him [ ... ... ] the great King of Honour.

The second watch, of which the great King of Honour is master, his own authority (extends) over the seven firmaments beneath him.

The third watch, of which the Adamant [is master, his authority] reigns from the firmament down below to the [earth]. [And he] invigorates, according to his authority, the sphere and the worlds of the atmosphere; and the other four worlds that are appointed on this earth.

The fourth watch, over which the King of Glory has power, is the three wheels. And his authority lies over the three earths upon the head of the Porter.

The fifth watch is the watch over which the Porter is authoritative. Again, he is master, according to his authority, over this great earth he stands upon, and the four fastenings under his feet.

Affliction has come about in these five watches from time to time.

So, in the watch of the Keeper of Splendour, Sin desired to spurt up towards the image of the Ambassador. Yet, they barred it from that place, and it fell back down in shame.

Again, an earthquake and treachery occurred in the watch of the great King of Honour, which the watchers who had arisen at the time and had [ ... ] they who were sent came down, until they were humbled.

Also, in the watch of the Adamant, the abortions came to the earth and fashioned [the] figure of flesh.

Again, in the watch of the King of Glory, a commotion happened in these three earths above the Porter. The pathway was obstructed for the ascent of these three wheels; that of wind, that of water, and that of fire.

Also, in the watch of the Porter, the fastenings beneath were laid bare and strained their chains. A great earthquake befell that place.

[Like] these five watch-stations, which exist in this great [ ... these five] camps: this is also the case with this body. There are another five camps there, and the Light Mind is watching over them, and the new man who is with him.

Also, the teaching of wisdom, which circulates in that body: to the likeness of the Virgin of Light, who goes up and comes down in this zone, above and below.

Also, love and joy, faith and truth: they are to the likeness of these two light ships. For, the living soul should go up in them and become free through them; and it ascends from the abysses below and arrives at the heights above.

There exist four worlds in this body of the flesh, and there exist countless times seven rulers in the body of the four worlds!

So, the first world is from its neck up; and also the seven rulers who are in this upper world are these: the two organs of sight, and the two of hearing, and the two of smell. The other one is the mouth, which is the organ of taste.

The second world is from its neck down to its entire upper torso. And also, the seven rulers who are in this second world are: its two arms that accord to the pattern of its two ears, which occur in its first world above; and its two breasts that are to the pattern of its organs of smell; and the two eyes of its heart; and the gullet of the stomach between the two breasts, which in the chest is the pattern of the mouth.

Once more, there exist seven rulers in the third world of the body, which are these: the fat, the lung, the spleen, the liver, the gall, and its two kidneys.

Also, the fourth world below. There are another seven there of this same kind: its two buttocks (?), and its two testicles, and its two loins; and the member out of which the seed comes, that begets all the forms.

Aries and Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius and Pisces; these are the twelve signs of the zodiac that occur in the worlds above, on the sphere. They are interlocked and bound on this turning wheel, the one in which they are [ ... ], fixed, and planted. And, regarding all these twelve signs of the zodiac, this is how they were appointed and made leaders [ ... ] on the sphere beneath the heaven that is the wheel of the stars.

Again, they occur like this, these that we have recited, one after another in this body. They are counted by order and number from the head to the feet. Its head is Aries. Its neck and its shoulders are Taurus. Its two arms Gemini. Its upper torso is Cancer. Its stomach is Leo. Its belly is Virgo. The vertical spine and its intestines (?) are Libra. Its genitals Scorpio. Its loins are Sagittarius. Its knees are Capricorn. Its shinbones are Aquarius. The soles of its feet are Pisces.

Behold, these are also distributed one after another. They exist in this body, as if turned to the side and bent to the pattern of the twelve signs of the zodiac. They too are thus, arranged one against another, head to tail, as they occur on the wheel [ ... ] up.

Also, they are counted by order and number of the body and all its members. Because from its head down to its hip shall count six to its left, and another six to its right.

The six that are down its right are these that I will recount: the right temple is Aries; its right shoulder is Taurus; its right arm is Gemini; its right rib-cage is Cancer; its stomach is Leo; the right side of its reproductive organs are Virgo.

Conversely, the other six about which we have spoken, which are to its left, in contrast they come from its hip to its head: its left [ ... ] is Libra; its left rib-cage is Scorpio; its left breast and left kidney is Sagittarius; its [left] elbow is Capricorn; its left shoulder is Aquarius; its left temple is Pisces. This is how the creator of the body himself has appointed them. He has [set] them in order and arranged them, one against its companion, head to tail.

There exist many powers in this body. They are the house-dwellers who are made the leaders in it. There are eight hundred and forty times ten thousand rulers made chiefs in the human body! They are distributed and set firm, quartered according to house. The count and number of their houses is two hundred and ten times ten thousand.

When all these rulers come creeping and moving within the body, they will meet one another; and they shall beset and destroy one another, and [ ... ] in them there. When [ ... t/il]] they shall erupt from the body of the person who [will] die; and make putrid boils and sores and burning wounds in the body. Either they might have made him sick within; or else they might come forth upon his outer side. Those wounds shall first be squeezed and discharge their liquid, and empty out their pus, until they are squeezed [ ... ... ] and the suppuration forced out from the wound. Afterwards, their scars shall close over; and they are healed and obtain relief from the places that came out upon them.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic scene-setting with ‘apostle… among the church… congregation… disciples’: hagiographic compilation frame.
- ¶2: Impersonal microcosm–macrocosm axiom; cosmos→cosmos correspondence stated as structure, not exhortation.
- ¶3: Classic substrate-style correspondential mapping: body parts ↔ cosmic strata/structures, including numbered cosmography (five garments, ten firmaments, three earths) and named cosmic functionaries (Porter).
- ¶4: Summary sentence continues the core correspondential register, but ends with a didactic second-person aside typical of later instructional delivery.
- ¶5: Core cosmological architecture (five camps; five sons of the Living Spirit) introduced, but prefaced by a later didactic formula.
- ¶6: Systematic enumeration of cosmic watch-stations with named beings and jurisdictional degrees (three heavens): impersonal structural cosmology.
- ¶7: Numbered degree structure (second watch; seven firmaments) in impersonal cosmological voice.
- ¶8: Cosmic governance/process description (invigorates sphere/atmosphere; four worlds) within system; no institutional pointing.
- ¶9: Enumerative cosmology: watch→three wheels; authority over three earths; named cosmic station (Porter).
- ¶10: Completes fivefold watch schema; purely internal cosmic mechanics (Porter; earth; four fastenings).
- ¶11: Programmatic transition into cosmic disturbance narratives; still within cosmic system (no audience exhortation/institutional mapping).
- ¶12: Cosmic conflict episode among beings/powers; ‘Sin’ functions as an agent within cosmological drama.
- ¶13: Internal mythic event sequence (watchers; humbling) tied to the five-watch cosmography.
- ¶14: Mythic cosmogonic/anthropogonic event stated as process; remains within the cosmic narrative system.
- ¶15: Degree mechanics (three wheels: wind/water/fire) obstructed—systemic cosmological process language.
- ¶16: Continues internal cosmological disturbance motif tied to cosmic architecture (fastenings/chains).
- ¶17: Underlying correspondential move (five cosmic camps ↔ five in the body; Light Mind/new man as inner cosmic agents) looks substrate-like, but the Elect-marking is editorial institutional pointing.
- ¶18: Explicit ethical application keyed to ‘The elect person’ and self-discipline (‘control… guide… towards the good’): cosmos→community/moral practice.
- ¶19: ‘Whoever will… subdue’ exhortational self-mastery; uses cosmic figure as moral analogue (application layer).
- ¶20: Moral control of lust/genitals framed as likeness of Adamant; application voice (‘Whoever will…’).
- ¶21: Ethical-ascetic discipline of food/fire within; second-person-indefinite exhortation (‘Whoever will… purify’).
- ¶22: Moral binding ‘with the chain of peace’ in the feet; analogy to Porter used for instruction rather than cosmological description.
- ¶23: Correspondence remains internal (bodily ‘circulation’ ↔ Virgin of Light’s cosmic motion); no institutional address.
- ¶24: Core soteriological mechanics (light ships as ascent vehicles) are present, but the excised clause shows a later ethical/applicatory insertion.
- ¶25: Numbered structural schema (four worlds; seven rulers) is substrate-like; prefaced by later didactic address.
- ¶26: Systematic microcosmic ‘world’ division with septenary organ-rulers; impersonal enumerative style.
- ¶27: Pure correspondential mapping (arm↔ear; breast↔smell; etc.) within a numbered bodily-cosmological schema.
- ¶28: Septenary ‘rulers’ list in the third bodily world; impersonal structural enumeration.
- ¶29: Completes fourfold bodily-world schema with sevenfold rulers; cosmo-biological generative language stays within system.
- ¶30: Dialogue attribution (‘enlightener… hearers’) is compilation frame; rule forbids it in core_text.
- ¶31: Underlying astral cosmography is core-like (twelve signs; wheel of stars), but it trails into an audience-directed instructional aside.
- ¶32: Direct zodiac→body vertical correspondence list: a hallmark correspondential substrate schema.
- ¶33: Second schema description (side/bent; head-to-tail) continues technical correspondential exposition; ‘Behold’ is rhetorical but not institutional.
- ¶34: Core content is a numeric/positional rule (six left/six right), but it is embedded in a later ‘we have proclaimed’ reporting register.
- ¶35: Right-side zodiac mapping continues the technical body–zodiac correspondence list (still internal, schematic).
- ¶36: Completes the bilateral zodiac schema; closes with an internal-system ‘creator of the body’ ordering statement, not church/institutional application.
- ¶37: Brief speech-transition formula (‘Once more he speaks’) is hagiographic framing.
- ¶38: Underlying content is a technical numerological-demonic schema (rulers; houses; distribution) typical of cosmological catalogues, but introduced by later exhortative scaffolding.
- ¶39: Demonic-physiological mechanics described as process (rulers moving/erupting/causing sores) without institutional exhortation: an internal-system cosmology of illness.

## Chapter 71
### Concerning the Gathering in of the [E]lements.

*This chapter is overwhelmingly the systematic eschatological mechanics of ascent: a chained sequence of gatherings from element to element and then through named salvific hypostases (Answer/Summons/Mind/Pillar/First Man/Ambassador) to the Aeons. Only the opening dialogue attribution is a later Kephalaia frame wrapper; the teaching itself stays cosmos→cosmos throughout with no institutional or NT overlay.*

In what way shall the elements be gathered in, from one to another?

The light shall be gathered in to the fire, and the fire itself gathered in to the water, and the water gathered in to the wind, and the wind gathered to the air, and the air gathered to the Answer, and the Answer gathered to the Summons. Also, the Summons to the purified Mind, which is the Intellectual. The Intellectual to the Pillar of Glory, [the Pillar of Glory to the First Man, the Man to the Ambassador, the Ambassador to] the aeons of greatness.

They are gathered in like this; but, they will be gathered on one occasion only, and go up above to the place of rest for ever and ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Contains a clear Kephalaia frame marker ("the enlightener speaks"), but the remaining question is a structural prompt for the cosmological sequence and plausibly preserves the teaching topic.
- ¶2: Impersonal, mechanistic chain-of-ascent teaching with a strict cosmos→cosmos mapping (elements → hypostases → cosmic stations). No citation, exhortation, or institutional application; named Manichaean cosmic beings appear in systematic exposition.
- ¶3: A brief concluding eschatological closure in the same impersonal explanatory voice; although the phrase "for ever and ever" is a liturgical idiom, the content remains internal to the cosmological-rest schema and does not pivot to pastoral application.

---

## Chapter 72
### Concerning the worn and torn apart Garments, or the tattered Clothes, or the Elements, the Cross, and the rest too.

*The chapter has a clear hagiographic/Q&A frame (¶1–6) introducing a technical glossary, followed by a sustained core teaching block (¶7–15) defining successive names of the Light-soul/power by mapping each name to a specific cosmological function/state (Pillar of Glory, rulers/firmaments, three earths, Porter, three garments of wind/fire/water, bound Light in dry/moist, summons/obedience, vegetative life as ‘slain soul’, and final reunification). Despite the chapter-level ‘fatigue’ signal, the latter paragraphs remain cosmological/eschatological rather than institutional; the drop appears to be a vocabulary-list artifact rather than a genuine pastoral graft here.*

“It is proper for you to understand this. The garments that are named ‘great garment’ are the five intellectuals that make perfect the body of the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. They were purified by the coming of the Ambassador.

“In contrast, the ones named ‘rag’: the rags are the devoured enlightening power. It is compounded and entangled in the body of the universe in the rulers who are above, who exist in all the firmaments. Also, its shine is that which lights up and is displayed in the stars of the chain and the powers of the course. And likewise, these rags that are entangled in these three earths below, above the head of the Porter [...] on the earth that is under the Porter. These are named the ‘rags’, because [...] set [...]

“However, the ‘great worn and torn apart garments’, which we have proclaimed, is this entangled living power. It is reaped, cut, and devoured. It is overpowered by the five worlds of the flesh, which creep as they move from place to place.

“On the other hand, [these] we have termed the ‘images and the great tattered clothes’ [...] are the three great garments of the wind, the fire, and the water. These the Living Spirit cloaked upon his body. With them he set in order things in the universe. With them also he emptied the vessels. He swept them out and laid them bare in front of the Porter at the foundation of all things.

“Conversely, ‘[the ele]ments’, we have called them this name, are this power that remains in all things below. It rises up in the wombs of all the earths, gathering together, and pouring forth upon all things.

“And then, the ‘[Light] Cross’ [we have termed] this bound ‘light power’. It [...] all [...] upon the earth, in the dry and the moist.

“Also, the one named the ‘counsel of life’ is the summons and the obedience. It moves in all these powers that we have named. It awakens them, gives them ease, and drives them to their movement.

“The one named ‘the soul that is slain’, which is killed, oppressed and murdered; it is this power of fruit, vegetables and seeds. They shall be threshed, plucked and cut down; and they give nourishment to the worlds of the flesh. Further: the wood should it be dry, and the garment should it become ragged, will perish. Again, it is [a pa]rt of this entire soul that is slain and killed; since also even this whole part of it they kill [...]

“For all these names [...] are a single [...] since the beginning, it [...] as it enlightens; but they separated into all these parts in this first contest. They became set in all these altered forms, and these many names. Of course, if now all these varieties are laid bare, and stripped of all these appearances, and parted from all these names; they will gather together and make a single form, and a single name, unaltered and unchangeable for ever in the land of their first essence, from which they were sent forth against the enemy.”

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic sitting/speaking scene (‘apostle… congregation… disciples’) is the Kephalaia dialogue frame.
- ¶2: Meta-textual ‘lesson… written in the scriptures’ and interrogative catalogue presented as a scripted Q&A prompt; functions as editorial introduction rather than expository cosmological mechanism.
- ¶3: Explicit appeal to ‘written and sealed… by a multitude of scriptures’ plus direct address (‘I ask you…’) continues the editorial Q&A scaffolding anchored in a scriptural archive.
- ¶4: Pure dialogue marker (‘His disciples say to him’)—container, not teaching.
- ¶5: Hagiographic praise/entreaty (‘source/fount of all wisdoms… we entreat you’) is stereotyped disciples’ frame speech.
- ¶6: Dialogue attribution with ‘apostle’ (and page marker) is an explicit compilation-frame seam; per rules, never part of substrate.
- ¶7: Systematic internal mapping of a technical name (‘great garment’) to a defined cosmic structure (five intellectuals → body of the Pillar of Glory/Perfect Man) and a cosmological process (purified by the Ambassador).
- ¶8: Cosmos→cosmos definition: ‘rag’ mapped to the devoured Light-power distributed through rulers/firmaments, stellar manifestations, and the three earths around the Porter; no institutional or exhortatory application.
- ¶9: Mechanistic description of how the entangled Living power is processed/consumed by the five worlds of Flesh—internal cosmological dynamics stated as ‘what it is/undergoes’.
- ¶10: Structured correspondential definition within the cosmic system (name → triad of garments → elements wind/fire/water → actions of the Living Spirit → cosmic ordering and emptying of vessels before the Porter).
- ¶11: Definition of ‘elements’ as the remaining Light-power below, described as an immanent process in the earths (‘wombs… gathering… pouring forth’); impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶12: Cosmic technical term (‘Light Cross’) defined as a state of bound Light-power diffused through terrestrial domains (dry/moist); not a Gospel citation despite ‘Cross’ terminology.
- ¶13: Internal functional mapping: ‘counsel of life’ = summons/obedience operating as an activating principle within the previously named powers.
- ¶14: Cosmos-in-nature register: ‘slain soul’ mapped to Light-power in vegetation/agricultural processing that feeds the worlds of Flesh; still descriptive (what happens to the power) rather than moral exhortation.
- ¶15: Core synthetic closure: eschatological reunification of the one Light-power behind multiple functional names/forms; remains within the cosmic drama (‘first contest… sent forth against the enemy’), not church discipline.
