## Chapter 73
### Concerning the Envy of Matter

*Core substrate is concentrated in ¶2–4 (and a definitional remnant in ¶1 and ¶8): Matter/Envy as a primordial Dark nature attacking key cosmic-historical figures (First Man; Image of the Ambassador; Adam). From ¶5 onward the chapter shifts into Christian/biographical/institutional application (Christ/cross; Mani and his church; catechumens; exhortation), matching the strong chapter-level editorial fatigue signal (pastoral drift in the second half).*

[The] entirety of envy is this first nature that occu[red] in the worlds of darkness.

In this envy that [ ... ] in it, the First Man [ ... ] its poiso[n ... ] its evil-doing. His garment[s] were [ ... ] it cut them and drew them; [ ... ]

It envied the first image of the Ambassador, which he displayed to it in the heights abov[e], i[n] his form that it saw. It established the seal and the image of flesh, and put the impression of t[h]at image on flesh, its very own birth.

[The th]ird time it envied Adam, the fir[st hum]an. It seized a power from him, and bore it forth from a [rib of] of Adam's [flesh].

This is the desire [of Mat]ter, the thought of death. This is the envy of its wi[cke]dness, that it casts upon the entire kindred of life from the very beginning

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula introduces a compact cosmological definition (envy as primordial Dark nature). Teaching voice becomes impersonal once the attribution is removed.
- ¶2: Cosmos→cosmos conflict report: Matter/Envy acting within the mythic system against the First Man (no institutional or audience address; no citation formulas).
- ¶3: System-internal mythic mechanism (Ambassador’s image; sealing/imprinting into flesh) stated as cosmological causation, not moral exhortation or church application.
- ¶4: Still written as internal myth-history (Matter seizing a power and generating from Adam’s flesh). The ‘rib’ motif suggests contact with Genesis tradition, but here it functions as a cosmological etiological detail rather than an explicit scriptural citation.
- ¶5: Explicit NT/Christian overlay markers: ‘blessed Christ’, ‘apostles’, ‘wood of the cross’—material entering via Christian narrative vocabulary rather than the impersonal cosmological register.
- ¶6: Biographical Mani-attribution and institutional anchoring (‘I… sent now’, ‘last generation’, ‘my… church’, ‘assembly of my catechumens’) shift the chapter into hagiographic/institutional application, consistent with editorial fatigue in the second half.
- ¶7: Application to communal life (sicknesses, rumors, accusations, hatred ‘among mankind’, attacks on ‘the teacher’ and ‘the righteous elect person’) maps the envy motif onto contemporary/social experience rather than staying within cosmic architecture.
- ¶8: Contains a brief impersonal definitional kernel (Matter’s desire = thought of death; envy cast upon the kindred of life) embedded within pastoral warning about negligence and an explicit ‘my time/my church’ anchor.
- ¶9: Pure exhortation/application voice (‘appropriate… wise person… steadfast… faith… hope’) with no cosmos→cosmos mapping; functions as moral admonition closing.

---

## Chapter 74
### Concerning the living Fire: It is present in Eight Places.

*Dominantly CORE: a systematic enumerative teaching locating the living fire in eight cosmic stations (Light Land, Father, First Man/Perfect Man, the two light ships, Living Spirit’s descent gear, new earth foundations, and an eschatological manifestation). Only the opening dialogue attribution (“the apostle speaks”) is later FRAME wrapping; the rest stays cosmos→cosmos with no institutional or NT overlay.*

The [holy] living f[ire] is present i[n] eight places.

The first place wherein it is, is this [light] land [where it is present and] exists. It is established beneath [it ... ut]terly beyond the totality. It is the foundation and ba]sis of that great earth, and the consideratio[n th]at is beneath them all.

The second place wherein it is, is the Father, the God of truth; because [the Fathe]r is established in his great, mighty consideration. So, [firs]t in the Father. The other powers that came from him were revealed through him, and (the fire) too came w[i]th them [and was revea]led by them.

The third [place wherein it is], is the First Man, h[e] wh[o ... ... ] armour. Again, it is this fire [ ... ... ] on the body of the Perfect Man.

[The fourth is this fir]e that was purified from all the [light] powers, [which is i]n the outer wall, in the ship of the day.

[The fifth i]s this fire that is in the light-giver of the night [ ... ] within the water.

[The] sixth is this f[i]re that is purified from the totality. The Living [Spirit] swathed it upon his body, over the summons and the obedience. He has established many things by it. He went down and stripped himself of it before the Por[t]er.

The seventh is this living fire that is of the new earth, and is the consideration. For it is the first foundation in the new earth, it they built anew [ ... ... ] the first course, that is a crown for the entire earth. T[hey] are below the totality, over the entire earth of da[rkne]ss [ ... ] in it, in the [ ... ] whole [ ... ]

[The] eigh[th] is the fire that shall be revealed in this [ ... ... ] person, and it signifies fear and war. Furthermore it [ ... ] it displays and manifests its sign in [its ter]ror. It shall be r[ev]ealed in these two light ships.

This i[s the way] that the holy living fire exists in these [eight places].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening is a formulaic dialogue attribution using “apostle” (FRAME marker), followed by a bare thesis statement that initiates the systematic eight-place scheme (CORE).
- ¶2: Impersonal structural voice; cosmic topography (“light land… beyond the totality”) and degree/foundation language; stays entirely within the cosmological system.
- ¶3: Systematic placement within divine hypostasis; emanation language (“powers that came from him”) and “consideration” as an internal cosmological attribute—no citation or application.
- ¶4: Cosmic being→body/armour correspondence (fire as swathing/armour); still purely cosmological enumeration.
- ¶5: Purification mechanics and placement in the “ship of the day” (sun-vehicle) are classic core cosmography; no institutional pointer.
- ¶6: Parallel station to ¶5 (night-ship/moon), continuing the numbered cosmological list.
- ¶7: Process-description (purified; swathed; went down; stripped) within the mythic-cosmic system (Living Spirit, Summons/Obedience, Porter). No audience address or polemic.
- ¶8: Degree/foundation architecture (“first foundation… first course… crown”) mapping fire↔consideration within the ‘new earth’ cosmology; remains internal to the system.
- ¶9: Eschatological/cosmic revelation still framed as a property/sign-function of the fire and its manifestation in the two light ships; no NT citation, no institutional application.
- ¶10: Closing summary sentence that simply seals the enumerative teaching; not a hagiographic doxology or community instruction, so it reads as part of the original schematic exposition.

---

## Chapter 75
### [Concerning the Letter (?)]

*The chapter opens with a standard dialogue frame, then preserves a compact core teaching on the primordial “summons” and “answer” exchanged between the Living Spirit and the First Man as foreknowing “letters” containing all events. The second half shows strong editorial fatigue: the letter motif is extended to the holy church, its good works, and direct exhortation about prayer—an institutional/pastoral graft that mimics the earlier teaching syntax but points outward to contemporary community practice.*

Consider the summons, which was sent from the Living Spirit in the beginning. He sent it to the First Man. It is a peace letter and greeting that he wrote and sent to his brother, in which all the tidings are written down, together with everything it will bring about and establish by that summons. That summons, the First Man [... is ordained to happen.

The answer that was sent from the First Man came to the heights, to the Living Spirit. That is also a letter, which was sent from the First Man to the Living Spirit, in which all the tidings are written down; and every war and the struggles that he had waged. It did [...] in the answer that the First Man [... ...] everything that had happened and everything [that would happen], all that had been done and whatever is made ready to be done by him. The Living Spirit knew all the wars and the dangers and the contests that the rulers had waged against the First Man.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic opening attribution (“Once again he speaks to his disciples…”) marking the hagiographic/dialogue container layer.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological exposition of a primordial dyad (Living Spirit ↔ First Man) using the letter-as-cosmic-mechanism motif; no audience-address, no institutional application, no citation formula.
- ¶3: Continues the same systematic cosmological mechanism: the ‘answer’ as a foreknowing record of cosmic conflict (rulers vs First Man). Despite slight rhetorical emphasis, it stays cosmos→cosmos and lacks ecclesial/institutional targeting.
- ¶4: Editorial extension of the letter motif to the ‘Light Mind… sent to the holy church’; the mapping’s second side leaves the closed cosmological system and points to the institutional present (“holy church”), a classic seam-style continuation even though it mimics core syntax. Christianized naming (“beloved Christ”) accompanies the church-application register shift.
- ¶5: Institutional/application material: ‘light product’ generated from the holy church, catalog of good works (fasting, prayers), and its presentation before Christ. Cosmos is used to validate communal practice; not the older impersonal mechanism layer.
- ¶6: Direct second-person exhortation (‘I say to you… ask… will be given you’) and prayer-instruction voice marks late pastoral application rather than cosmological substrate.

## Chapter 79
### Concerning the Fasting of the Saints

*This chapter is dominated by late institutional/pastoral instruction on fasting (including weekly practice and alms), wrapped in a standard dialogue opening. Two paragraphs preserve older doctrinal kernels in a more impersonal, process-oriented voice: the mechanics of purification of the soul/light in relation to food and the undefiled condition of the Children of Light. The numbered 'four works' structure appears to be an editorial pastoral organization into which these kernels were fitted.*

This soul that comes into him in the administration of his food, day by day; it shall be made holy, cleansed, purified, and washed from the adulteration of the darkness that is mixed in with it.

the mystery of [the children] of light in whom there is neither corruption nor [...] the food, nor wound it. Rather, they are holy, [there is nothing] in them that defiles, as they live in peace.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue attribution (“Once more the enlightener speaks…”) is a hagiographic/compilation frame marker.
- ¶2: Institutional topic-setting about saints’ fasting and its usefulness; no cosmological mechanism, written as practical doctrinal instruction.
- ¶3: Ascetic/disciplinary rationale (“punish his body by fasting… subdues…”) in a prescriptive conditional aimed at practitioners; not cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶4: The extracted sentence is mechanistic and impersonal (purification/mixture language tied to food), reflecting Manichaean cosmological soteriology rather than institutional instruction.
- ¶5: Core fragment describes an undefiled ontological condition (“children of light… nothing… that defiles”) rather than instructing an audience; the paragraph’s opening is a later applicatory pivot.
- ¶6: Ethical/practice instruction (“restrain their hands…”) embedded in fasting discourse; likely institutional discipline (cf. ‘seals’ praxis) despite cosmological terms like “Cross” and “living soul.”
- ¶7: Generalizing exhortation to persist daily and to make the body’s members fast; explicit practice-focus and community discipline voice.
- ¶8: Institutional accommodation (“lord’s day”) plus alms as participation; clearly later community practice rather than substrate cosmology.

## Chapter 82
### The Chapter of Righteous [Judgement]

*This chapter is dominated by late dialogical frame and pastoral/institutional admonition about reprimand, anger, and repentance. Only one paragraph (¶9) presents a self-contained cosmological mechanism (the heavenly light-givers displaying two aspects to purify the soul/powers), which fits the older correspondential-cosmological substrate. A Matthean saying (Mt 6:21) appears in ¶10, marking Christian overlay.*

For the light-givers that are in the heavens shall be revealed with these two aspects, in the aspect of love and the aspect of fear. They display these two aspects of the powers above that are in the heavens, because of their soul that passes through the totality. For in these two appearances, the aspect of love that is beauty and sublimity, together with the other aspect of confrontation that is in them, they shall purify in them the powers above.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Strong hagiographic/dialogue container: “Once again…”, disciple address and petition (“I beseech you…”), biographical/apostle framing; content is situational community counsel, not systematic cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶2: Continuation of the disciple’s first-person question/argument within the dialogue scene; lacks cosmological architecture and functions as part of the Q&A setup.
- ¶3: Explicit petition formula (“So, now I beseech you, my master…”) and anxiety about personal sin—dialogue frame + application voice rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶4: Although introduced by a dialogue attribution (“Then the apostle speaks…”), the paragraph’s substance is disciplinary counsel about rebuke, shame, repentance, and managing a ‘brother’—cosmos is not being mapped; this is community-instruction material.
- ¶5: Extended ethical taxonomy of ‘evil anger’ (pride, cursing, retaliation, refusing judgement). This is moral/communal guidance without cosmological correspondential mechanism.
- ¶6: Directly practical instruction about handling obdurate persons and defining ‘strong reprimand’ that leads to repentance—pastoral discipline register.
- ¶7: Didactic simile of the wise doctor using hot/cool remedies; a rhetorical analogy applied to correction, not a systematic internal cosmology mapping layer.
- ¶8: Application-heavy expansion: rebuke/humbling word, correcting ‘brother’ with love/fear, fear leading to repentance. Institutional interpersonal focus indicates later pastoral use of correspondential language.
- ¶9: Impersonal cosmological mechanism: heavenly ‘light-givers’ manifest paired aspects and effect purification of soul/powers through cosmic process (“passes through the totality”). This stays within the cosmic system (heavens/light-givers/powers), unlike the surrounding community admonitions.
- ¶10: Contains a Gospel/NT import (Mt 6:21: heart/treasure saying) embedded in exhortation (“you too…”). Even without an explicit citation formula, the identifiable NT entry-vector makes the paragraph Christian overlay.
- ¶11: Closing dialogue response and affirmation (“When that teacher heard… he said…”), typical frame closure/praise of the master’s lesson rather than teaching substrate.

## Chapter 83
### Concerning the Man who is ugly in his Body, [but] beautiful [in his Soul].

*Most of the chapter is hagiographic scene-setting (Mani in a public assembly) plus moral admonition culminating in an explicit Gospel saying (NT overlay). The oldest recoverable teaching layer here is concentrated in the impersonal, technical exposition of pearl formation (¶9–14, with a dialogue-attribution stripped in ¶9) and the cosmological return/rest of liberated souls (¶17). The later editor then applies the pearl imagery to the holy church and apostles (¶15–16) and ends with direct exhortation (¶18).*

“Pearls shall arise not in every place in the sea, nor be formed in the sea as a whole. Rather, in various places that are in this sea, pearls are formed in them [ ... ... ] that sea, in which the [pearls] shall be formed [ ... ] this [ ... ... ] what the sea shall [ ... ... ] its fire above and comes [down ... ... ] and it makes foam like the drop of water that flows [ ... ] down in rainwater [ ... ] is the water [ ... ... d]own first [ ... ... ] foam and comes down [ ... ... ] the sweet waters [ ... ... ] the waters.

“This drop of water shall [ ... ] down to the sweet waters and [ ... ] and they absorbed them and were combined with the [ ... ]. They did not [d]escend to the depths of the sea, but they [ ... ... ] it floated on the surface of the waters [ ... ... ] to it. The foam and the pearl-shell shall be formed [ ... ] this wholesome drop [ ... ... ] it, and it becomes a pe[arl ... ] that makes a drop of rainwater [ ... ] waters [ ... ] it not being whole. It breaks and separates out into many droplets, and it has time to become a drop of sweet water [ ... ] and comes up in the sea of [ ... ] rain and sweet water; and it is accomodated in the shell, which at first is foam. They shall be joined with each other at [this] time, and are shaped and become a great pea[r]l, a great and valued kind.

“When, however, a drop of rainwater falls, and that drop breaks into many droplets and various , they shall be formed into and be confined in [n]umerous pearls, in the shell and the pearl-shell. One might form two pearls, another may form three, others may form five; some mould more than these, some fewer.

“Now, when you might [find a] whole drop, and the shell receives it, it shall become a great and valued [pe]arl as its worth is perfected. [However], if these two droplets will have time (to adhere) to one another before any (water particle) escapes, and they mix with e[ach o]ther, and the shell [ ... ] before they break into [ ... ] within [ ... ] in a great kind [ ... ... ] the drop of rain, which [ ... ... ] another one, that [ ... ... ] the [w]aters form them in [ ... and] in a great, valued commodity.

“Behold, [I, I have] taught you how sea-pearls shall be formed. I have told you that as a pearl shall come into existence by means of rainwater that has [ti]me to become foam, the pearl-shell shall come into existence by means of the foam, and the foam itself comes into being by means of the transformation and the [ ... ] of the sea.

“Then immediately at the time when [ ... ... ] the pearl divers know it, they shall [ ... ] and they [ ... d]own to those places and they bring pearls up from the depths of the sea, and each pearl diver finds according to the fortune that is [ordai]ned for him. The pearl divers shall [gi]ve them to the traders, and the traders give them to the kings and the nobles.

“Fo[r a]ll the souls that ascend in the flesh of mankind and are freed shall be brought back to the great aeons of light. A place of rest comes about for them, at that place in the aeons of greatness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Narrative scene-setting with ‘the apostle’ in a public gathering; hagiographic/compilational framing rather than teaching exposition.
- ¶2: Continues the narrative portrait of an elect man; institutional descriptors (‘elect’, ‘commandments’) function as story apparatus, not systematic cosmological teaching.
- ¶3: Congregational reaction scene (mocking, scoffing) in narrative voice; not a cosmological/correspondential mapping layer.
- ¶4: Hagiographic action (embrace/kiss) featuring ‘the glorious one’/‘apostle’; typical editorial story-frame.
- ¶5: Dialogue lead-in (‘He says to them:’) attached to the public assembly setting; a framing hinge rather than core exposition.
- ¶6: Moral admonition anchored to a Gospel-style authority claim (‘For the saviour says…’) and a ‘least of the faithful/angel sees the face of the Father’ motif (NT entry vector). Even where it uses inner/outer man language, the paragraph’s climactic warrant is Gospel-citation overlay.
- ¶7: Narrative transition describing the audience response and seating; part of the chapter’s hagiographic container.
- ¶8: Formal disciple question (‘Tell us, our master…’) is a classic Kephalaia Q&A frame device introducing the next teaching block.
- ¶9: Underlying content is impersonal, technical explanation (how pearls form) without ecclesial address; but it is introduced by a compilational dialogue-attribution that must be stripped.
- ¶10: Extended, stepwise process-description in an impersonal explanatory voice; no institutional identification or citation formula.
- ¶11: Systematic numerical variation within the process account; remains within the descriptive ‘how it is formed’ register (no application to church/audience).
- ¶12: Continues the technical formation account with conditional cases (whole drop vs. mixed droplets); still non-institutional exposition.
- ¶13: Summative closure of the process-teaching; first-person recap, but still within the same impersonal instructional register (no ecclesial mapping yet).
- ¶14: Parabolic narrative mechanics (divers → traders → kings/nobles) still stated within the imagery itself, prior to overt ecclesial identification.
- ¶15: Explicit institutional application (‘This is also what the holy church is like’) crosses from image-system into community identity; classic cosmos/image → church-pointing move.
- ¶16: Continues ecclesial application (‘apostles are like the divers’) even while naming cosmic terms (‘light-givers’, ‘aeons’); the paragraph’s function is institutional mapping, so it belongs to the pastoral/application layer.
- ¶17: Returns to impersonal cosmological-soteriological mechanism (souls freed → brought back to Aeons of Light → rest place), without direct church/audience address.
- ¶18: Direct second-person exhortation (‘You too, my beloved ones, struggle…’) and imperative application voice; explicitly pastoral closing appeal.

## Chapter 84
### Concerning Wisdom; it is far superior when on the Tongue than in the Heart of the Person.

*This chapter is dominated by a non-institutional teaching unit (child-in-womb and fire-in-wood analogies) explaining why wisdom increases when expressed. The main late material is the Kephalaia dialogue apparatus (disciple petitions, apostle-attribution formulas, and the formulaic closing). No NT citation overlay and no ecclesial/pastoral graft is present; the core here is homiletic-correspondential rather than systematic cosmological mapping.*

Well [do] you ask! And great is this lesson for which you have sought, [that is] whence comes my great joy, on account of this wis-dom that I utter? [ ... ] it is superior in my mouth when I proclaim it than when it is set in m[y heart]. You yourself rejoice in it; and the other one who hears it from you, he shall rejoice in it, and be enlightened [by] it and receive thereby permanent strength.

For like this matter, just so a small b[o]y who is conceived in the belly of his mother. He [ ... he] turns in his mother's womb, filling her womb. The mother knows and understands that this child she conceives is alive within her. She rejoices over him until [the tim]e when she gives birth. And he comes from her alive with his [limbs whole] and perfect in beauty, without defect, [in] the living open air that is more sp[ac]ious than the first air he was in. He fills his eyes with the light and speaks with his living voice in the way of they who are born.

Now, the time when this woman conceives the child in her belly, her joy at conceiving him in her womb is not so [ve]ry great as when she gives birth and sees him; and is full of his [be]auty and stature in the space of a single moment. The love and joy over him shall be a hundred [t]imes greater than it was, now that she has given birth and seen him.

For the first time when she conceived him in her belly, his beauty and the sight of his eyes was hidden from his m[o/th]er; but when she gave birth to him she saw his beauty. His s[t]ature and his loveliness came before the e[ye]s of his f[athe]r [a]nd his mother and all his relatives. They shall rejoic[e] over him more and more when they look upon him face to face and see his beauty and delightfulness.

Just as in this simile, the wisdom that is present [in] the heart of the person is like the living child who is co[nceived] in the belly of his mother. And when he is taught and seals it in his heart, it becomes like the child who shall be born, and they see his beauty.

So, in this way, the wisdom that the person proclaims, speak[ng] it from his heart, shall be advanced more and mor[e]. Its en-hancement and glory shall double from the time when the bea-uty and splendour of the saying will be displayed before the eyes [of t]hey who hear it, and it shall also advance for you [ ... ... ] your hearing, and you are astonished at what you proclai[m].

Once again, the wisdom is like this, while it is hidden in the heart of the person. [Bef]ore he has uttered it, it is just like [the blaze] of fire that is hidden in wood. An[d] that wood is [set aflame] by the blaze of the fire, but the garment of fir[e that exists in] the wood is not apparent. Indeed, you can see [ ... ] wood and they put them in a single house. It is impossible to [put li]ght to that house as long as the light within [ ... ] until the time when they are added to the fire, and the light [comes f]orth from them. It is possible for that entire house [to be] lit by the light of a single piece of wood.

This is also the case with the wisdom that is in the heart of the person. [It] is like the fire that is hidden in the wood, as its light is not [d]isplayed. For its part, the wisdom is like this: its li[ght] is hidden and its glory is hidden in the heart; but when the person will proclaim it, its glory shall be displayed before the eyes and the ears of a multitude.

For in this respect the wisdom is like this child about whom I have told you, the one who [was] born from the woman. Now, when he will be born, his father and mother and family circle shall rejoice over him. However, you find others grieving by reason of him, [s]ince they are strangers to him. These are not reckoned among his family. [They do n]ot rejoice over him, because he is not of their race.

This is also [the case] with the wisdom. When it is proclaimed by the mouth of the teacher, these who are [a]kin to it shall receive it to them and rejoice in it; but [those] who are strangers to it neither rejoice in it [ ... ] nor receive it to them.

Just like the light of [the fire, which I] proclaim[ed] to you, that shall come from the wood [and be apparent o]utside before the eyes of every one. So, [wh]oever looks shall see the light that has [come fro]m the wood; but whoever is blind does not see the [fir]e.

This is also the case with the wisdom, when it will be pro-claimed. The person, in whom is the [Mi]nd, of him is the wisdom. Whenever he may hear it, he shall receive it in to him; but the one who has no Mind in him is a stranger to it. He neither receives it in to him, nor shall he listen to [it].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Strong Q&A frame: “Once again… disciple… before the apostle… I entreat you, my master.” Even though it paraphrases the topic, the paragraph functions as editorial setup rather than impersonal teaching exposition.
- ¶2: Continues the editorial question apparatus (disciple speech + repeated entreaty). This reads as an eliciting prompt and self-report rather than the teaching layer’s own expository voice.
- ¶3: The attribution formula is late frame, but the remaining content is a compact teaching maxim about the effect of proclamation (joy/enlightenment/strength) without institutional or NT anchoring.
- ¶4: Impersonal analogical exposition (no dialogue formula, no institutional targets). The teaching proceeds by correspondence (womb→hidden life; birth→manifestation; air/light/voice→manifest life).
- ¶5: Continues the same simile as structured teaching (concealment vs manifestation), without frame/pastoral directives.
- ¶6: Still purely analogical-causal explanation (hidden/seen; face to face) with no editorial anchoring to community practice.
- ¶7: Explicit correspondence mapping (wisdom-in-heart ↔ conceived child; proclamation/manifestation implied by birth). This stays within the teaching’s internal symbolic system (no cosmos→institution pointing).
- ¶8: Process-teaching voice (advance/double/displayed), focused on how utterance amplifies wisdom through hearing—no later institutional application.
- ¶9: Second sustained analogy (fire-in-wood) used as correspondential mechanics (hidden potency vs manifest light) without editorial polemic or church discipline.
- ¶10: Direct interpretive mapping of the simile (hidden light/glory → displayed to eyes/ears). Still not cosmos→institution, and no citation formulas.
- ¶11: Returns to the Kephalaia dialogue apparatus (“Once again… disciple… speaks to the apostle”) introducing a second question; functions as editorial staging.
- ¶12: Attribution and promise-to-explain (“the apostle speaks… I will persuade you…”) with no teaching substance; typical dialogue frame linkage.
- ¶13: Extends the analogy internally (kin vs strangers) to explain differential reception; no ecclesial categories (elect/auditors) are introduced.
- ¶14: Interpretive mapping of the kin/stranger simile to wisdom-hearers. “teacher” is generic here (not an office list), and the mapping stays within the teaching logic.
- ¶15: Analogy extension (seeing vs blindness) remains general and non-institutional, preparing the doctrinal criterion in the next paragraph.
- ¶16: Introduces an internal, system-consistent criterion (presence/absence of Mind as an ontic capacity) rather than institutional identity; this reads as teaching substance rather than later pastoral regulation.
- ¶17: Formulaic closing reaction (“rejoiced… made obeisance… sat down”), matching the chapter-level ‘formulaic closing’ flag; classic hagiographic wrap-up.

## Chapter 85
### Concerning the Cross of Light: [...] trample upon it.

*This chapter is dominated by later pastoral/institutional application (missions, alms collection, elect/catechumen practice) wrapped in a dialogue frame, with marked pastoral drift in the second half. The oldest recoverable teaching here is mainly a cosmological-soteriological kernel: the Living Soul/Cross of Light can undergo necessary distress for healing/liberation without reckoning it as sin (medical analogy), plus a (partly lacunose) mechanism linking destructive speech to demonic binding. One explicit Gospel citation is a later Christian overlay.*

Again, do not be afraid even with this; because the Living Soul is like this. As a person, if a disease will take hold of his limbs, and his heart [is sl]ow, his soul distressed, and he has pain in his disease. He seeks after a doctor to cure him, to make him strong of heart. And he cures, attends to, and gives him health [from] the disease that has risen up against him. So he thinks about this.

[And] a wise person comes, knowing the formulas proper to a skilled doctor. If he shall achieve a healing of the one whom he [... ...] it, he recites over him the formulas that he knows, and he even tramples with his foot upon all his limbs! That person, who is ill, knows that this person is trampling upon him so that he might achieve a healing; rather, not doing this to him out of some enmity he had against him beforehand. Instead, all that he does, he does to him that he might make a healing of his body. And he expels [the] disease and the pain, trampling upon him to occasion this benefit. That person, who is ill, he can bear no anger nor hatred against this doctor, who tramples upon him. He can not hate him out of enmity, because he knows that he does this to him to his benefit.

However, should someone come and trample upon him in enmity; he may hate, prevent and not permit him! For he knows that he tramples upon him in enmity; and as he strikes out of a wanton whim, he piles another illness upon his disease.

Again, this too is the case with the Living Soul; whether in the earth, or in the Cross of Light. Should you walk along upon it on the path, should you trample the Cross, it may take no reckoning against you in anger nor wrath. For it knows that [you] walk upon it for relief and healing, to proclaim on its behalf. [You] walk because of it, to reveal its mysteries. Also, the distress of the bones, which you have oppressed, befits not sin, but is set towards the victory. Again, because of this nothing will be lost for you due to the distress of the bones and your toil; rather, it all accumulates to the relief (of the soul).

Rather, the word that wounds is this: should a person utter a saying for the sake of the murder of a person, or the murder of animals, or for [the sake of] the murder of trees and the Cross of Light, the lying word and the wrathful and the bitter, or a word of envy and [...] only, or a word of accusation, which someone will spread against his brother; this is the [wor]d that wounds [...]. Should the person utter it, it befits [si]n; because [he has br]ought forth from it the demons [...] concerning this [...] the righteous person, or [...] all of them, which I have proclaimed, in order that [...] his word to the fetter; because anyone who would [...] his word to the fetter. And again, he [...] his coming forth, they will bind him in the place wherein he has bound his [...].

[He is li]ke this, a wise doctor who might turn back [... ...] his disease. As a wound is in his body, he cuts [...] this person who is ill, in his wisdom, [...] he [...] this wound, and he cuts it. After these things [...] and he places the soluble drugs upon it, so that [they will be able to diss]olve. He might incise it with the iron, and he casts it out [f]rom him, with the soluble drugs. And the wound is purified [fr]om the fever. After these events, he shall place the soothing drugs [...] and it heals up. That person, who is ill, he shall give [...] but he shall not [...] he [shall not] generate anger in his heart against him; because he knows that he is making the aspect of his benefit and his healing. After the cut that he may make, and the cauterizing that he may do there, he shall even give rewards to the doctor! He gives his thanks and is a friend to him his whole life through.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame (“Once again… questioned the apostle”) and explicit setting “in the congregation of the church”; functions as a framed rehearsal of praxis rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Institutional church logistics (superior/teacher, foreign brethren, alms, brought to the church) and anxious self-scrutiny; cosmos is used to regulate community practice.
- ¶3: Begins with hagiographic speech-attribution (“the apostle… says”), then resolves a practical case about obedience to superiors and travel for church work; application voice dominates.
- ¶4: Explicitly addresses “Every elect and righteous person” and legitimates walking for “work of God”; institutional category “elect” and paraenetic reward language mark later pastoral guidance.
- ¶5: General ethical contrast (lust/acquisition vs. sin/error) without systematic cosmological mapping; paraenetic extension of the walking rule.
- ¶6: Returns to teacher-sending and the disciple’s scruple; anchored in community obedience structure rather than impersonal cosmological mechanics.
- ¶7: Begins the impersonal cosmological justification via a natural analogy (healing) rather than institutional rules; introduces the Living Soul as the subject of the mechanism (distress endured for cure/liberation).
- ¶8: Extended mechanism-analogy: necessary trampling/pain as therapeutic action; no church offices/alms/catechumen structure, and the logic is explanatory rather than prescriptive.
- ¶9: Completes the core distinction (healing-intent vs enmity-intent) needed for the later application; still within the explanatory analogy, not institutional practice.
- ¶10: Core cosmological mechanism (Living Soul in earth/Cross of Light does not reckon necessary trampling as wrath) is interrupted by a Gospel citation formula (“As the saviour has said…”), indicating later NT overlay grafted onto an older explanatory unit.
- ¶11: Returns to the institutional scenario (teacher/foreign brethren/alms) and reassures about speech; strongly application-driven and anchored to alms administration.
- ¶12: Despite ethical exempla, the paragraph grounds guilt in a cosmological mechanism (demons brought forth; binding/fettering in a corresponding place), rather than in church discipline; no NT citation or institutional seam markers.
- ¶13: Applies the ‘word’ distinction directly to alms-speech and release/gathering; this is practical community regulation rather than the impersonal mechanism itself.
- ¶14: Continuation of the therapeutic-necessity logic in a detailed, explanatory register (incision/iron/drugs/cautery) without institutional offices; functions as core analogy supporting the cosmological claim about necessary distress for salvation.
- ¶15: Explicit institutional mapping of the analogy (elect, alms-giving, brought to the church; ‘purified in the image of the saints’)—cosmos is used to justify church feeding practice, so this is later pastoral application.
- ¶16: Directly about “the holy church that the apostle shall establish,” listing ascetic disciplines; institutional/paraenetic material characteristic of later church practice.
- ¶17: High application voice (“I command you, my brothers and my limbs”) and concrete eating/alms discipline with catechumen instruction; clearly institutional paraenesis.
- ¶18: Dialogue attribution and summative reassurance (“Then speaks the apostle… You have no sin therein.”) function as a formal closure beat, not substrate exposition.
- ¶19: Hagiographic reaction formula (rejoicing, thanks to ‘my master’) and generalized praise of persuasion; standard Kephalaia framing close.

## Chapter 86
### The Chapter of the Man who asks: Why [am] I sometimes at peace, and troubled at other times?

*The chapter is built as a framed Q&A. The opening and closing are hagiographic frame; the disciple’s long self-description includes institutional piety (fasting, psalms, prayers) and reads as later pastoral coloration. The oldest teaching substrate is concentrated in the explanatory mechanism: purification by the Light Mind, disturbances via birth-signs/stars and the ‘roots/conduits’ of the heavenly powers, and the mixed composition of food and drink affecting inner states—plus the notable doctrine of ‘justified souls’ present in nourishment (transmigration/merit language).*

The soul that assumes the body, when the Light Mind will come to it, shall be purified by the power of wisdom and obedience, and it is cleansed and made a new man. There is no trouble in (the soul), nor confusion nor disturbance.

However, when a disturbance will arise for him and he will be troubled, this disturbance shall go into him in [ ... ], first through his birth-signs and his difficult stars that [ ... ] they turn over him and stir him and trouble him with lust and anger and depression and grief, as he wills. Also, as he wills, the powers of heaven shall trouble him through their roots, to which he is attached.

Again, there are times you shall find the powers of heaven are peaceful and tranquil for him, and it comes to [ ... ] a difficult part comes into him by the nourishment that he has eaten, or else indeed in some [ ... ] or in the water that they have drunk. Again, trouble and confusion and anger will increase in him, and lust multiplies upon him together with depression and grief; because of the nourishment of the bread he has eaten and the water he has drunk, which are full of bothersome parts, a vengeful counsel. They shall enter his body, [mixed in] with these foods, and they even become joined in with the wicked parts of the body and the sin that is in him; transferring the anger and the lust and the depression and the grief, these wicked thoughts of the body.

that should you be troubled [ ... ... ] them through the conduits and the roots of they above [ ... ... ] the flood that comes into you.

There are times, however, if you shall find that the nourishment that comes into you is pure [ ... ... ], so it is greater in light and life while the waste is less in it, and the evil-doing is diminished from it. Even the atmosphere above is still, being purified through the [ ... ... ] also the image is calmed from [ ... ... ] increases in you through those good parts of the food that come into you. And they find you at ease and tranquil, as you are well governed in your abodes because of the living part, which is greater on account of the light of these justified souls that are in it. The ones that have perfected their deeds. Their dues have ceased, while their soul is carefree [ ... ] they have been allied with these living souls that [are p]resent in you. Due to this you shall find them in you, calm in tranquillity, and they come from you without trouble; and you are found flourishing of body. Even your deeds are determined, being well constructed after their manner, and your doctrines are in order, as your words [ ... ] your soul is carefree within you, ascending like a bird.

When [your bo]dy is troubled, you shall be troubled by the conduits of they below, the difficult parts that come into you by food and the act of eating. Again, when the conduits above are calmed from you, even your food that has come into you is clear and pure. Your heart shall be found ordered in its place, your counsels at peace from disturbance.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame (‘Once again… I beseech you, my master’).
- ¶2: First-person rhetorical setup inside the Q&A frame; experiential piety and self-report, not impersonal cosmological mechanism.
- ¶3: Continuation of the disciple’s self-report (confusion, passions, social impact ‘for my brothers’). Application/psychological rhetoric rather than structural cosmological exposition.
- ¶4: Institutional devotional practice vocabulary (fasting, praying, psalms) and moral/social comportment (‘indulgent to my brothers’) mark later church-life application rather than substrate teaching.
- ¶5: Beseeching request formula concluding the question; hagiographic frame voice.
- ¶6: Core teaching begins with impersonal cosmological mechanism (Light Mind purifies; ‘new man’; state-change description). The surrounding speaker-attribution is later frame.
- ¶7: System-internal causality: astral configuration (‘birth-signs’, ‘difficult stars’) and ‘powers of heaven’ acting through ‘roots’—a structural cosmological explanation rather than exhortation.
- ¶8: Cosmos→cosmos/material mechanism: food/water as mixed carriers of ‘parts’ that join to bodily wicked parts, transferring passions; no institutional targeting despite second-person phrasing.
- ¶9: The remaining fragment preserves technical causal terms (‘conduits’, ‘roots’, ‘flood’ as influx), but the recap formula indicates later compilation voice layered over it.
- ¶10: Sustained mechanistic exposition: purity of nourishment (light/life vs waste), calm of ‘atmosphere above’/‘image’, and the ‘justified souls’ present in food increasing the living balance—strongly system-internal, though the transmigration/‘dues have ceased’ phrasing may reflect later syncretic development within the teaching tradition.
- ¶11: Core remainder is a concise two-channel model (conduits below vs conduits above) linking bodily disturbance and food-derived ‘parts’ to inner order—impersonal structural summary embedded in later recap voice.
- ¶12: Formulaic closing doxology/praise of the apostle (‘glorified… You are glorious and blessed’), typical hagiographic frame.

## Chapter 87
### Concerning the Alms, that [ ... ] life in the Church.

*This chapter is dominated by institutional/pastoral exposition about alms, the holy church, and catechumens (¶¶2–6, 11), wrapped in a hagiographic opening (¶1). The oldest teaching substrate appears as a self-contained cosmological mechanism of ascent and rest: the Living Soul in mixture can exit only through the Sun and Moon, and these light-givers rest finally in the Land of Light (¶¶7–10).*

Likewise this [liv]ing Sou[l] that today is set in mixture; for it wishes to [as]cend and go to the house of its people, but it kn[ow[s a]nd understands that it has no open door in all the powers of heaven and earth. For they are its oppression that [ ... ] it everywhere. Indeed, it has no open door except the sun and the moon themselves, the light-givers of the heavens [ ... / ... ] they become the place of rest for it. And they become a door that opens for it [in] the coming forth, and (the soul) comes out through them to the country of the household of i[ts] people.

Again, further in, also the light-givers of the heavens have no place of rest amongst all the powers of heaven; except for the lan[d] of light, that has indeed been theirs for ever.

So, thus the living soul is purified of all afflictions; and they provide a rest for it, and open a door that opens to the house [of] its people.

Also, it is the case that the land of lig[ht] will become the final receiver for the light-givers of [the] heavens, and they will rest themselves therein and rule in it [for] ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic narrative frame (“Once again… The enlightener… He says to his disciples”) typical of Kephalaia compilation apparatus.
- ¶2: Maps alms to contemporary religious landscape (“every sect… their catechumens… holy church… commandments of alms”) and polemic; cosmos→institution pointing, consistent with editorial/institutional layer.
- ¶3: Defines the holy church in social categories (“brothers and sisters”) and treats alms redemption via the church—an institutional mechanism rather than cosmic architecture.
- ¶4: Institutional dependence chain (church ↔ catechumens) expressed as present-world logistics (“in this entire world… through the catechumens”), characteristic of later church operational teaching.
- ¶5: Begins explicitly with catechumen institution (“assembly of catechumens”) and uses an agricultural analogy to motivate their role; the correspondence is deployed to point at a community structure, not to expound cosmic degrees.
- ¶6: Explicit editorial application of the tree/earth analogy to “the [holy] church” and catechumens; institutional vocabulary dominates and completes the pastoral graft.
- ¶7: Impersonal cosmological mechanics (mixture → ascent; “open door”; “powers of heaven and earth”; Sun/Moon as functional gateways/rest-stations) stays fully within the cosmic system. The single anchoring word “today” does not redirect to institutional application.
- ¶8: Pure cosmic-domain mapping: light-givers’ rest-station is the Land of Light; no institutional or audience-address elements.
- ¶9: Continues the same ascent/rest mechanism (purification via cosmic “door” and “rest”), still cosmos→cosmos with no contemporary referents.
- ¶10: System-closure statement: final receiver/resting place and eternal rule of the light-givers in the Land of Light—structural cosmology typical of the older teaching layer.
- ¶11: Returns to institutional chain (holy church ↔ alms of catechumens) with a concluding nod to the Land of Light; the cosmos is used to validate church practice, indicating late application rather than substrate exposition.

## Chapter 88
### Concerning the Catechumen who found fault with the Elect: why he is angry.

*This chapter is overwhelmingly a situational pastoral/homiletic piece (catechumen vs. Elect), wrapped in narrative frame. The only recoverable older substrate is a small cluster of dualist-anthropological statements (divinity from the heights; alien body; the “two essences”) embedded inside exhortation and institutional categories (Elect/saints). No systematic cosmos→cosmos mapping sequence appears here; the correspondential tree image functions as moral application, not cosmological exposition.*

They are gods as they stand firm in the image of the gods. The divinity that is planted in them came to them from the heights and [dw]elt in them. They have done the will of the greatness.

You know, once and for all, they are established in a body that is not their own, hating the flesh of Sin that makes the other dwell in a foreign land.

For you have known the mystery of the two essences, you have understood that what is good and what is evil dwell in every person.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic narrative opening with titles (“my master, the apostle”) and scene-setting; hagiographic/dialogue frame rather than teaching.
- ¶2: Speech of the catechumen within the dialogue setup; rhetorical complaint about ‘righteous ones’ and behavior, not cosmological exposition.
- ¶3: Dialogue attribution (“our enlightener speaks”)—a classic compiling-frame marker.
- ¶4: Exhortation plus an illustrative parable (scented-oil tree) used to correct behavior; correspondence is deployed to address community conduct (pointing), not to map the cosmic system (being).
- ¶5: Underlying cosmological anthropology (indwelling divinity from the heights; ‘image of the gods’) is plausibly older teaching, but it is embedded in an ecclesial comparison about the Elect.
- ¶6: This preserves a compact piece of dualist mechanism (alien embodiment; Sin as an agentive force), which stays within the Manichaean cosmic-anthropological system, though originally framed to justify the Elect’s behavior.
- ¶7: A brief doctrinal kernel (two essences; mixed indwelling of good/evil) embedded in direct second-person admonition—likely an older teaching fragment used pastorally.
- ¶8: Direct accusation and moral instruction to the catechumen (sins, lifestyle), plus institutional teacher-disciple relations; ‘Cross of Light’ appears, but only as part of ethical reproach and community discipline.
- ¶9: Pure exhortation/application voice (“love and honour them… do not speak against them”) with no independent cosmological exposition.
- ¶10: Narrative transition and emotional response scene (“weeping”); part of dialogue frame/closure movement.
- ¶11: Catechumen’s penitential reply addressed to “my master,” reiterating lesson within the narrative container; not a systematic teaching layer, and includes late-style confessional posture.

---

## Chapter 90
### Concerning the Fifteen Paths; and whether the Catechumen would have avoided causing his Wealth to go on the Three Paths to Gehenna

*Only a small core substrate survives here: a compact, systematic cosmological teaching on the fifteen paths in the “zone” and the three waste-paths to the gehennas (¶2–4, embedded inside a question-summary). The remainder is dominated by later institutional soteriology about elect/catechumens, the apostle’s pre-selection of “the entire church,” seals of faith, transmigration as purification, and practical piety (fasting/prayer/alms), matching the chapter’s strong pastoral drift and Gardner’s redaction/buddhist-connection flags.*

‘Fifteen paths are drawn out in the zone. Four paths are pure and belong [to the] light, leading up to life. Eight other paths are [mixed], leading above from that place. The light shall go [u]p and become free through them; be purified and go in [to the] ships. However, the waste is separated and thrown [dow]n to transmigr[a]tion and the other three paths of [waste ...] discharged to the gehennas’.

[that the three] paths that belong to waste, the ones that are drawn to the gehennas, [a]re drawn from the fleshes. O[n]e of them is the appetite for [lawl]essness, that of people and of all flesh. The second is the [ ... ] all bodies, which is slaughter, [with] which all flesh is consumed. The third is the damaging action, which [ ... ] the Cross of Light and every body; together with the rest of the error and the blasphemy that wounds the gods.

Th[ese th]ree paths [ ... ] lead to the gehennas. And every person shall follow after hi[s] de[e]ds, whether to life or indeed to death.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic dialogue frame: “Once again…”, “elect stood up”, “apostle”, “I beseech you, my master”.
- ¶2: Core cosmological-correspondential schematic: numbered structure (15 paths; 4/8/3), impersonal mechanics (Light ascends to ships; Waste separated to transmigration/gehennas) stays within the cosmic system. Embedded via a later Q-summary voice.
- ¶3: Systematic triad defining the three waste-paths with cosmological referents (fleshes, Cross of Light, gehennas) and impersonal definitional tone; the “you have explained” wrapper is later Q&A framing.
- ¶4: Concluding axiom linking paths to post-mortem outcome (“follow after his deeds”) functions as part of the cosmological-judgment mechanism; embedded in a later address-to-teacher recap.
- ¶5: Extended question with strong Q-formula (“Now, I beseech you, my master…”) and institutional category “catechumen/faith of God”; functions as editorial dialogue scaffolding for later soteriological discussion.
- ¶6: Continues the disciple’s problem-setting about “catechume/ns… set in the world” before receiving faith; institutional framing rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶7: Dialogue attribution (“Then the apostle speaks…”) plus reassurance; classic Kephalaia frame marker (‘apostle’).
- ¶8: Flagged editorial seam: institutional extension about “souls of the elect and the catechumens,” “entire church,” and the apostle’s pre-manifestation selection; cosmos→church mapping and ‘apostle in flesh’ biography signal later institutional theology.
- ¶9: Applies salvation mechanics to contemporary religious landscape (“sects… heresies”) and institutional ‘seal of the faith/knowledge’; angels as pastoral assurance that deeds won’t go to gehennas.
- ¶10: Didactic analogy (royal horse/king’s seal) in service of the catechumen ‘mark of faith’ doctrine; not impersonal cosmic mapping.
- ¶11: Institutional soteriology: catechumen ‘mark/seal of truth,’ ‘form chosen,’ pity, deeds not going to gehennas because of faith; oriented to community identity and assurance.
- ¶12: Explicit church practice layer: fasting, prayer, alms “to the saints,” nullifying earlier deeds; disciplinary/pastoral register dominates.
- ¶13: Institutional rites and ranks (“right hand of peace,” “rank… catechumenate”) integrated with salvation logistics (ship of the night, deeds freed). The overall paragraph is an applied ecclesial soteriology rather than standalone substrate exposition.
- ¶14: Directly asserts catechumen agency to ‘free all his deeds… while being in his body’; pastoral reassurance addressed to catechumen status.
- ¶15: Extended illustrative simile about king/city/host; pedagogical analogy used for applied doctrine rather than systematic cosmological mapping.
- ¶16: Continuation of analogy (some go before/with/after); rhetorical exposition in support of applied soteriology.
- ¶17: Summative reiteration of the analogy; didactic/pastoral style.
- ¶18: Application explicitly to “the catechumen… with his deeds,” using cosmological locations (firmaments, land of the living) but subordinated to institutional catechumen doctrine; not a cosmos→cosmos mapping unit independent of church categories.
- ¶19: Closing instructional reassurance (“I have instructed you…”) about catechumen and angels; pastoral wrap-up rather than core substrate.

## Chapter 91
### Also concerning the Catechumen; shall he be saved in a single Body?

*This chapter is overwhelmingly institutional/pastoral (catechumen discipline, fasting, alms, church protection, merit accounting) wrapped in dialogue frame. The only recoverable older substrate consists of brief, more impersonal eschatological mechanism statements about post-mortem ascent and purification (firmaments/heavens; purification; ‘light image’; land of rest). There is explicit Christian overlay via a Pauline citation (¶4) and a likely Matthean proverb echo embedded in an otherwise mechanistic sequence (¶20).*

when he comes out from his body, his soul shall [... ...] in the firmaments above, and he travels to the place of rest.

When, however, they come forth from their body, they travel on their way and pass by in the place above, and go in to the life. They shall be purified in the heavens, and they are plucked just as a fruit that ripens is plucked from the tree.

Afterwards he is purified, whether indeed above or below. He shall be purified according to the worth of his deeds, and cleansed and washed and adorned. Afterwards, he is sculpted a light image; and [he glid]es up and reaches the land of rest,

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula and catechumen topic mark later compilation context, but the clause on post-mortem passage through firmaments to a ‘place of rest’ reads like a freestanding cosmological-eschatological mechanism that could predate the catechumenate application.
- ¶2: Direct petition/Q&A frame (“Now I beseech you, my master… instruct me…”) plus audience-management (“my other brothers”) is editorial container language.
- ¶3: Although introduced by a dialogue tag, the substance is a behavioral archetype for ‘perfect catechumens’ (marriage, property, relatives, ‘holy church’)—cosmos→institution/ethics rather than cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶4: Explicit ‘saviour… apostle’ citation of 1 Corinthians 7 language (“let these who have wives be like…”) indicates New Testament entry vector (Christian overlay), despite moral/ascetical fit with Manichaean practice.
- ¶5: Seam-like recap applying the preceding material to catechumen institutional categories (“perfect catechumens… like the elect… sign…”) functions as editorial consolidation/extension; not part of the older substrate.
- ¶6: Institutional discipline list (fasting, prayer, alms, ‘holy church’, ‘elect’, wife/children) and moral psychology (“potential for evil-doing is dead”) are pastoral catechesis, not systematic cosmology.
- ¶7: Exhortative identification (“Behold, this is the sign…”) plus reference to an authored book (“Treasure of Life”) and catechumen category are institutional/authorial, not substrate exposition.
- ¶8: Opening sentences describe a general post-mortem ascent/purification process in cosmic terms (above/heavens/life) with a natural correspondence (fruit plucked when ripe). The paragraph then pivots to church practice (alms to elect), a later application layer.
- ¶9: Authorial cross-reference (“I have written down in the Treasure of Life”) and merit-by-contribution-to-church framing are institutional.
- ¶10: Prescriptive instruction and ecclesial mediation (“pray… forgiveness… from God and the holy church”; ‘deeds… added to his account’) are pastoral/legal accounting.
- ¶11: Hagiographic closure: disciples praise/bless the teacher, laud his revelation to elect/catechumens, and use staircase imagery in a doxological register.
- ¶12: Renewed beseeching Q&A frame introducing a new topic (prior sins before joining the church); catalog of vices functions as question setup rather than substrate mechanism.
- ¶13: Though it uses Manichaean cosmological vocabulary (‘separated the light from the darkness’, ‘living soul’), the structure is sacramental/institutional (receiving ‘right hand of peace’ from the Light Mind ‘who dwells in the holy church’; asking forgiveness), consistent with later church practice framing.
- ¶14: Absolution doctrine is stated conditionally on persistence in belief; oriented to church-entry and renunciation of sects/idols—moral/juridical rather than cosmological mechanics.
- ¶15: Backsliding warning and repentance logic, explicitly keyed to catechumen/elect states, is disciplinary exhortation.
- ¶16: Brief institutional promise of absolution through fasting/prayer/alms—pastoral economy.
- ¶17: Detailed calendrical rule (fifty fasts on lord’s days; sexual restraint; dietary prohibitions; non-violence to ‘living soul’) is catechumen discipline.
- ¶18: Hours of prayer, alms, garment for saints, ‘daily communion’ and merit bookkeeping (‘half… for the good, the other half for sins’) are institutional practice.
- ¶19: Merit arithmetic (‘sins… separated into five parts’; church protection absolves four) and reliance on ‘holy church’/elect love dominate; cosmological phrases serve as justificatory rhetoric within the institutional scheme.
- ¶20: Middle of the paragraph shifts into a mechanistic eschatology (purification above/below; transformation into a ‘light image’; ascent to rest) consistent with older cosmological teaching; the surrounding moral accounting and the heart/treasure saying reflect later pastoral/Christianized framing.
- ¶21: Conditional recompense statement tied to steadfastness in catechumenate—disciplinary summary.
- ¶22: Moral warning about lying/apostasy and renewed total accounting of sins—pastoral admonition.
- ¶23: Salvation assurance is framed as conditional exhortation (‘if… steadfast…’) rather than cosmological exposition; no systematic internal mapping.
- ¶24: Closing hagiographic reaction formula (disciples glorify teacher). The added cosmological phrase (‘first light… through the Mind… riches of its knowledge’) functions as doxological ornament inside the frame, not standalone substrate teaching.

## Chapter 94
### Concerning the Purification of these Four Elements [that have been place]d in the Flesh.

*This kephalaion is dominated by an impersonal, systematic correspondential teaching layer: the four elements’ pollution-discharge chain (element → bodily secretion → affect → bodily condition) and the mechanism of purification in the body/new man. It is wrapped by a brief hagiographic sitting-scene frame (¶1) and closed by an institutional application contrasting elect vs. sinners (¶9).*

The four elements [are what is] purified. From the beginning they are holy ones, living ones, and enlighteners. However, when they undertood [ ... ] in that first struggle, the waste was [mixed] in with them, and the darkness of the enemy was mixed with them. And when they were come into the body, stripping off these polluted garments, they set them in the flesh. Their pollutions, which they have stripped off, shall be displayed in a [ ... ] and become a danger to the body.

The discharge of this pollution, that [the fir]e shall disgorge and place in the body, is [blo]od. The discharge of the blood is anger. The discharge of anger is the humours.

Also, the emissions that the waters discharge from them are lust. The discharge of lust is bitterness. The emission of bitterness is f[e]ver.

The emission that light shall disgorge is flesh. The emission of flesh is gloom. [The] emission of gloom is obstinacy.

The emissions that the tree shall disgorge, they are the voices of the winds of shame. The emission of winds of shame [is ... ]

The emission of the [tear] is the [ ... ] of these twelve emissions.

[ ... ] these four elements shall [ ... ] what are gathered in are [f]ound by the management of this soul food [that] enters the body. When they enter the body, they are cleansed and purified and established [i]n their living image, which is the new man. They shall live [ ... ] and receive the Light Mind and be purified in their image; and they come forth, being cleansed and holy. They attain their first rest.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic setup (“Once again… the apostle… sitting… He speaks thus”) marks an editorial dialogue-frame, not the teaching substrate.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological mechanism (elements, first struggle, mixture with waste/darkness, stripping garments, pollution lodged in flesh) stays within the cosmic/anthropological system; no institutional address or Gospel overlay.
- ¶3: Highly structured correspondential chain (element → secretion → passion → bodily state) in an expository voice; classic substrate mapping.
- ¶4: Continues the same systematic discharge-mapping; page marker removed per extraction rules.
- ¶5: Further element-to-affect correspondence presented as ‘what things are’ rather than exhortation; consistent with the core chain-logic.
- ¶6: Fragmentary but still in the same enumerative correspondential register (source → emission → named quality). The ‘tree’ is treated as a correspondential term within the system, not an institutional referent.
- ¶7: Summative enumerative line (“these twelve emissions”) indicates continuation of a numbered mapping set; despite lacunae, the structure is core-style.
- ¶8: Cosmological soteriology expressed as process (ingestion of soul-food, purification, establishment in ‘new man’, reception of Light Mind, attainment of rest) without church-institution mapping or citation formulas.
- ¶9: Institutional application anchors purification to the Manichaean social structure (“reach the elect” vs. “sinners”), moving from cosmic mechanics to community categories; thus later pastoral/institutional layer even though ‘transmigration’ language remains cosmological.

## Chapter 95
### The Apostle asks his Disciples: What is Cloud?

*Dominantly CORE: a systematic fivefold cosmological-correspondential mapping of clouds to the five light elements (fire, water, wind, light, mingled air), including the mechanics of purification of Lives, the stripping/descent of rulers, angelic seizure, and confinement in the seven outer ditches/paths. FRAME appears in the formulaic opening, the disciples’ question, and the closing doxology; a few teaching paragraphs are wrapped with dialogue/imperative meta-speech (treated as MIXED). No NT/Christian citation overlay; no institutional/pastoral grafting.*

Happen you know that it shall be released from five places and ascend over this great earth and [ ... ] and it is revealed and seen in the atmosphere between.

So, the cloud shall ascend from fire to the heavens towards the likeness of the Virgin of Light that she shall display to it. Its sign is the flashes that occur with lightning storms [ ... i]ts exchange [ ... ]. And they shall be purified by her towards [ ... ] the light that she shall reveal [ ... ]. The rulers shall be released by the lightning storms and they are freed and sent.

However, [these] angels shall immediately be summoned to them, so that they will seize them, because they know that they shall never do good. Every place they will touch they shall make dead and a loss. Whenever the angels will make towards them and come beside [ ... ], they shall flee in their constraint before them. They go into whatever they will meet upon and assume it, like a robber being abandoned and fleeing before one stronger than him, as he runs after him to arrest him.

Now, those rulers that shall be freed from these clouds and assume whatever they will meet upon [ ... ] into the [ ... / ... ] that they did not [ ... ] will seize this thing, whether indeed a tree to which they have found the way, or animals, or people. Simply, every place that they will assume they shall burn and destroy by the fire of their body. Namely, the rulers, [the] children of fire, they that shall be stripped of the fire.

In contrast, [the] cloud that shall be raised up from water, and comes above towards the Virgin of Light; its sign is the thunder storms and the booms that shall come in the atmosphere in various forms and are heard. [ ... ] Again, there are times if the rulers that belong to them, which exist in the cloud, shall on occasion be constrained [in a] water [clou]d. And they flee from it and abandon it.

Now, [they] too are so, in that every place that they will [reach], they shall make frost and hail and snow. [They rui]n the seed-corn and the fruits and the plants; and they [ ... ] every place [that] they will reach.

Again, [ang]els shall be summoned to them who seize them. Still, while the angels [ru]sh after them in order to seize them, they shall make this destruction.

Also, [the cl]oud that will be raised up from wind ascends to the atmosphere between, towards the likeness of the Virgin that she shall display to it. Its sign is [the s]torm wind that shall blow with bitterness.

So, in those winds and tempests shall the lives be purified. Again, the rulers will be stripped of the cloud of wind and they descend from it, safe in their image. All the rivers and the seas that they will reach and arrive at, they shall make waves in them and raise [ ... ] and they [ ... ] the ships. They wreak destruction [ ... ] and they seize and bring them [ ... ].

Also the cloud, the one of light, immediately they will be stripped of it [ ... / ... ] the Virgin. She shall display its sign too [ ... ]. And its sign is this, in that you shall find it sailing in stillness and calm. The lives in it shall be cleansed in stillness and calm. Again, the rulers that will be stripped of it [ ... ] that will come forth [ ... ] they shall put [ ... ] and they take [ ... / ... ] there [ ... ] the angels seize them.

Again, the cloud that shall be raised up from the mingled air comes above towards [the image] of the Virgin. Its sign is this, in that [ ... ] are purified in it in stillness and [rest ... these rulers] that will be stripped of that cloud and come from it; every place that they will find [ ... ] they shall make it [ ... ] one of [ ... ] until [ ... ] they are seized and taken to their midst.

Their strippings, that they shall leave behind them in various places they will have reached, whether the produce of the trees or the offspring of the fleshes, they shall be taken midst [ ... ] wither away. They shall inflict great dangers, but again those rulers also the angels shall send to their midst. They bring the light from them and cast the demons down to these seven outer ditches of the great sea; which is the vessel of the waters that are outside the world.

Those ditches and the [ ... ] until they reach the earths below [ ... ] feet of the Porter.

for sometimes these angels shall be sent to them, to seize the rulers. Whenever the rulers [ ... / ... ] for the angels rush after them. They beseech them [ ... ] they that are given the command. However, [t]hose [rul]ers shall be freed from them alone and assume [ ... ] and [the fle]sh. They are consumed by this offspring [ ... / ... ] tree and that of flesh. The angels shall set a [ ... ] over them, that they might guard them until the hour when [those r]ulers will be stripped of tree and [flesh; a]nd they go up above. They shall be gathered [ ... ] above, and they are poured out [ ... ] in their first for[ms ... im]ages in which they first were. They take the light from them, which had remained behind in them. And they too are cast down to these seven outer [path]s that are in the great sea; the place to where these former ones [were c/as]t. They shall reach their midst [ ... ] and the light that is in them is taken.

And they are cast to the outer prison by the angels, when the command is given to them through the power of the Virgin of Light. She has authority over the entire zone, and cleanses the life that is in it.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic scene-setting and dialogue frame: ‘Once again, the apostle… sitting… congregation… he says to his disciples’. This is hagiographic container, not the impersonal substrate.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological claim establishing a fivefold structure (‘released from five places’) with no institutional application.
- ¶3: Systematic element→cloud mapping (fire-cloud) plus purification mechanics under a named cosmic power (Virgin of Light). Entirely cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶4: Cosmic process description (angelic pursuit/seizure; rulers’ flight/assumption) using simile but no frame or institutional anchoring.
- ¶5: Internal cosmology of rulers ‘freed’ from cloud, assuming forms, acting by the ‘fire of their body’; closes with classificatory naming (‘children of fire’).
- ¶6: Second member of the fivefold set (water-cloud) with ‘sign’ language and the same purification/stripping mechanics; cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶7: Mechanics of the water-cloud rulers expressed through correspondential weather effects; remains descriptive rather than hortatory.
- ¶8: Continues the same cosmic policing cycle (summons→pursuit→seizure) without editorial frame.
- ¶9: Third mapping (wind-cloud) with stable template: origin element → ascent → Virgin’s likeness → sign.
- ¶10: Process-oriented purification/stripping language plus rulers’ effects on seas/ships; still within the cosmological system.
- ¶11: Fourth mapping (light-cloud). Despite second-person ‘you shall find’, the paragraph stays cosmos→cosmos (sign, cleansing, stripping, seizure) with no institutional application.
- ¶12: Fifth mapping (mingled air-cloud) continues the same structural template; systematic set-completion signal.
- ¶13: Internal eschatological mechanics: recovery of light and confinement in ‘seven outer ditches’—cosmic geography, not community practice.
- ¶14: Explicit Q&A frame (‘Then his disciples say to him: Instruct us, our master’) introducing the next explanation.
- ¶15: Core cosmological geography (ditches reaching to earths below the feet of the Porter) wrapped in a dialogue attribution.
- ¶16: Underlying passage is core process-mechanics (pursuit, assumption of tree/flesh, guarded stripping, reversion to first forms, extraction of light, casting to outer paths). Opening imperative suggests editorial delivery layer.
- ¶17: Retained lines are impersonal cosmological claims about authority in the zone and the mechanism of casting/seizure; removed portion is explicit pedagogical self-reference typical of chapter endings.
- ¶18: Formulaic closing praise/thanksgiving by disciples (‘they glorified him… we give thanks…’)—hagiographic closure.
