## Chapter 96
### The Three Earths that Exist, they bear Fruit.

*The oldest teaching layer is concentrated in ¶¶2–3 (and the teaching portion of ¶1): a threefold correspondential schema of “earth/field” (physical earth → Living Soul in the cosmic zone → later grafted ‘holy church’). ¶4 is a classic editorial seam: it mimics the same “earth/field + farmer” pattern but crosses from cosmos→institution (holy church, elect, catechumens), so the whole paragraph is late pastoral extension despite its cosmological diction (Light Mind). ¶5 is explicit exhortation/application to the audience (preach, toil), hence pastoral.*

Three earths exist in the universe, and are being toiled over. At first they [...] the farmer in them. Afterwards they bear [good] fruit to they who toil over them.

One of them is this great earth that people are living upon. They are ploughing in it [...], it with iron hoes, they cultivate, [making] the furrows, turning it over [...] Afterwards they harvest fruit from [it ...

The second field is the living soul, the holy one. It is mixed in the entire zone, above and below. The farmers who toil over it are these two great light-givers, which journey through the heavens. They are toiling and cultivating it, so that they will draw it towards their aspect. And it leaps up and comes to the heights. It goes upwards, out from every place wherein it is, and it adds to them.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with a formulaic frame marker, then immediately shifts into impersonal, structural exposition (“Three earths exist…”) characteristic of the cosmological-correspondential substrate.
- ¶2: Impersonal, descriptive “how it works” presentation establishing the first term in a numbered triad; no citation, exhortation, or institutional targeting.
- ¶3: Clear cosmos→cosmos mapping: ‘field’ = Living Soul in the zone; ‘farmers’ = the two light-givers (sun and moon) in heavenly motion drawing it upward. This is systematic correspondential teaching without institutional application.
- ¶4: Editorial seam/extension: continues the ‘third earth/field + farmer’ template but the mapping crosses into institutional present (holy church, elect, catechumens). Per the seam rule, the entire paragraph belongs to the late pastoral graft, even though it uses cosmological names (Light Mind).
- ¶5: Direct second-person exhortation and mission-instruction (“You yourselves… Preach… Open the eyes… Rather, toil!”) shifts from describing cosmic processes to addressing audience behavior—late pastoral/application layer.

---

## Chapter 97
### Concerning the Three Creations of the Flesh: the ones that were brought forth [...]; and the ones that were begotten by the Abortions; and the ones that were formed of themselves.

*This chapter is almost entirely a systematic, impersonal teaching sequence classifying animal/plant generation and nourishment within a Manichaean cosmological mechanism (Ambassador, rulers/powers, “thought” descending). Only the brief dialogue-introduction is later hagiographic frame.*

“All these animals that you see, and also the rest of the [crea]tures [...] of the earth, confirm them in three likeness[es ...]: some from [...] abortions. Others, however, form their own selves in their shape [...].

“Also, their nourishment is thre[e ...] light in them, if they shall wish [...]. [...] they eat see[ds and] fr[uit ...]; they eat seeds and fruit. The [...] it entirely, and they [...] the three archetyp[es] from their nature and the [...] the time when they are on the ea[rth ...]; they are seized [...] in their [...]. The Ambassador [...] all powers [...].

“[N] it [...], first [...]. Plant them generally to this name: shoots and grass. It formed the bad, bitter trees, the reeds and the thorns and the [...] and these others that are like them.

“For that thought belongs to the flesh-eating rulers. Still, these [that were] planted (belong to) grass and gourds, since they are in their essence. Nevertheless, [this t]ho[ugh]t that came down from the rulers, as they are in fruit and grass first and shall not be planted in flesh and blood, it formed the trees that shall give nourishing fru[it], as their gourds are sweet.

“Again, this thou[ght] that came down from the powers that eat m[ea]t and also eat grass and gourds, it has formed the trees that shall not produce any nourishment. Their fruits belong neither to good nor evil.”

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame (“Once again our enlightener speaks…”) typical of the Kephalaia’s editorial apparatus; not part of the teaching substrate.
- ¶2: Impersonal classificatory teaching (threefold typology of generation) with cosmos→cosmos description; no audience exhortation, citation, or institutional application.
- ¶3: Continues the mechanistic exposition (nourishment/light; archetypes; cosmic timing), and names the Ambassador as a cosmological agent—internal system explanation rather than later application.
- ¶4: Taxonomic, generative language about plant kinds (“shoots and grass… bad, bitter trees…”) presented as a process within the system; no frame/pastoral overlay. (Page marker removed per extraction rules.)
- ¶5: Core cosmological causality: a descending “thought” from rulers/powers generating categories of vegetation and fruit; remains entirely within the mythic-cosmic mechanism (no polemical ‘today’, no church address).
- ¶6: Ends the classification by iterating the same internal causal schema (powers → thought → plant outcomes), with a neutral category (“neither to good nor evil”) typical of systematic substrate exposition rather than later moral/pastoral use.

---

## Chapter 98
### What is Virginal; or, otherwise, what is Continent?

*The chapter is built as a five-step definitional ladder (“virginal” vs “continent”) mostly in a systematic cosmological/soteriological register. The opening Q&A is hagiographic frame. The final two steps drift into community-institutional categories (“elect”) and then human sexual practice; these are later applications layered onto the underlying degree-structure teaching.*

What is called ‘virginal’ is assigned to the mystery of the aeons of greatness. And they called it ‘virginal’ by the category of all the light that did not [taste] of death, nor come to the enemy’s war.

However, what is called ‘continent’ occurs to the mystery of the light that came and was mixed with the darkness. It was defiled in bodies [ ... ] and went up to the light. [They] called [it ‘co]ntinent’.

Furthermore, the holy vir[gin]als [ ... ... ] should they come out against the en[emy ... ... not] taste death, nor [ ... ].

On the other hand, what is called ‘continent’ [is this li]ght that mixed with the darkness, was purif[ie]d, came [up f]rom all the creatures, and was confirmed in the great[ness]. They called it ‘continent’ since it tasted [of the l]ust; but afterwards was cleansed from it and [become an] holy one.

Furthermore, what is called [‘vi]rginal’ is this light that was purified by the ima[ge] of the [A]mbassador. It wen[t up and] attained [the] he[ights. It was] confirmed in the image of the gods.

Again, what is [called] ‘continent’ is the remainder of this light th[at] remained behind, from what was cleansed. It goes up and comes down in transmigration. Al[so], there is a part [of] it that shall come and attain the form of [hum]an [fl]esh. Again, that one too shall be chosen in the flesh, [accept] the hope, and become an holy one. And they name it ‘continent’.

Furthermore, what is named ‘virginal’ is the Light Mind. However, on the other hand, what is called ‘contin[ent]’ is the new man, should it be purified from this old man. And it is cleansed and strips off the sin that is compounded with it, and it becomes a continent one.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic scene-setting (“the apostle… among the congregation of his church”) and direct-address questions; this is compiling-community frame, not impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Formal disciple request (“Proclaim to us, my master… good gifts… through you”): classic dialogue frame that wraps teaching.
- ¶3: Core definitional mapping (term → cosmic domain/state of Light) in an impersonal, structural voice; only the introductory speech formula is later frame.
- ¶4: Cosmos→cosmos definition: Light-in-mixture vs Light’s ascent; no institutional audience address or citation.
- ¶5: Lacunose, but continues the same “virginal” category as a cosmic class that does not ‘taste death’; no explicit turn to church practice, though the fragment is uncertain.
- ¶6: Systematic process-description of purification and confirmation in greatness; “lust” functions as a mixture-condition within the cosmology, not as a community exhortation.
- ¶7: Cosmological mechanism anchored in named beings (Ambassador) and image/confirmation language; remains fully inside the system (no institutional targets).
- ¶8: Degree-step description of remaining Light and transmigration into flesh; still explanatory (how the Light moves/stabilizes), not hortatory.
- ¶9: Underlying core mapping (Virginal/Continent → Light Mind/new man) is degree-structured, but the ‘elect’ identification is a later institutional graft; ‘old man/new man’ also echoes later Christian idiom, reinforcing mixed layering.
- ¶10: Directly about human sexual status and renunciation “in the flesh” (man/woman/intercourse); this is behavioral-ascetic application rather than cosmological architecture.
- ¶11: Meta-concluding speaker remark (“I have taught you… in many forms”) functioning as closure rather than continuing the systematic cosmological mappings.

---

## Chapter 100
### Concerning the Dragon with Fourteen Heads; what it is and [...]

*The chapter’s teaching substrate is a systematic microcosm mapping: the “dragon” is a spiritual force/thought associated with the body, having fourteen “heads” (sense/torso faculties) and five “dens” (internal organs). This is largely CORE in ¶3–¶6, with Q&A framing in ¶1–¶2 and a closing disciple-response in ¶9. ¶7–¶8 show late drift toward exhortation and Manichaean institutional labels (“elect”), consistent with the strong chapter-level editorial fatigue signal.*

They say that there is a dragon with fourteen heads, and it is gathered in and [ ... ] den with five refuges. What really is a corporeal dragon with fourteen heads? Or is it another spiritual lesson?

There is never a dragon in the flesh with fourteen heads, as the Magi say! Rather, this is a spiritual lesson. It was uttered in virginity [ ... ] the Magi did not understand [ ... ] all their words that are written [ ... spiri]tual (?).

Again, this is how this earth [ ... ] to it, as it is accounted a corporeal dragon [ ... ] I will instruct you [ ... ] the Dragon is this doctrine [ ... ] of the flesh [ ... ] also the fourteen heads that are distributed over the Dragon are these.

The seven sense organs on the head of the body are the two of sight by which one sees; the two of sound by which one hears; the two of scent by which one smells; also the tongue [ ... ] by which one selects, receiving there the different tastes that occur in every form.

These are the seven heads of the dragon, the seven sense organs that are on the upper part of the person; [ ... ] below. The seven of the torso are these: two [ ... ]; the two [ ... ] ruler [ ... ] the seven heads that are above; so with the ones below they total and make fourteen heads of the dragon.

Conversely, the five dens about which they have spoken, where the Dragon is congregated and protrudes, are these: the first is the tongue; the second is the lungs; the third is the heart; the fourth is the spleen; the fifth is the blood that dwells in them. Again, if it shall look out from them [and reveal its likeness and its observation above and below, it wages war by] its fourteen heads.

Whoever will recognise them with the dragon, which is the thought of the body, and struggles with it and is victorious and kills it in them He receives the victory without suffering on the day of his coming forth.

the dragon that exists with its fourteen heads and five dens, wherein it is concealed and hidden. It does through them [ ... / its des]ire all the time.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Q&A container and honorific address (“disciple… apostle… my master… beseech”) indicate FRAME, but the question itself is structurally specific (14 heads, 5 refuges) and likely preserves the topic-heading of an older teaching unit.
- ¶2: Dialogue attribution is a later compilation frame marker (“apostle says…”). The remaining content is definitional/clarificatory (spiritual vs. fleshly) and functions as the opening thesis of the teaching.
- ¶3: Impersonal explanatory voice moving into a numbered/partitioned exposition (“the fourteen heads… are these”) matches the correspondential substrate. “Dragon” is capitalized here as a personified operative force.
- ¶4: Pure microcosm structure: enumerated faculties/organs mapped as the “heads,” with sensory correspondences (sight/sound/scent/taste). No institutional or exhortative reach beyond the system.
- ¶5: Continuation of the systematic partition (upper vs. below; totals to fourteen). This is classic substrate list-building: structure completes itself without application to church life.
- ¶6: Highly systematic “five dens” enumeration mapped to internal organs, plus internal mechanics (“look out… wages war”)—still cosmos/body-system→system, not cosmos→institution. Capitalized “Dragon” as an agent.
- ¶7: This paragraph pivots from description to application: second-person/virtue framing (“whoever… struggles… victorious”) and especially the institutional term “elect” mark a later pastoralizing use of the teaching. The definitional clause (“the dragon… is the thought of the body”) reads as older substrate.
- ¶8: Summative restatement of the internal mechanism (concealed in dens; operates through desire) looks like substrate recap, but the “I have taught you… behold” closure is typical of compiled catechetical delivery and likely secondary.
- ¶9: Disciple’s reaction speech with blessing/praise (“My heart has been persuaded… Blessed is every one…”) is a classic hagiographic/edifying frame closure rather than system-description.

---

## Chapter 101
### [Concer]ning why, if the Person shall look down into Water, [ ... ]

*Dominantly correspondential-cosmological teaching: a natural phenomenon (reflection/shadow) is mapped onto cosmic process (the Summons descending to the Worlds of Darkness; the Obedience ascending to the Living Spirit; astral/zodiacal generation; embodiment from earth/abyss). Later compilation frame survives mainly as dialogue attributions and didactic address (“I reveal… to you”), which can be cleanly stripped. No clear NT overlay appears in the extant paragraphs; the main secondary layer here is the hagiographic dialogue frame and occasional application voice.*

When the person is above water and looking down [into the wat]ers, if he shall see his face reflected why shall you find his head turned upside down and also his feet upwards? Yet, when he stares down into the waters, his face shall not be visible, nor can he see it there?

Happen you know this, that this universe is established [of m]ystery, and is entirely full of mystery.

[And] this: The face of people and the shape of trees shall appear turned upside down in the water as this is signified in [the] mystery of the summons, when it was sent down to the worlds of darkness towards the First Man. Sin[ce ...] in this way it cast itself down [...] like a person [who] plunges headlong into water; so it is also with the summons. It cast itself headlong to the worlds below, with strength and diligence.

And this too: Shall the person lift his head up from the water, his face shall not be visible to him, being bent below. This characterises the mystery of the obedience that ascended from below, from with the First Man his father to the Living Spirit.

The face of people and of animals and of trees is visible in the water, hanging upside down. This occurs to the mystery of the stars and the zodiac, which hang upside down and are visible in the great sea. For the face of people and beasts and all trees hang on the root of the stars and the zodiac, being begotten from them. Just as their fathers, who are spread out above, hang upside down; so it is also that the mystery of their fathers is being revealed to them whenever they stare down into the water. This is, that you shall find their shapes inverted.

Not only this, but [the] child who is born; as the ho[ur] approaches for him to be born, he hangs upside down and is born head first. Indeed, due to this, his mystery shall be revealed in these waters people stare down into.

Behold, you will find the shadow of the person who walks along is joined to the earth all the time. If the person is troubled [...] his shadow up upon the earth [...] above. It is impossible for him to do [... ...] on the earth. See, this mystery is [a] great sign, signifying that the entire body came from the earth and had ascended from the abyss. Again, due to this, its shadow is joined on the earth, turned downwards, and hanging from above.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The “disciple/apostle” attribution is later frame; the bare problem statement about reflection functions as the teaching’s natural correspondential datum and can stand without the frame.
- ¶2: Frame marker “apostle says” is late; the aphoristic cosmological premise (“universe… full of mystery”) fits the impersonal teaching voice once detached from the dialogue wrapper.
- ¶3: Systematic cosmos→cosmos correspondence: inverted reflection → the Summons’ descent to the Worlds of Darkness/First Man; impersonal explanatory voice without institutional pointing.
- ¶4: Core mapping continues (reflection condition → Obedience ascending First Man→Living Spirit), but the closing self-referential address (“I revealed… to you”) signals later pedagogical/application voice.
- ¶5: Underlying substrate is a cosmological-astral correspondence (reflection inversion → stars/zodiac in the ‘great sea’ as generating ‘fathers’). The prefatory ‘listen/I may teach you’ is later instructional staging.
- ¶6: Natural-process correspondence (foetal inversion/birth) presented as a ‘mystery’ revealed through water-reflection; no institutional anchoring or citation formulas.
- ¶7: Core correspondential teaching: shadow-fastened-to-earth → body’s origin from earth/abyss and its ‘hanging’ relation. The initial ‘I reveal… to you’ is a late didactic seam, but the rest remains impersonal cosmological explanation.

---

## Chapter 103
### Concerning the Five Wonders that the Light Mind shall display in the Elect

*This chapter is dominated by institutional/ethical material (preaching, brethren, church discipline, legal excommunication) expressed as a fivefold list of virtues/signs in the Elect. Only one short paragraph (¶7) reads like an older cosmological-soteriological mechanism statement (Light Mind acting as an agent purifying/choosing the new man) without overt church/institutional anchoring. The opening uses a standard apostolic attribution frame.*

The Light Mind puts these five signs in the new man, [whom he has] purified, cleansed, supported and chosen [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic editorial introduction with dialogue attribution (“Once again the apostle speaks”). The content is programmatic and anchored to the community category “the elect,” not a cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶2: Virtue-list applied to the elect’s activity (“preach and proclaim”) in exhortative/institutional register; no cosmological mechanics.
- ¶3: Continues ethical/institutional sequence (faith arising in hearers after preaching). Community-facing moral pedagogy rather than systematic cosmology.
- ¶4: Application to “assembly of his brethren” and peace-making through preaching; explicitly communal/institutional anchoring (even with lacunae).
- ¶5: Ethical exhortation (“love … the brothers and sisters”) tied to the seated audience; not an internal cosmic correspondence.
- ¶6: Explicit church discipline and institutional vocabulary (“legal excommunication,” “church,” exclusion of people). Clear later pastoral/institutional layer.
- ¶7: Impersonal, agentive cosmological-soteriological claim: a named cosmic entity (Light Mind) effects purification/selection in the “new man,” stated as mechanism rather than church rule. Still somewhat anthropological, but not overtly anchored to contemporary institutions.
- ¶8: Beatitude/exhortation style (“Blessed is every one … is diligent”), oriented to personal conduct; not systematic cosmological exposition.
- ¶9: Paraenetic culmination with moralized metaphors (“faultless vessel,” “pearl without price”); reads as exhortative/application rhetoric (possibly echoing Gospel pearl imagery, but without explicit citation formula).

---

## Chapter 104
### Concerning Food: It shall be allocated to Five Products of the human Body.

*After a brief hagiographic opening, the chapter is a compact, systematic fivefold teaching: food entering the body is apportioned into five 'productions'. The voice is impersonal and expository, with numbered structure and no NT citation, institutional application, or exhortation—consistent with the older teaching substrate (cosmos/body mechanism rather than church practice).*

This sustenance of various kinds that people gather in and eat, and enters the body; it shall be distributed to five productions.

The first product is this that shall issue from the person in rapture and rises up in the Mind, and it comes out from all his limbs, and is immeasurable.

The second is this that shall issue from the person in voice and word.

The third is this that shall spring from them in strength and activity.

The fourth is what is engendered by the pleasure of lu[s]t in men and women.

The fifth is this that shall be formed and sculpted in the flesh, and be engendered and come from them. It is this corporeal creature; only its parents can recognise this creature that they beget. They are attentive to its desire and its thought and its [lo]ve every day, all the time. However, these other four forms they neither notice nor are concerned about; because they are not displayed.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue-frame incipit ('Once again... to his disciples'), characteristic of the compiled Kephalaia hagiographic wrapper.
- ¶2: Impersonal mechanism-statement introducing a numbered fivefold structure; no address, polemic, or institutional mapping.
- ¶3: Numbered exposition continuing the fivefold schema; body-to-faculty language ('Mind', limbs) presented descriptively rather than hortatively.
- ¶4: Plain continuation of the enumerated list; no later markers (no disciples/audience address, no ecclesial application).
- ¶5: Still within the closed fivefold production-series; abstract qualities stated as products without moralizing.
- ¶6: Enumerated mechanism statement; despite sexual vocabulary, it remains descriptive (what is engendered) rather than disciplinary command.
- ¶7: Completes the fivefold set and adds an impersonal observational contrast (visible child vs. 'not displayed' productions). No seam into church practice or exhortation; remains within the teaching’s explanatory register.

---

## Chapter 106
### There is no Joy that shall remain in the World till the End.

*Aside from a brief formulaic opening attribution, this chapter is a continuous, systematic cosmological exposition: during the time of mixture even the Sun and Moon cannot rejoice fully, because they behold (1) the Enemy’s rule in the cosmos and (2) the Living Soul’s ongoing oppression until final cleansing and fettering of Death.*

There is not [o]ne person [existing in] the world, standing firm [i]n mixture, for whom joy remains to the end.

Not only people, even the Sun and the Moon, the light-givers of the heavens, [ ... ] nor are they oppressed, they know not [ ... s]et firm in a summons and an [obe]dience. Nevertheless, they too, they do not rejoice completely.

So, one: Their eyes stare at their Enemy, they [perceiv]e him and also look at his ugly shapes. They [s]ee him, that he is alive and established and is king above and below, in secret and manifest. He does every thing that he wants according to his wil[1].

[The s]econd: They perceive the Living Soul, they see [i]t and that it is ensnared and set in a great [fetter] above and below, in the tree and in the flesh, [ ... ] with every oppression. It is being pressed, drawn near to [and] sliced and eaten as it comes up and down; from above below and from below above. It [is] despoiled and moved from body to body.

Now, because of their Living Soul that is loved by them and honoured of them, when they see it in oppression th[ey can] not [re]joice completely.

So, one about the soul; and one about their Enemy whom they see ruling [in] what is not his own. He is proud and satisfied with a wealth that is not his own. Indeed, due to this, they do not rejoice in total; but bear up and swallow their heart until they cleanse the Living Soul and take it from the Enemy's hands. Then they obliterate the Death that is their enemy and they gather it in [and fetter it in the lump] for ever and ever.

When they will chain their Enemy in the bond at the [las]t, then they will rejoice and no longer grieve from this t[ime]. Also, they become the very same as they were from the [start] for ever!

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with a standard Kephalaia frame formula (later editorial container), but the aphoristic teaching that follows is impersonal and cosmological (mixture-time condition), with no institutional or NT anchoring.
- ¶2: Cosmic-persona teaching: Sun/Moon as heavenly agents within the system; no citation, no audience-directed exhortation, no institutional ‘pointing at’.
- ¶3: Numbered, impersonal reason-giving within the cosmological drama; the Enemy is described as a reigning cosmic power (system-internal mapping of rule ‘above/below; secret/manifest’).
- ¶4: Systematic cosmological mechanism: the Living Soul’s bondage and transmigratory processing through ‘tree/flesh’ and bodies—expository voice, no polemical or church-application turn.
- ¶5: Brief inferential link (‘Now, because…’) that remains wholly within the cosmic agents’ affective state toward the Living Soul; no editorial seam into community practice.
- ¶6: Recapitulates the two cosmic reasons and moves into eschatological process (cleansing/extraction/fettering). Despite the emotive phrasing, it remains system-internal (no institutional targets or exhortation to the audience).
- ¶7: Pure cosmological eschaton: final binding of the Enemy and restoration ‘as from the start.’ No frame, no church application, no NT overlay.

---

## Chapter 107
### Concerning the Form of the Word, that [ ... 30 ... ]

*The chapter is essentially a compact correspondential/mechanistic teaching on how a spoken word is produced through five bodily members, reinforced by an analogy to five craftsmen producing a coin. Only the initial discourse-attribution formula in ¶1 belongs to the later hagiographic frame; the rest is impersonal, structural exposition without institutional application or NT citation.*

the production of the word t[hat comes] from the mouth and is heard by the [ears]. [ ... ] and the throat draws it up and the tongue spreads it out and the teeth cut it and the lips g[at]her it! The word shall come forth through the power of th[ese five] members and be heard outside.

Simila[rly] the coin: One shall pour it out and an [othe]r beat it and another trim it as it is turned, and a[nother] put the stamp on it and another wipe it in the sieve (?). [Behold], these five craftsmen shall shape and beautify [their] coin, and it comes amongst mankind. It becomes a posse[ssion] to be received and given.

This is also the case with the [wor]d, as it is formed and embellished by five [member]s. It comes forth and is heard by the ears [of] others.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening formula “Once again he speaks … He says” marks the compilational frame, but the content that follows is a structured five-member mechanism (throat/tongue/teeth/lips etc.), characteristic of the older impersonal teaching voice.
- ¶2: Impersonal analogical exposition extending the fivefold structure (five craftsmen/actions) with no institutional targeting or citation; functions as internal correspondential pedagogy rather than later pastoral application.
- ¶3: Concluding recapitulation in the same structural voice, closing the fivefold mapping without frame markers, exhortation, or external (church/NT) reference.

---

## Chapter 108
### Concerning the Seed Grain that shall be formed by the Elements, and also be destroyed by them

*After a brief hagiographic dialogue header, the chapter is largely a compact cosmological teaching using the seed-grain/tree as a correspondential image for elemental formation and blight in the mixture. One paragraph (¶3) pivots from cosmic description to ecclesial actors (“teachers and the elect”), indicating a later institutional application grafted onto an older correspondential line.*

“This seed grain and this barley grain that you see shall be formed and [...] and beautified by the five elements. Now, the warmth and the cold nourish the seed grain and the entire tree. So, just as these things nourish it [...] it is destroyed [...] through [...] hunger.

“In this way also Righteousness gathers the five to it.

“In this way too, a lack and a shortfall shall occur from place to place through them; and they who have chosen it have gathered it in and beautified and attained it, while an affliction shall arise for it through those.

“People also shall be caused to stumble on their account, through the energy of Matter that dwells in them. It has poured its shadow over them, just as it shall also pour its shadow over the elements and destroy the fruits of all the trees.”

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue apparatus: “apostle”, “builder of the church”, and the setting “speaks to his disciples” are compilation-frame markers.
- ¶2: Impersonal process-teaching: elements as formative/nourishing agencies and the same system as destructive agency; cosmos→cosmos mapping (elements ↔ growth/decay) without institutional address.
- ¶3: Begins in the same systemic explanatory voice (“Righteousness gathers the five”), then pivots to ecclesial-identificatory language (“teachers and the elect”), indicating later pastoral/application use of the correspondence.
- ¶4: Continues the impersonal cycle logic (gain here implies deprivation elsewhere; affliction arising through the same agencies). No explicit institutional vocabulary; reads as a systemic consequence within the world of mixture (though the pronouns could be read as continuing the prior application).
- ¶5: Cosmological agency language: Matter as an indwelling force with causal efficacy (“poured its shadow”), applied consistently to humans and elements within the same mythic-physical system (cosmos→cosmos mechanism, not exhortation or citation).

---

## Chapter 109
### Concerning the Fifty Lord's Days; to what Mysteries do they correspond? Or the Second (Fifty); to whose Sign (are they)?

*The chapter opens with a formal Q&A frame about institutional fasting (catechumens/elect). The oldest layer appears chiefly in ¶2–¶4 as a numerical-cosmological exposition (five garments × five powers → twenty-five limbs/sons; ascent of the Summons/Answer/Obedience; living food). The second half (¶5–¶8) drifts strongly into institutional legitimation and then explicitly NT/apostolic exempla (apostles, Christ’s temptation, Simon the leper, tomb), matching the chapter’s high editorial-fatigue signal.*

Happen you know that [in eac]h one of these five garments that the [First] Man summoned there are five powers. They shall be counted to the [ ... each] other, these five, and five times [ ... ] their number. However, at the time [ ... ] the Summons went down. The Answer wished to ascend with it. And the Summons with the Obedience [constitute] twenty-five characteristics in their twenty-five limbs, [which] are the sons of the Man. The Summons and [the Obed]ience were raised to the heights through them.

Just as their fa[ther] the Living Man raised himself up through them, by these these other ones have set up all things a[bov]e and below. Indeed, these are what are called pentecost, the fifty great days. They fasted from their [foo]d, [w]hich is their own.

So, from that time until [the time] of [the adv]ent of Jesus the Splendour, the glorious one who came [ ... / ... t]heir food which is the [ ... ] of sou[l], which is the Summons and the Obedience [ ... ] that spiritual food. They rele[ased ... ] all these fifty days, which [ ... ] this living food suffices for all of them. It filled them all with power and richness and life. Light [ ... ] this living food that [ ... ] became rich.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue frame with disciple-question formula (‘I beseech you, my master’), plus explicit institutional categories (catechumens/elect) anchoring the topic in community practice rather than cosmic mechanism.
- ¶2: Core substrate shows systematic numbered correspondences (five garments × five powers; twenty-five limbs/sons) and impersonal cosmological mechanics (descent/ascent of Summons/Answer/Obedience). The only later material is the brief speech-attribution wrapper.
- ¶3: Begins in a cosmological register (Living Man; cosmic ‘setting up’ above/below) but then is used to ground ‘holy days’ terminology; the excised clause is an editorial move from cosmic description to institutional calendrics.
- ¶4: Sustained cosmological exposition: ‘food’ is defined internally as the Summons/Obedience (cosmic agencies), and the effects are described as power/richness/life—mechanics within the Light-world economy, not institutional regulation.
- ¶5: Moves from cosmological counting to institutional calendrics and practice (‘one hundred holy days’, ‘elect fast’), using the mysteries as justification for community observance—cosmos → church application.
- ¶6: Explicitly legitimates the fasts via other ‘sects’ and apostolic precedent (‘apostles themselves fasted… revealed them to their disciples’). This is Christian/apostolic overlay language entering via post-NT ecclesial tradition.
- ¶7: Direct NT narrative exempla: Christ tempted by the devil, Simon the leper, three days in the sepulchre—material that enters the Kephalaia through Gospel tradition.
- ¶8: Institutional distribution and justification (‘bestowed… on the entire church’, catechumens/elect) explicitly applies the mysteries to church practice; even with ‘First Man/second man’ language, the paragraph’s function is ecclesial policy/interpretation.

---

## Chapter 110
### Concerning the Nourishment of the Person, for there are Powers in it [ ... / ... ]

*This short chapter is dominated by systematic cosmological-physiological teaching (body as a purification apparatus; enumerated inner powers; cleansing/expulsion; transmigration mechanics). The only clear later material is a brief dialogue/frame attribution at the start of ¶1, which can be cleanly removed without damaging the teaching sequence.*

daily to the body of the person [ ... ] there are two hundred and fifty thousand seals in it [ ... ] there are another two hundred and fifty thousand rulers [ ... ] they are cleansed and purified and enter [ ... ] by the voice, by the word, and by the [ ... ... ] quiet with the silence at the time when his heart [ ... ... ] of God the Father.

Also, the twelve [ ... ... ] rulers that belong to the Darkness shall be swept out outside in wandering and in transmigration [ ... ... ] some of them [ ... through] this transmigration, and others among them shall be brought back.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core teaching is the impersonal, enumerative physiological-cosmological mechanism (seals/rulers; cleansing/purification; entry by voice/word/silence). The opening "Once again speaks..." is a compilation-frame marker; the remaining material stays within cosmic mechanics rather than exhortation or institutional application.
- ¶2: Systematic cosmological process-description (twelve rulers of Darkness; expulsion; wandering/transmigration/return) with no citation formulas, no audience address, and no institutional application—characteristic of the older teaching stratum.

---

## Chapter 111
### Concerning the Four Archetypes that occur in the Eye, and the Fifth that is hidden in them; to whom do they belong?

*Highly fragmentary chapter. What survives is mainly a cosmological-correspondential teaching about perception (eye/pupil), fourfold structure plus a hidden fifth, and elemental/ontological assignments (Fire, Abyss/Darkness, Living Air/Light). The only clear later layer is the recurring dialogue-attribution formula (“Once again he speaks:”) plus brief audience-address/explanation rhetoric in ¶3; there is no institutional/pastoral extension and no NT overlay.*

There are four aspects among mankind that perceive, as they perceive [...] without these four that are hidden in them [...] they belong [...]. So, [...].

[...] that is in the [...] belongs to the fire. The Darkness belongs to the abyss; but the Light that perceives what is hidden in all these things, being clear, is the Living Air.

The pupil of the eye shall not [...] the mystery of the [...] is the door [...] below [...] the other four cardinal points. For in the [...] above. He has [...] great [...] they came down [...] living [...]. The First Man came forth from it and knew all things.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The surviving teaching is a bare structural claim (four perceiving aspects + a hidden element), consistent with older degree-mapping; but it is introduced by a compilation frame formula, so the paragraph is mixed.
- ¶2: Pure correspondential assignment within the cosmological system (elements/realms ↔ perceptive principle), with no audience address, no institutional application, and no citation apparatus—typical of the core teaching layer.
- ¶3: Underlying core content is systematic and cosmological (pupil/door, four cardinal points with an ‘above/below’ architecture, descent, and the First Man as a defined cosmic agent). The opening is a didactic frame with direct audience-address that likely entered with the later compilation layer.

---

## Chapter 112
### The Human is less than all the Things of the Universe, and he is rebellious beyond them all.

*This chapter is dominated by hagiographic travel narrative and polemical/theodicy application (humanity’s blasphemy; denying that demons come from God). The oldest cosmological substrate appears only in brief embedded fragments: (a) Jesus’ non-anthropocentric, cosmic ‘task’ in the great universe and his revelation to Adam and Eve; (b) compressed cosmology items (aeons/Greatness, Light vs Darkness, descent to the province of death) preserved inside later catechetical list-format; and (c) a short cosmological description of the Cross of Light embedded in a moral injunction. No paragraph is “pure core” without later framing; the core survives mostly as extractable fragments inside later catechesis.*

Jesus did not come and s[a]ve the world because of mankind alone, but [ ... he] came and revealed on earth [ ... ] was strong outside in [ ... ] And [whe]n he had finished doing his [t]ask outside [i]n the great u[ni]verse, he came [ ... he wen]t further with Adam and Eve and revealed [to them].

they belong to the race of Ligh[t].

the aeons [of the greatness], how they occur; and [the manner of] the Darkness, how it t[o]o exists.

the [L]ight great-[ness], how it has been active against the pow[er of] Darkness and conquered it.

he came to the province of death [ ... ... ] that is flesh, until he should find profit and bring them from the gate of the underworld below, for [they had] been swa[ll]owed into it.

the rebels shall be bound in a great fetter, they who shall [r]ebel against the g[o]od and against the righteousness

the Cross of Light, which g[rie]ves in the totality, being present in what is visible and what is not vis[ib]le.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic itinerary scene with the title “apostle” and narrative staging; functions as editorial container, not cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Continues the illustrative story (measurement comparison) in a narrated setting; present-time anchor (“today”) and anecdotal rhetoric. The phrase “five wo[r]ds of the f]lesh” may echo an older scheme, but it is too embedded in the later exemplum to extract securely.
- ¶3: Application/theodicy rhetoric contrasting mute creation with human blasphemy; not systematic cosmos→cosmos mapping but moralizing/polemical discourse directed at contemporary belief about evil.
- ¶4: Explicit polemic against attributing rulers/demons to God; maps doctrine onto contemporary accusations (“they say…”), crossing from being-description to argumentative application.
- ¶5: Classic dialogue prompt (“one of his disciples… Tell us, our master”)—editorial Q&A apparatus.
- ¶6: NT-vector material: Jesus as “son of the Living God,” crucifixion, cross, persecution—dependent on Christian passion tradition rather than internal cosmological exposition.
- ¶7: Core fragment is cosmological and non-institutional (Jesus’ ‘task’ in the great universe; revelation to primordial humans). The surrounding apostolic-lineage and list-introduction is later catechetical framing.
- ¶8: Old doctrinal identity formula (“race of Light”) preserved as a bullet-point inside a later ‘five things’ catechetical list.
- ¶9: Cosmos-internal ontological topics (aeons/Greatness; Darkness’ mode of existence) indicate substrate teaching, but they enter here via a later list format about what was ‘revealed to them’.
- ¶10: Mythic-mechanical Light vs Darkness conquest belongs to the cosmological substrate, but is preserved in an enumerated catechism-style item.
- ¶11: Cosmological-soteriological mechanics (descent to the ‘province of death’/flesh; extraction from underworld gate) read as older mythic teaching embedded in later instructional enumeration.
- ¶12: Eschatological binding motif can belong to core mechanics, but the paragraph in situ is a catechetical ‘fifth item’ and is explicitly tied to apostolic proclamation (late authority anchor).
- ¶13: Dialogue attribution (“Once [again] the apostle speaks”) plus catechetical announcement about ‘the faithful’ indicates later instructional framing, not substrate exposition.
- ¶14: Catechetical identity marking of ‘the faithful’ in list format; community-facing instruction rather than impersonal cosmological architecture.
- ¶15: Institutional authority marker (“the [a]postle who has been manifested”) and community faith confession; not cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶16: The imperative ethical layer (‘restraint and charity’) is later pastoral application, but the Cross of Light as a cosmic, all-pervasive structure (‘visible and not visible’) is a substrate cosmological datum.

---

## Chapter 113
### The Chapter on whether any [Lig]ht comes from the Three Vessels.

*This chapter is almost entirely a compact cosmological-mechanistic exposition (light-rays entering via gates/doors, firmament-by-firmament clarification, division into an taken-up portion vs cast-down portion). The only clearly later material is the dialogue frame and question prompt in ¶1, plus the brief dialogue attribution introducing the teaching in ¶2.*

[ ... ] b[u]t should the [ ... ] ascend [ ... ] light of the rays that [ ... ] which are received, which shine and go in through these great open gates, through these doors, firmament by firmament.

For four [gates o]pen in each world, distributed at the four points of the compass, opening downwards to the vessels. So, due to this, the [light of the] rays shall go in through these openings and it shines on [that] place, and pulls through them the light and the little pi[llar (?) that ex]ists there.

From that place [it] shall be poured [out to the] firmaments. It shall also be clarified in these firmaments [ ... ] a part there is cleansed and it is [all] taken up with the living ones. However, its other part [ ... ] shall all [be cast] down.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue frame marker: "Once again the disciples questioned our enlightener. They say:" The content is a topic request rather than an expository mechanism; despite cosmological nouns (three vessels, universe), it functions as editorial framing.
- ¶2: Core mechanistic exposition (rays received; entering through gates/doors; ascent through firmaments) introduced by a Mani-compiler dialogue tag using "apostle" (frame), indicating an older teaching unit embedded in later attribution.
- ¶3: Systematic cosmological architecture (four gates per world; four compass points; downward openings to the vessels) described in impersonal, structural voice; mapping stays within the cosmic system.
- ¶4: Process-description of purification/refinement across firmaments with a twofold sorting outcome (taken up with the living ones vs cast down). No citation, exhortation, or institutional anchoring; entirely mechanistic/cosmological.

---

## Chapter 114
### Concerning the Three Images that are in the righteous Person.

*Nearly the entire chapter is systematic anthropological-cosmological process teaching: a threefold structure (spiritual/psychic/corporeal) and the mechanics of purification of the living soul through these degrees, culminating in the action of the Light Virgin/Virgin of Light. Only the brief hagiographic speech-introduction is later frame.*

Three images occur in the elect person.

The first is the spiritual image, which is the new man, which the Light Mind shall form in him; and it goes into him and dwells in him.

The second image is the remnant and remainder of the new man, which is the psychic image that is bound in the flesh, as the old man [ ... since the] times of this psychic image.

The other one is the corporeal image [that is] added to them all.

These three images [ ... ] entirely. When it will [ ... ... ] through the nourishment of food. If it shall sink [ ... ... ] down to the corporeal image. [And] the corporeal, [that] is in it, shall divest itself of it and place it in the [ ... ] image. [ ... ] again it rises up and falls to the [ ... ] image; it shall divest itself of the psychical of the enemies, which [ ... ... ] anger. The lust [ ... ... ... ] with it.

Then shall a Light Virgin come and reveal the spiritual image that is [there], which [is] the new man. That virgin acts as a guide. [She g]oes on before and it is extended to the heights above, and received into this spiritual image. And she [sculpts] it and adorns it with the new man [wi]thin. It is sealed with all the limbs of this Light Virgin who is present and dwells in the new man.

So, this is [h]ow this living limb shall be [puri]fied and live, the one that comes into the body of [the ri]ghteous one from without through the administration of food of various ki[nds], like this. The living soul shall be clea[n]sed entirely every [d]ay and traverse these three [im]a[ges].

So, it shall divest itself of the body, which is not its own, in the corporeal. It shall also divest itself of the souls that are not its own, these that are mixed with it in the psychical [ ... ... ] anger and desire and [ ... ]-ness and foolishness and envy and strife; and these other wicked teachings that are not its own.

However, in [the] spiritual image itself it shall live and be joined with [lo]ng-sufferingness, the perfection of faith and love that reigns over them all. It is the Virgin of Light who robes the new man and who shall be cal[l]ed ‘the hour of life’. S[he] is the f[irst], but s[h]e is also the last.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic dialogue frame (“Once again our enlightener speaks...”), not part of the teaching substrate.
- ¶2: Programmatic doctrinal heading for the ensuing threefold degree-teaching; impersonal and structural rather than exhortative.
- ¶3: Degree-mapping within the system (image → new man → Light Mind formation), expository voice.
- ¶4: Continues the numbered degree-structure; contrasts new/old man within an internal anthropological-cosmic schema.
- ¶5: Completes the threefold set; terse structural statement typical of a teaching list.
- ¶6: Process mechanics (descent/ascent; divestment/transfer) describing what happens through degrees; still cosmos-within-body rather than institutional exhortation.
- ¶7: System-internal cosmological agent (Light Virgin) functioning in a defined soteriological mechanism (guiding, sculpting, sealing).
- ¶8: Summative explanation of the purification mechanism via food and traversal of the three images; remains descriptive rather than prescriptive.
- ¶9: Continuing process-description of separation from mixture (not-its-own accretions); vice-list functions as what is shed, not a moral command.
- ¶10: Climactic completion of the threefold ascent in the spiritual image, with the Virgin of Light as operative perfection/robing agent; still expository and system-internal (no Gospel citation formula).

---

## Chapter 115
### The Catechumen asks the Apostle: will Rest come about for Someone who has come out of the Body, if the Saints pray over and make an Alms-offering for him?

*The chapter is framed as a catechumen’s question about alms/prayer for the dead; the second half shows strong institutional/pastoral drift (editorial fatigue). The oldest recoverable substrate is preserved mainly in two archetypal “first/second entreaty” cosmological episodes (Mother of Life→Living Spirit; gods→Third Ambassador), plus a fragmented “third entreaty” sequence including Jesus the Splendour as a cosmic being. Later hands repeatedly pivot from cosmos→cosmos narration to cosmos→church practice (elect/catechumens, alms, remembrance, holy church), often as summary/charge paragraphs; a seam-flagged institutional extension appears at ¶42.*

For this entreaty has been since the beginning [...] the gods first made entreaty of their Father. They were granted their entreaty and their request.

The Great Spirit, the Mother of Life, she claimed and prayed and besought and glorified and praised the first established one, who is the Father. She besought him an entreaty. She claimed of him a request. She received a great gift; she and the many powers with her.

She besought an entreaty on behalf of the First Man, because of how he had come forth and thrown himself down. He became distant from the Mother of Life. He separated from her and gave himself; in great affliction. He joined war on earth with the destruction and the grief and the weakness; in the worlds of Darkness; in the abysses made of fear; in the midst of the demons' ditch; amongst the powers of the Devil!

Indeed, he is like a king standing in the midst of his enemies. And when the Mother of Life petitioned and prayed and glorified and praised the Father, the first established one, she did petition him that he might send a power; a protector and redeemer and helper for the son of God who is set in affliction.

Now, just as she had besought and interceded, so likewise was her prayer fulfilled and received into the presence of the Father of Greatness. He turned and gave to her her entreaty: the great power, the Living Spirit, the giver of ease. The Mother of Life and the Living Spirit came with great power to the borders of the territory. They brought the First Man up from that war and struggle, wherein he is set.

He gave himself as a grace for the Mother of Life. He became estranged and far from her, the one whom his enemies (place) in great affliction, the one who is in the land of Darkness since he became weak.

The Living Spirit grasped and took him in. He freed him. He set him right and gave him ease in the land of rest [...] the Living Spirit, the first giver of ease [...] the will of these three. The welcomer [...] find with the welcomer that [...

[...] a great power [...] of the loved one [...] the First Man who had been far away. He had become distant. She had besought on his behalf. He was set fast in affliction. (The Living Spirit) went to the place of destruction and weakness, the one that freed and redeemed him and gave him ease from the affliction. He set him right with rest and joy. He has himself become rested, with him for ever.

[...] and they built the universe and completed it with its firmaments, with its wheels, with the ships of light that are in the heights, with its fastenings, with its earths and its walls, with its vessels. They completed the universe and set it right by the fathers of light.

Then, the Great Spirit stood with the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder with the Living Spirit and the First Man. These five stood to prayer; they glorified and praised the Father, the first established one. They besought of him an entreaty. They claimed of him a request, that he might give them the power [...] the leader, a guide to all the things of activity that they had constructed. That he might come and purify the living soul that is set in affliction.

They prayed and besought their Father, and he received from them their prayer [...] he gave them their entreaty and their request [...] he summoned from him [...] which is the Third Ambassador. He, he came from the power of his greatness. He saw the entire soul, how it is entangled in a chain and set fast. It sees the things of activity that his brothers have constructed, that have come from the heights. Again, he saw the living soul, that it is entangled and set fast in a great torture. It is oppressed in the stink of the abyss that is joined with it.

And therefore, he, the Ambassador [...] in his charity. So, he revealed his way, before this underworld that is dug deep down, to free the living soul. He redeemed it from all the fleshes of death wherein it is pained. He gave it ease. He set it right in the place of rest and joy.

And again, he too, the Ambassador, he fulfilled the will of these three; the will of his brothers who had besought the Father for him. They claimed of him a request, together with the [...] of the Father, the one that [...] he gave the entreaty that they had besought of him.

The third is the transferral of the living soul that he had freed. He released it and gave it ease from the affliction. He became king and first to the entire body.

Again, let us come to the third entreaty when they besought him the third time [...] displayed his image [...] he hid his image from the [...] he stood firm [...] they revealed [...] the light [...] the First Man [...] mock [...] to him in [...] which is exalted [...] garment [...] they were freed [...] to him [...] their voice [...] the body [...] prayer [...] it [...] beseech [...] the body [...] beseech [...] she besought [...] in [...] be free [...] Ambassador [...] and also [...]

[...] with the living ones and [...] for ever [...] the first [...] from the [...] he gave him the hope [...]

Also, Jesus the Splendour perfected the will [...] the will [...] they found the light [...] the will of the [...] that he had [...] he gave them the power that is Jesus the Splendour. He granted the will of the elements. [...] for ever. This first [...] he was humbled, he was made free [...] the [...]-ness is displayed to his enemies. He received the hope [...] reveal [...] Jesus the Splendour, the light power [...] and the entreaty of the [...] of light [...]

The first: [...] of the living soul that is entangled and bound in the entire universe. For it shall be freed and cleansed and purified and redeemed

It is redeemed and purified and attains its first essence.

this is how this soul shall find ease and escape that body. It goes into the land of light [...] soul that was redeemed because of it [...] comes about for it; and it goes to the land of life and rest [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic scene-setting (“Once again… our enlightener… congregation”) is hagiographic framing.
- ¶2: Petition/Q&A formula (“I beseech you, my master… recount…”) belongs to the compiling dialogue frame.
- ¶3: Question continues in catechumen voice; doctrinal tension posed, not cosmological exposition.
- ¶4: Reiterated request to be instructed; social anchoring (“honoured amongst people”) signals editorial present.
- ¶5: Dialogue attribution (“Then the apostle speaks…”) plus institutional categories (elect) and practical promise of answered prayer; not cosmos→cosmos mapping.
- ¶6: Opens with cosmological antiquity claim (gods entreating the Father) consistent with archetypal myth; then turns to church establishment and Christ wording (later application/overlay).
- ¶7: Rhetorical transition in first-person instruction without cosmological content; functions as editorial teaching scaffolding.
- ¶8: Archetypal cosmological episode; named emanational beings in systematic mythic action (cosmos→cosmos).
- ¶9: Pure cosmological narrative of the First Man’s descent/war in Darkness; no institutional targeting.
- ¶10: Continues the archetypal supplication logic within the emanational system; “son of God” appears as a cosmic-role epithet here rather than an NT citation.
- ¶11: Systematic emanational response: Father of Greatness→Living Spirit; impersonal mechanism of rescue (cosmos→cosmos).
- ¶12: Cosmic plight and estrangement in Darkness; no church-language.
- ¶13: Emanational rescue and relocation into “land of rest”; structural cosmology (despite lacunae).
- ¶14: Completes the first archetypal “entreaty→rescue→rest” pattern internally to the cosmos.
- ¶15: Didactic recap in first person (“See now, I have taught you…”); functions as editorial structuring rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶16: Explicit pivot to contemporary practice (“faithful saints… over the one who has been released from his body”); cosmos used to authorize church intercession.
- ¶17: Constituent parts of the universe listed in cosmological register (firmaments/wheels/ships of light).
- ¶18: Second archetypal supplication: enumerated cosmic actors (“these five”) requesting a salvific leader; still cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶19: Emanational grant (Third Ambassador) and diagnosis of the soul’s entanglement; mechanistic cosmology rather than moral exhortation.
- ¶20: Cosmic descent/way-revelation and extraction of the living soul; “fleshes of death” is mythic-cosmological, not institutional.
- ¶21: Continues internal “will/entreaty/request” mechanics among cosmic triads and the Father.
- ¶22: Cosmological result statement (“transferral of the living soul”); no church anchoring.
- ¶23: First-person recap marker (“Behold, I have instructed you…”) signals editorial organization of exempla.
- ¶24: Institutional application to “perfect elect… catechumen… prayer in faith”; explicitly cosmos→community practice.
- ¶25: Though highly lacunose, it remains within mythic/cosmic action (image/light/First Man/Ambassador) rather than church discipline.
- ¶26: Fragmentary continuation of the mythic sequence; no institutional markers survive in the readable bits.
- ¶27: Jesus the Splendour appears as a defined cosmological power (not an NT citation); the passage stays within emanational will/power/elements.
- ¶28: Present-tense didactic address (“Happen you know this… you are set firm…”) without new cosmological mapping; likely editorial linkage.
- ¶29: Institutional anchor (“petition of the holy church”) in a fragment; cosmos→church language.
- ¶30: Directly about alms/remembrance for the dead; practice-focused rather than cosmological mechanism.
- ¶31: Preserves a core cosmological claim (living soul bound in the universe; liberation/purification) embedded in a later list of ‘victories’ attached to alms-for-the-dead practice.
- ¶32: Explicit ecclesial benefit (“rest for the holy church… children of the church”) and alms logic; editorial institutional layer.
- ¶33: Too fragmentary, but framed as a ‘victory’ list in the alms context; no recoverable cosmos-only substrate here.
- ¶34: Household/kinship examples and remembrance/alms practice; institutional-social anchoring.
- ¶35: Continues catechumen/alms logic and sin-remission framing; late practical layer.
- ¶36: Fragmentary but dominated by kinship/sickness/sin/catechumen vocabulary; not systematic cosmology.
- ¶37: Contains explicit Christian token (“Cross”) and catechumen/institutional logic; entered through Christian-Manichaean synthesis language rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶38: Extended human-legal analogy (bound person, chief) for intercession; not cosmic architecture.
- ¶39: Too fragmentary; sits inside the analogy sequence.
- ¶40: Dialogue tag plus moral/legal analogy about sin and intercession; late explanatory layer.
- ¶41: Continues charity-as-payment analogy among ‘masters’; practical/social logic, not cosmological mapping.
- ¶42: Seam-flagged editorial extension: explicit catechumen/household/church/remembrance/holy church language applying the teaching to institutional ritual.
- ¶43: A brief cosmos-only redemption formula survives (purification; return to ‘first essence’) inside a later intercession explanation sequence following an institutional seam.
- ¶44: Contains a recognizably old ascent register (escape body; land of Light/life/rest) embedded in a late intercession argument.
- ¶45: Strong institutional charge to catechumens with alms/remembrances and pastoral exhortation; dialogue attribution and practice directives dominate.
- ¶46: Formulaic closing response (praise/obeisance/thanks) is hagiographic frame material.

## Chapter 116
### Concerning why if a [Nail] is cut the Person shall not be sick; but if a Limb is cut he shall immediately be pained.

*This chapter is largely dialogue frame (disciple question + apostolic response formulae + closing). The only recoverable older teaching-like content is a short correspondential analogy (body↔earth; limbs↔trees; regrowth varieties) explaining why some cuttings cause pain and others do not. It stays within natural/microcosmic correspondences and does not pivot to church institutions or Gospel citation, but it lacks the distinctive systematic cosmological architecture found in the strongest CORE strata; hence it is treated as a core fragment embedded in a later instructional frame.*

Happen you know th[at the] body is like the earth, wh[ile these] limbs als[o ...] of it are like the trees. There are varieties [...] of tree you shall find growing on the earth. They ar[is]e and grow again and stand firm. They have not [... ...] Again, there are others that if they are cut shall not [grow ...] and they grow, as they are li[ke ...] which shall be cut and grow [...] and [...] they too that occur [...] they shall be cut and not grow, resembling [... neither] grow nor shall they [... ...] it [sh]all please him on [...] and grows [...] in impurity [... ...] from him. This flesh that is corrupt [... ...] in the liv[in]g flesh and they [... ...] are somewhat sick.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic dialogue frame (“Once again… questioned the apostle”). Hagiographic/compilational wrapper.
- ¶2: Disciple’s addressed question (“Tell me, my master…”) with physiological setup; functions as Q-frame rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶3: Beseeching/request formula; pure dialogue apparatus.
- ¶4: Speech attribution (“Then the apostle speaks…”); explicit Layer-2 dialogue marker.
- ¶5: Underlying content is a correspondential microcosm analogy (body↔earth; limbs↔trees; cut/regrow vs cut/not-regrow) stated as ‘how it works’ without institutional application or NT citation. The opening is clearly dialogue-frame; hence MIXED.
- ¶6: Closing self-referential teaching-completion formula (“Behold, I have taught you…”); functions as editorial closure rather than substrate exposition.
- ¶7: Narrative reaction tag (“disciple heard… he sat down”); hagiographic closure.

## Chapter 117
### Concerning why Some shall delay to come forth from the Body, and Others die in an instant.

*Extremely fragmentary kephalaion dominated by dialogue frame/questions. Only ¶2 preserves a non-framing, enumerative explanation ("The second...") that likely belonged to the older systematic teaching about differentiated death/departure timings, but the cosmological mechanism is lost in lacunae; classification remains tentative.*

The [sec]ond [ ... ] if he shall fall [ ... ... ] one [ ... ... ] one hour [ ... ... ] others who shall [ ... ... ] and they remain. They shall delay until they [ ... ... ] he is freed, and they delay to come toward [ ... ... ]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Hagiographic/dialogue frame: disciples address "the apostle" and "our master" with a beseeching question formula; this is compilation frame language rather than impersonal teaching.
- ¶2: Non-framing, impersonal enumerative structure ("The second...") consistent with an underlying systematic explanation distinguishing cases (immediate vs delayed release). Very lacunose; lack of explicit cosmological vocabulary may be due to damage rather than late composition.
- ¶3: Repetition of beseeching address to "our master" asking for instruction; Q&A framing typical of the Kephalaia editorial container.
- ¶4: Dialogue attribution formula ("Then he says...") without preserved teaching content; such attributions are part of the later compilation frame.

No stable frame formulas, institutional/pastoral material, or NT-citation markers survive in the extant text; what remains looks like broken teaching prose (light/universe, possible numerical structuring: “seven…”, “these other three”). Any layering judgment is therefore highly uncertain and based mainly on absence of late markers rather than positive core structure.*

[ ... 1º ... ] upon [ ... ] from [ ... ] thus [ ... ]

[ ... 5 a]ll of them [ ... 11 ... ] that they shall [ ... 14 ... ] seven people [ ... 15 ... ] Again, he too, this per[son ... ] the time when [ ... ] of the light [ ... 19 ... ] the universe [ ... 2º ... ] is revealed [ ... ] take to the light [ ... utt]erly, the way that he exists [ ... 25 ... ] from [ ... ] these other three [ ... ]

## Chapter 119
### Chapter 119: [ ... ]

*The chapter preserves several mythic/cosmological statements about the First Man (approval in the Aeons of Light; sinlessness; steadfastness amid the worlds of Darkness; purification/separation work). But the core is repeatedly repurposed into an exemplar for the community (address to “my brothers and my limbs,” catechumen language, standing firm in the body), and the chapter ends with a clear hagiographic closing. Editorial fatigue is visible: the mid–late paragraphs shift from cosmological description to exhortation and institutional identity-markers.*

[ ... ] and the [righte]o[usness ... ] is more than [the ... ] the voice through them.

This one, whom they called “approved” [ ... ... ] they approved him in the midst of the ae[ons of] light. These numerous multitudes set him a[part], and he came forth against the enemy. They gave this name [ ... ... ] this matter [ ... ... ] who are numerous, that are in the aeons of light and who [are without] measure or number.

This one, [whom they called] “righteous”, because he is the first righteous one who had no sin amongst all of the aeons that belong to the light and the thought [ ... ] they knew him, so that [ ...]

Again, just as the only begotten ones from your [only] begotten race and you [ ... ] according to flesh [ ... ... o]nly begot-ten also from gold and silver and all the [ ... ] of the universe.

[the F]irst Man, himself stood firm in the [midst] of all the worlds of darkness. He did not love [the] worlds of darkness, nor did he lust for them! Rather, h[is] thought and love for the household of his people is in h[i]m. Anything else that the enemy [ ... ] with his violent de[e]ds. Behold, (the First Man) toils and struggles; so that (the enemy) would be separat[e from] them and be cast out from them. And he purifies them and ma[ke]s them f[r]ee, and makes them as they were from the beginning.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Highly lacunose, but lacks frame formulas, citations, or institutional address; reads like an internal doctrinal sentence fragment (“righteousness… voice”) rather than narration or rule. Uncertain due to damage.
- ¶2: Dialogue attribution with “The a[postle] speaks…” is a compilation-frame marker; it situates the teaching historically rather than expounding the cosmic system.
- ¶3: Impersonal cosmological exposition: approval/setting-apart occurs within the Aeons of Light; multitudes without number; mission “against the enemy.” No institutional or citation vector.
- ¶4: First half is internal-to-cosmos (Aeons of Light; first righteous; sinlessness). Then it pivots to ecclesial language (“catechumen”) and exemplar-obedience, indicating later application using the mythic figure.
- ¶5: Christological epithet “only begotten” is likely imported via Christian/Johannine vocabulary (NT vector), even without an explicit citation formula; it reads as Christianizing re-titling rather than a numbered/systematic cosmological mapping.
- ¶6: Direct address and exhortation (“I tell you, my brothers and my limbs”), community categories (“catechumen[s]”), and moral application (“after the likeness…,” unyielding, rebels). Cosmos→community pointing dominates; treat as institutional/pastoral layer rather than recoverable substrate.
- ¶7: Lacunose but appears to make an internal correspondential comparison (race/flesh alongside gold/silver/universe) rather than issuing rules or citing Gospel; dating is uncertain because “only begotten” vocabulary can be Christianized even when used cosmologically.
- ¶8: Core mythic mechanics remain internal to the system: steadfastness amid the worlds of Darkness; separation/casting out; purification/restoration “as from the beginning.” The opening address (“your father”) is a secondary, homiletic framing layer.
- ¶9: Second-person exhortation (“You too… stand firm in this body… lusts”) is application voice anchoring the myth to ascetic practice; not a substrate exposition.
- ¶10: Very damaged, but follows the exhortational sequence and uses optative/wish language (“may they discover…”). Likely continuation of applied admonition about “remembrance,” not a fresh internal cosmological mapping. Uncertain due to lacunae.
- ¶11: Formulaic closing reaction scene (disciples rejoice, give glory, praise, obeisance) is hagiographic/editorial frame material.

## Chapter 120
### Concerning the Two Essences

*This chapter is dominated by late polemical/application rhetoric against monotheism and by Christian/Gospel proof-texting (two-trees / five-trees, beloved son, holy spirit, last-day advent). The only recoverable substrate fragment is the bare cosmological axiom of the two primordial Essences (Light and Darkness), embedded inside a hagiographic vision-frame in ¶1. Strong editorial fatigue is visible: the second half concentrates exhortation and judgement language.*

the] two essences that are present at the beginning [ ... ] the Light and the Darkness, that which is good and that which is evi[], life and] death.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Core fragment is a compact, system-internal cosmological claim (two primordial Essences: Light/Darkness). The surrounding visionary scene and narration (“Once again… our father… he saw… before him”) are compilation frame markers.
- ¶2: Direct audience address and polemical setup against contemporaries who claim “a single essence”; cosmos→contemporary doctrinal dispute (application voice), not systematic cosmological exposition.
- ¶3: Rhetorical catalogue of human vices and social-religious practices (idols, adultery, Mammon, sorceries). This is cosmos→world moral/polemical argumentation rather than system-internal correspondential mapping.
- ¶4: Imperatival polemic (“why do you annul… If they are his, you do them!”) and disciplinary/judgement rhetoric; not cosmological mechanism.
- ¶5: Christianizing argumentative overlay: “beloved son”, “apostles”, and clear dependence on the Gospel ‘tree/fruit’ proof-text (“bitter trees that give not fruit”). Even where it gestures to dualism, the entry-vector is NT-style proof.
- ¶6: Explicit citation style (“Once again he says”) plus father/son/holy spirit and the ‘five trees’ development of the good/evil trees proof-text: Gospel-derived overlay material.
- ¶7: Eschatological condemnation at “the last day” and “his advent” in the presence of God/“beloved son”/“holy spirit” — Christian eschatological register characteristic of later overlay.
- ¶8: Application/exhortation to the hearers about post-mortem verification and impartial judgement; lacks system-internal cosmological mapping and functions as a closing warning (pastoral application).

## Chapter 121
### Concerning the Sect of the Basket

*The chapter opens with hagiographic/narrative framing about a Babylonian presbyter and an explanation of the sect-name, then shifts into an applied analogy (universe-as-tree) used polemically about alms/identity (pastoral/application). The oldest teaching substrate appears mainly in the broken cosmological catalogue of angels/pillars/sea/earth and the twelve powers/three pillars material; however, paragraph 5 shows editorial wrapping (dialogue bridge) and a probable institutional intrusion (“holy church”), consistent with the strong chapter-level editorial-fatigue signal.*

The [...] and the great [...] and the great Mind that is [...] this Youth that is with the [...] thirty-six angels [...] twenty-two angels [...] but [...] of the twenty-two [...] the seven pillars that stan[d ...] the sea that surrounds the universe [...] four angels that are distributed to the [...] universe, supporting the mightiness [...] below, which is the earth of all the heavens [...] these other five gods, the sons of the Man, they that [...] the universe is stood firm [...] the Summons and the Obedience [...] the splendour of the [...] and the Adamant of Light [...] they are become enemies [...] they guide it.

The third also, [...] below, there are twelve powers standing firm there. However, the number of the twelve images [...] the three great pillars of mightiness that [...] pillars supporting the disk [...] the other three [...] they are the garments that the Living Spirit [...] he put them at that time [...] that threw them, together with [...] which is displayed therein [...] this [...] perfect before [...] great Porter [...] in his power [...] these are the three great [...] against [...] are in the midst of people [...] knows [...] entrusts his soul to them [...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic hagiographic scene-setting (“Once again… our enlightener… sitting…”) with narrative biography (Babylon; a presbyter; sect identification). This is editorial container, not cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Etiological explanation of the sect-name via fruit-picking/basket practice; mundane institutional/ethnographic description with no internal cosmos→cosmos mapping and no systematic cosmography. Functions as narrative setup rather than substrate teaching.
- ¶3: Dialogue attribution (“The apostle speaks…”) plus second-person polemical application: cosmos is used to ‘point at’ the presbyter/sect (“you… son of the basket”), and the analogy is explicitly leveraged into alms-language. This is application-layer use of correspondential rhetoric rather than impersonal cosmological mechanism.
- ¶4: Direct blessing/exhortation and explicit institutional anchoring (“alms-giving… possessions… gold and silver… the basket, which is the holy church!”). Cosmos→church identification marks a later ecclesial application layer.
- ¶5: Core substrate is visible in the catalogue-style cosmography (Mind/Youth, numbered angels, seven pillars, encircling sea, earth beneath heavens, five gods/sons of the Man, Summons/Obedience, Adamant of Light). The paragraph is nevertheless editorially wrapped and likely contaminated by an ecclesial gloss (“holy church”), matching the chapter’s second-half pastoral drift.
- ¶6: Impersonal, structural cosmography: fixed numbers (twelve powers; three great pillars; other three as ‘garments’), cosmic agents (Living Spirit; great Porter), and mechanical/support language (‘supporting the disk’). Despite the fragmentary close mentioning ‘people/soul’, the voice remains descriptive rather than exhortative, and the mapping stays within the cosmic architecture.
