## Chapter 122
### Concerning the 'Assent' and the 'Amen'

*The chapter is dominated by late liturgical/pastoral exposition about congregational prayer, sealing, healing, and temptation; this matches the strong editorial-fatigue signal and Gardner’s “christian_connection” flag. A small core substrate survives where ‘assent’ and ‘amen’ are grounded in the cosmic archetypes Summons and Obedience as “portals” opening mercy for the First Man (and related Light beings). Paragraph 4 shows a clear pivot where the cosmic mechanism is extended into institutional ‘holy church/teacher’ identifications (a cosmos→contemporary-world crossing).*

The assent and the amen have come about from these great ones of the powers. They were given the name as the assent belongs to the summons and the amen belongs to the answer.

Just as the summons and the obedience are the ones that opened the portals of mercy and entreaty for the First Man, so they also became portals for the Mother of Life and the Father of the living. The First Man sought and found his redeemer. And also the Mother of Life and the Father of the living sought [...] this runner, who came forth from them, who is the First Man; but the summons and the obedience became the portals for them.

Now, in this way they have become portals for the aeons of greatness towards the First Man, and the soul that was conjoined with death. They became helpers [...] of the First Man during his ascent. They gave him [...] the Father of Life and the Mother of Life [...]. They are purifiers of the living soul, being helpers and bestowers of remembrance for it, be it either in [...] in the tree or in the creation of flesh [...].

... the assent and the amen go up to the ships, and they [...] upon him in the [...] power [...]. ... as the [...] at a place [...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Direct dialogue request formula (“Once again, one of the disciples… Tell me, my master… Tell us”), functioning as hagiographic/container frame rather than impersonal cosmological exposition.
- ¶2: Core mapping is cosmos→cosmos (assent/amen ↔ Summons/Answer), but introduced by a later dialogue attribution that must be stripped.
- ¶3: Impersonal, systematic cosmological mechanism: Summons/Obedience as “portals” enabling redemption; fully internal to Manichaean cosmic hierarchy (First Man, Mother of Life, Father of the living). No institutional or exhortative pivot.
- ¶4: Opens as core cosmological continuation (aeons of greatness; ascent; purification/remembrance), then crosses into editor’s present via ‘holy church’ and ecclesial roles—classic graft using teaching syntax to point at institutional realities.
- ¶5: Liturgical-technical instruction about uttering a formula over psalm/prayer and the assent/amen “sealing the blesser”; operational church usage rather than cosmological architecture.
- ¶6: Congregational practice (“when the congregation… they all answer…”) describing ritual efficacy; institutional/prayer rubric.
- ¶7: Application to the audience (“all of you… you rejoice… came to you profitably”) and devotional personification; not an impersonal cosmological mapping sequence.
- ¶8: Ritual acoustics/image-language is tied explicitly to crowds responding and concludes with “their… alms,” anchoring the mechanism in congregational praxis (cosmos used to validate practice).
- ¶9: Healing-in-prayer scenario (sickness, bodily danger) framed as community action; pastoral utility teaching rather than the old cosmological substrate.
- ¶10: Continuation of the healing-help claim; fragmentary but still within the practical-benefit register.
- ¶11: Explicit communal/elect setting and moral-temptation language (“among the elect… confesses his sin… charity… temptation… lust”); institutional moral care layer.
- ¶12: General rubric of prayer said ‘over that person’ bringing power/help; pastoral application.
- ¶13: Despite lacunae, the technical cosmological image “ships” (sun/moon vessels) and ascent-language points to an internal cosmic mechanics fragment; dating is uncertain because it survives embedded in a practical-help context.
- ¶14: Narrativized rescue/help scenario with guidance, enemies, judgment, goods—application-voice storytelling about worldly peril; not substrate exposition.
- ¶15: Direct deictic/exhortative turn (“Behold… your own cry…”), functioning as rhetorical application.
- ¶16: Late legitimating note invoking “apostles, the first fathers” (explicit compilation/community authority register).
- ¶17: Formulaic closing response/glorification tag; identified chapter-level ‘formulaic closing’ structure.
